> **Headline of rev 4: this revision was EXECUTED, not asserted.** Rev 3 supplied a `nikic/php-parser` enforcement generator; the gate ran it and it **crashed** on the live router (`ReflectionException: Method Closure::__invoke() does not exist`) and, once the crash was bypassed, produced `136 CONTROLLER / 2 FORMREQUEST`, baseline **138**, with the `POLICY` bucket gone. Two structural causes: its walker never visited `NullsafeMethodCall`, so every `return $this->user()?->can('…')` FormRequest was invisible; and its `POLICY` predicate was *"calls `$this->authorize()`"* rather than the audit's *"the ability is a bare, non-dotted policy word"*. **Rev 4 stops reimplementing the audit's oracle and IS it** — the same booted-kernel, regex-over-method-source analyzer, made re-runnable, made read-only-safe, and extended to emit the fixture registry's fields. **It was run against the shared checkout on 2026-09-11 and reproduced the audit exactly on the first attempt: 1054 rows · 642 MIDDLEWARE / 142 AUTH_ONLY / 130 CONTROLLER / 68 SUPERADMIN_ONLY / 46 FORMREQUEST / 18 PUBLIC / 8 POLICY, baseline 176, zero reflection errors.** Every count in this plan is re-derived from that run's own output: the eleven non-POS cluster sizes, the twenty-four POS controller classes, and — a correction the measurement forced — **the single POS `FORMREQUEST` row belongs to `ManagerPinController::verify`, not `PosAuthController`**, so POS is twenty-five commits and that one is a *behaviour change*, because `VerifyManagerPinRequest::authorize()` is `return $this->user() !== null;` — an authentication check with no permission behind it. Plus: the fixture machinery is supplied in full (all **fifteen** factories — not twenty; the twenty entries share five), `RouteActionFixtureContractTest`'s six assertions and `generate-unfixtured-routes.php`'s body; the first-red claim is replaced with the assertion that is actually red; every add list is written out with **no** `…`, no *"+ tail"* and no `<Module>`; and `$SCRATCH` is a runnable command rather than a path containing `<session-id>`.

*(Rev 3 was the fix round on gate r2 — register `docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-11-rbac-plan-234-codex-gate-r2.md`; prompt `docs/handoff/CODEX-PROMPT-plan-gate-RBAC-234-round-2-2026-09-11.md`. Its ID-by-ID mapping is the **Change log rev 2 → rev 3** section.)*

> **Headline of rev 3.** Three blockers, and all three are the same failure in three places: rev 2 named an artefact and did not supply the thing that produces it. **(1) The 176-route baseline had no generator.** Phase 0 said *"generate a classification"* from `route:list`, which route metadata alone cannot do — a `CONTROLLER` row is a fact about a controller **method body**, and a `FORMREQUEST` row a fact about a FormRequest's `authorize()`. Rev 3 supplies **`apps/api/tools/generate-enforcement-baseline.php`** in full: it boots the router, resolves each route's controller and its FormRequest parameters through reflection, walks their method bodies with `nikic/php-parser` (the same dependency wave 1's PHPStan rules and wave 0b's `PermissionWriterCensus` already use), and classifies each route into the seven buckets the audit CSV uses — with the exact command and the expected `130 CONTROLLER / 46 FORMREQUEST = 176`. **(2) Seventeen of the twenty route fixtures did not resolve.** Re-derived every one against `dev`: `roles.*`, `users.*` and `vouchers.issue-goodwill` resolve; Coupon, Promotion, Scheduling, Menu, BatchExpiry and Workshop declare **no route names at all**; `replenishment-requests.approve` does not exist; the PlatformIntegration names are `platform.*`; and `service-accounts.*` are wave 2b's. The fixture registry is therefore **keyed by `METHOD uri`**, with `name` as an optional cross-check, and all twenty entries are re-stated with the method, URI and mutation evidence that exist. **(3) The fixture schema could not produce its own expectation.** It recorded the request and not the **permission key**, so `templateGrants()` had no route-to-key input. Every entry now carries `permission`, sourced from `can:` where the route has one and from the generator's classification where it does not — and `RouteActionFixtureContractTest` asserts the two agree. Plus the majors: `$SCRATCH` is defined; the locale files are `common.json`, not `permissions.json`; **every cluster and every POS controller has a fully enumerated add list** — and enumerating them found that POS's 91 routes span **24 controllers, not 10**, so the lane's ordinal allocation is restated; the reviewer gate attaches per cluster; and `RouteActionFixtures.php`, `unfixtured-routes.json`'s format and its starting ceiling are supplied.

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** One enforcement style everywhere, and every exception list emptied. Route middleware becomes the **only** action gate: the in-controller checks and the FormRequest-only gates move to `can:` / `require.any.permission:`, deleting ratchet baseline entries as they go. The POS legacy role ladder is removed now that `authority_stale` plus a permission payload can replace it. `MembershipRole` and Spatie roles stop being two vocabularies for one noun. And a per-role regression matrix asserts, for the first time, what each seeded role **cannot** do.

**Architecture:** Nothing new is designed. This wave is a **sweep with a ratchet**: each module's cluster is one commit, each commit lowers a ceiling, and the ceilings only fall. The one behavioural change is the POS ladder removal, which is gated behind a real offline test and a POS release. Everything else is a move of an existing check from one layer to another, proven by a per-route allow-and-deny assertion rather than by "it still compiles".

**Tech Stack:** Laravel 12 / PHP 8.4 / Spatie laravel-permission 6.25 / Stancl Tenancy 3.10 / PHPUnit 11 / PHPStan L8 / Pint / React 19 / Vitest / Tauri 2 (POS) / Playwright.

## Entry condition

**One thing, and it is both lanes of wave 2.**

1. **Wave 2 merged into local `dev` — 2a AND 2b.** The regression matrix asserts deny paths that only exist after 2a (`LastAdminFloor`, F-1, the D4 shaping) and 2b (`EnforceTokenScope`, `principal_kind`). The `MembershipRole` reconciliation reads `UserController.php:929`, which 2a rewrites. The POS ladder removal depends on 2b having made `pinHolders()` human-only.
2. **Wave 2 DEPLOYED** — `tenants:migrate-rolling` + `permissions:sync-fleet` green on staging, and `GET /api/v1/admin/monitoring/health` reporting `permissions_sync.healthy = true`. **Task 3-7 (retiring the 22 deprecated keys) additionally requires ONE FULL RELEASE to have elapsed since wave 1 deployed the deprecation flags** — see **Q-w3-5**, which states what "one release" means mechanically rather than as a feeling.

**Mechanical checks:**

```
cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp
git fetch --all --prune
git merge-base --is-ancestor lane/rbac-w2a dev && echo "2a MERGED" || echo "2a NOT MERGED — STOP"
git merge-base --is-ancestor lane/rbac-w2b dev && echo "2b MERGED" || echo "2b NOT MERGED — STOP"
git log dev --oneline -1 -- apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Middleware/EnforceTokenScope.php   # must return a commit
git log dev --oneline -1 -- apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Enums/PrincipalKind.php                  # must return a commit
git log dev --oneline -1 -- apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/Authorization/LastAdminFloor.php         # must return a commit
# 2b's users migration actually applied on staging, not merely merged:
#   psql … -d tenant<uuid> -c "\d+ users" | grep principal_kind
```

**Overlap.** Wave 3 touches **more files than any other wave in this programme** — **130** controller methods across nine modules, **46** FormRequests across eight (**176** in total — see **Q-w3-2**), plus the POS app and the glossary. It is therefore run as **one lane with many small commits**, not split, and it **cannot run concurrently with any other lane touching those modules**. Before opening:

```
for lane in $(git branch --list 'lane/*' --format='%(refname:short)'); do
  git merge-base --is-ancestor "$lane" dev && continue
  echo "== $lane"
  git diff --name-only "dev...$lane" -- \
    apps/api/app/Modules/POS apps/api/app/Modules/Workshop apps/api/app/Modules/Scheduling \
    apps/api/app/Modules/Identity apps/api/app/Modules/Menu apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry \
    apps/api/app/Modules/Coupon apps/api/app/Modules/Promotion apps/api/app/Modules/Voucher \
    apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant apps/api/app/Modules/PlatformIntegration apps/api/app/Modules/Replenishment \
    apps/pos/src docs/glossary.md apps/web/src/components/organisms/Sidebar/Sidebar.tsx
done
```

**Any hit is a blocker.** A sweep that renumbers **176** call sites cannot be merged against a lane that also edits them. If a lane must run concurrently, **shrink this wave to the modules that lane does not touch** and record the deferred modules as a residual — do **not** attempt the merge.

      **Phase 0 re-derives the POS controller count before the allocation is used**, from the generator's own output rather than from this table:
      ```
      php -r '$b = json_decode(file_get_contents("tests/Architecture/baselines/enforcement-style-baseline.json"), true);
        $c = [];
        foreach ($b as $r) { if (str_contains($r["controller"], "Modules\\POS\\")) { $c[$r["controller"]] = ($c[$r["controller"]] ?? 0) + 1; } }
        arsort($c); printf("%d routes across %d controllers%s", array_sum($c), count($c), PHP_EOL);'
      ```
      **Expected: `91 routes across 24 controllers`.** If the controller count differs, **the tail shifts with it** and the handback records the new allocation — which is why the tail's tasks are listed by ordinal here rather than having their ordinals written into their own headings only.
      **Task 3-6 step 1 is executed LAST in the lane**, after step 4, precisely so its variable-length block takes a contiguous tail of ordinals (**`0.6.48` onward**) rather than interleaving with the fixed ones — its module count is not known until the by-file list is generated. **The handback records the tail's mapping in order.** No commit reuses a subject, and the verification checklist asserts it mechanically.
      > **THE THREE PRODUCER-DEPENDENT INVENTORIES ARE A DISPATCH-BRIEF PRECONDITION, not an implementation-time discovery** (gate r4, Wave 3 **MAJOR 2**). Three lists in this plan are legitimately generated rather than written — they depend on what waves 0b/1/2 actually land — but *"generated at commit time"* is how an implementer ends up designing them:
      > | Inventory | Where it is generated | What must be in the DISPATCH BRIEF before work starts |
      > |---|---|---|
      > | the `SystemRoleName` caller list (Task 3-4, `…rbac-wave-3.md` "Step 2") | Phase 0's `grep -rn 'SystemRoleName'` at the lane base | the **resolved caller list**, file:line, and the disposition of each |
      > | the permission-literal by-file list and its module tail (`0.6.48` onward) | Phase 0's by-file literal run | the **resolved module-to-ordinal mapping**, one line per module, so `0.6.48…0.6.N` is fixed before the first of them is written |
      > | the deprecated-key manifest ownership (Task 3-7) | Phase 0, against wave 1's shipped manifests | the **resolved key-to-manifest map** for all twenty-two |
      > **Phase 0 produces all three, the orchestrator pastes them into the brief, and the brief is what the implementer receives.** A lane that starts without them is not dispatchable: the plan gate has no way to check a list that does not exist yet, and neither does the reviewer.
- [ ] **E-1..E-4, the enforcement standard, stated so a reviewer can check each conversion:**
      - **E-1** — the action gate is the **route middleware** (`can:` for a single key, `require.any.permission:` for an any-of). Never `permission:` — that alias has never existed in this repository.
      - **E-2** — a controller check may add **defence in depth** but never **replaces** the route gate.
      - **E-3** — **row-level** authorization (this user may touch *this* row) stays in a **policy** or an explicit ownership check; it is not expressible as a route middleware string and must not be forced into one.
      - **E-4** — a FormRequest's `authorize()` **may remain only as a duplicate of the middleware, or return `true`**. It is never the sole gate.
- [ ] **Every conversion ships an allow AND a deny assertion.** A route moved from a controller check to `can:` with only a 200 test proves nothing: today's `dev:apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/RBACTest.php` acts as `admin` throughout and therefore exercises **only** allow paths. **A conversion with no deny test is not done.**
- [ ] **No conversion widens or narrows who may do the thing.** For each route, the plan records *who could before* and *who can after*, and they must be the same set. A difference is a **finding to raise**, not a refactor to ship. This is the single most likely defect in a **176**-site sweep.
- [ ] **Constructor injection only** (`private readonly`), never `app()`. **Strict types**, no `mixed`, no `any`. **Enums** for every status/type value.
- [ ] **Both ratchet ceilings are shrink-only** and fall in the same commit as the conversion that earns them. `UNCOVERED_WRITE_CEILING` and `UNCOVERED_READ_CEILING` stay separate so closing reads can never buy write headroom.
- [ ] **`permissions:prune-orphans --confirm` is a deliberate OPERATOR action, never a deploy step.** `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` (wave 1) must keep passing, and it will fail the build if the command is added to a deploy row.
- [ ] **No `origin` push.** Merge into **local** `dev`; the owner promotes. `dev` is fast-forward-only, never force-pushed (CLAUDE.md rule 21).
- [ ] **Worktree:** `git -C /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp worktree add .worktrees/rbac-w3 -b lane/rbac-w3 dev`, from the shared checkout. Never edit or commit in the shared `dev` worktree.
- [ ] **PHPUnit by path only.** Never the full suite locally.
- [ ] **PG lane env — both variables, always:**
      ```
      DB_CONNECTION=pgsql DB_HOST=127.0.0.1 DB_PORT=5433 \
      DB_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w3 DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w3_central \
      ./vendor/bin/phpunit <path>
      ```
- [ ] **Empirical evidence, not compilation** (owner rule, 2026-08-31). Each converted module gets a **UI happy path** in the browser with `page.on('response')` 5xx capture and console-error capture, both asserted at **zero**, and toasts asserted by text. A module whose routes changed gate and whose screen was never opened is not verified.
- [ ] **The POS ladder removal is a POS RELEASE.** It ships with an offline-behaviour test on `apps/pos` (vitest) and is the one task in this wave that changes runtime behaviour on a device that may be offline for days. It is committed **last** among the POS work and is separately revertable.
- [ ] **Convention 09** is satisfied by the three named tests in the "Convention-09 obligations" section, not by a waiver. **Convention 11**: every noun this wave touches ends the wave with one row in `docs/glossary.md`, one table, one primary write path and one operator surface.

## Decisions and open questions

Six. One is the spec's own **OQ-3**, which this wave does **not** implement and must not be confused with; five are gaps this plan resolves, each with the default implemented and the bounded alternative.

### OQ-3 — SoD baseline retirement is NOT wave 3's

Spec §9.2's replacement OQ-3 asks whether any of the eighteen baselined SoD combinations should be retired. **Wave 3 changes no template grant and therefore cannot change that number.** It is listed here only so a reviewer does not look for it, and so nobody reads a conversion that touches an `invoices.post` route as an SoD decision. If the owner rules during this wave, it is a **separate follow-up lane**; the ratchet guarantees the number can only fall, so nothing is lost by waiting.

### Q-w3-1 — the `Phase` ordinal

The programme's ordinals are 0a = `Phase 0.1.<task>`, 0b = `Phase 0.2.<task>`, **wave 1 = `Phase 1.<task>`** (a two-segment subject outside the `0.x` series — `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-wave-1.md:150`, rev 6.3 `949dfabdf`), 2a = `Phase 0.4.0` … `0.4.9`, 2b = **`Phase 0.5.2` … `0.5.9`** with `0.5.1` unused (`…-wave-2.md` rev 3). **`Phase 0.3.*` is never taken**, and rev 1 of this plan wrongly listed wave 1 as `0.3` (plan gate r1, cross-plan **MAJOR 1**). **This plan implements `Phase 0.6.<n>`**, whose full allocation is the table in the Global Constraints. Phase 0 confirms it against `dev`'s history, matching **both** subject shapes:
```
git -C /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp log dev --oneline --grep='^Phase [0-9]' -80 \
  | sed -E 's/^[0-9a-f]+ (Phase [0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)?)\..*/\1/' | sort -u
```
**Expected:** `Phase 0.1`, `Phase 0.2`, `Phase 0.4`, `Phase 0.5`, `Phase 1` — and **not** `Phase 0.3`, which is a deliberate gap. Wave 2's lane-2b subjects run to **`0.5.9`** after its rev-3 re-sequence; `0.6` is unaffected either way. If an earlier wave took something else, take the next free ordinal and **record the shift in the handback** so wave 4 can re-anchor.

### Q-w3-2 — **CLOSED as an arithmetic defect in rev 1: the sweep is 130 / 46 = 176**

`docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-09-roles-permissions/02-route-enforcement-sweep.md:37-38` states **CONTROLLER 130** and **FORMREQUEST 46**, and **the audit is right**. Rev 1 of this plan said 127 / 44 and attributed the difference to *"unescaped commas inside a quoted CSV field"*. **There are no unescaped commas.** The CSV is standards-compliant: every field containing a comma is double-quoted, and any RFC-4180 parser reads it correctly. Rev 1's `awk -F,` is not one — it splits on **every** comma, including the quoted ones, so five rows' `$8` landed on a fragment of the `controller_check_permissions` column instead of on `classification`, and those five rows vanished from the tally (plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B3-1**, citation audit item 1).

**Re-derived with a real parser, at the same file:**
```
python3 - <<'EOF'
import csv, collections
p = 'docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-09-roles-permissions/02-route-enforcement-sweep.csv'
rows = list(csv.reader(open(p)))
print('data rows:', len(rows) - 1)                       # 1054
i = rows[0].index('classification')                      # 7
for k, v in collections.Counter(r[i] for r in rows[1:]).most_common():
    print(f'{v:5}  {k}')
EOF
#  1054 data rows
#   642  MIDDLEWARE
#   142  AUTH_ONLY
#   130  CONTROLLER
#    68  SUPERADMIN_ONLY
#    46  FORMREQUEST
#    18  PUBLIC
#     8  POLICY
```
`642 + 142 + 130 + 68 + 46 + 18 + 8 = 1054`, which is the row count — rev 1's tally summed to 1 049 and nobody noticed, because five rows had simply disappeared. A fresh `route:list` at `dev:630afa86f` produces the **same 1 054 method/URI pairs**, so the CSV is neither stale nor short.

**The per-cluster deltas**, from the same parse, grouping on the `controller` column's `Modules\<Name>` segment:

| Cluster | rev 1 | **rev 2** |
|---|---|---|
| Identity | 6 + 5 | **7 + 7** |
| Workshop | 12 + 15 | **14 + 15** |
| POS | 91 + 1 | **91 + 1** (unchanged) |
| every other cluster | — | **unchanged** |

**This plan still implements the sweep against the LIVE ROUTER**, not against a CSV: Task 3-1 Step 1 regenerates the classification from the booted router into `EnforcementStyleRatchetTest`'s baseline, and **that** count is the ceiling. What changes is the **expectation** the regeneration is checked against — **130 / 46**, not 127 / 44. **If the regenerated count is not within a handful of 130 / 46, stop and reconcile before converting anything**; a legitimate shortfall is a route waves 0a–2 have already gated, and Task 3-1 Step 1 names each such route in the commit body rather than absorbing it into a number.

### Q-w3-3 — POS is 92 of the 176, spans TWENTY-FIVE classes, and is converted last, one commit per class

> **Rev 3 corrects the controller count from ten to TWENTY-FOUR** (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 3**, found while enumerating the add lists). Rev 2's "ten" was the **grep's** figure — ten controllers containing thirty-eight `Gate::authorize` call sites — which is the quantity **M3-5** already ruled is not route arithmetic, and rev 2 then used it to allocate ten commit ordinals anyway. Grouping the audit CSV's 91 POS `CONTROLLER` rows on the `controller` column gives twenty-four distinct controllers. **The router is the oracle, here as everywhere in this wave**, and fourteen controllers had no commit at all.

`Gate::authorize` and its siblings in `apps/api/app/Modules/POS` are not one idiom in one place. The POS also has the **most offline-sensitive** clients and the **most device-authored** contracts (CLAUDE.md rule 20).

**The twenty-five classes and their route counts**, from the EXECUTED generator's own grouping (`ENFORCEMENT_ROWS_OUT` → group by `controller`), run 2026-09-11, in commit order — **device-sync family first** (Step 2), then the rest smallest-first so the largest diff is reviewed last (Step 3). All live under `apps/api/app/Modules/POS/Presentation/Controllers/`.

| Ordinal | Controller | Routes | Step |
|---|---|---:|---|
| `0.6.13` | `AuditEventSyncController` | 1 | 2 — device sync |
| `0.6.14` | `PosCustomerSyncController` | 1 | 2 — device sync |
| `0.6.15` | `PosPendingCustomerController` | 1 | 2 — device sync |
| `0.6.16` | `PosStockLevelController` | 1 | 2 — device sync |
| `0.6.17` | `SyncController` | 1 | 2 — device sync |
| `0.6.18` | `ZReportSyncController` | 1 | 2 — device sync |
| `0.6.19` | `VoucherSyncController` | 4 | 2 — device sync |
| `0.6.20` | `FiscalSchemaCutoverController` | 1 | 3 |
| `0.6.21` | `PosVariantController` | 1 | 3 |
| `0.6.22` | `ReceiptFilterOptionsController` | 1 | 3 |
| `0.6.23` | `ShiftToleranceController` | 1 | 3 |
| `0.6.24` | `StockDistributionController` | 1 | 3 |
| `0.6.25` | `V4RefundAuthoringAcknowledgementController` | 1 | 3 |
| `0.6.26` | `PosReplenishmentController` | 2 | 3 |
| `0.6.27` | `CashDrawerController` | 4 | 3 |
| `0.6.28` | `KitchenDisplayController` | 4 | 3 |
| `0.6.29` | `PosAuthController` | 5 | 3 |
| `0.6.30` | `ReceiptController` | 5 | 3 |
| `0.6.31` | `ShiftController` | 6 | 3 |
| `0.6.32` | `ReportController` | 7 | 3 |
| `0.6.33` | `AnalyticsController` | 8 | 3 |
| `0.6.34` | `OrderController` | 8 | 3 |
| `0.6.35` | `TableController` | 10 | 3 |
| `0.6.36` | `TerminalController` | 16 | 3 |
| **`0.6.37`** | **`ManagerPinController`** — the twenty-fifth class, hosting the **single POS `FORMREQUEST` row** | **1** *(FORMREQUEST)* | **3, last** |
| | **Total** | **91 + 1 = 92** | |

> **Step 2's family is SEVEN controllers, not four.** Rev 2 named `AuditEventSyncController`, `PosCustomerSyncController`, `PosStockLevelController` and `PosPendingCustomerController`; `SyncController`, `ZReportSyncController` and `VoucherSyncController` are the same shape — device-authored queues whose 403 the operator may never see — and Step 2's retry-behaviour rule applies to all seven or it is arbitrary.
> **`TerminalController` (16) and `TableController` (10) are the two largest and rev 2 gave neither a commit.** They are also the two whose grep count is near zero, which is exactly why the grep was the wrong oracle: their gates are `->can()` and `authorize()` on the request, not `Gate::authorize`.
> **The POS module has FIVE route files, not one** (gate r3, Wave 3 **MAJOR 2** — rev 3 asserted *"`apps/api/app/Modules/POS/routes.php` appears in all twenty-four add lists"*, which is false). `git ls-files 'apps/api/app/Modules/POS/routes*.php'` gives `routes.php`, `routes_orders.php`, `routes_tables.php`, `routes_kitchen.php`, `routes_held_orders.php`, and the twenty-five classes' routes are declared across **four** of them:
>
> | Route file | Controllers whose routes it declares |
> |---|---|
> | `apps/api/app/Modules/POS/routes_orders.php` | `OrderController` (`0.6.34`) |
> | `apps/api/app/Modules/POS/routes_tables.php` | `TableController` (`0.6.35`) |
> | `apps/api/app/Modules/POS/routes_kitchen.php` | `KitchenDisplayController` (`0.6.28`) |
> | `apps/api/app/Modules/POS/routes.php` | **the other twenty-two**, `ManagerPinController` included (`:260`) |
>
> That matters because it is the **concurrency** argument: twenty-two commits editing `routes.php` sequentially is a clean history and three commits editing their own file never touch it. It is also why each add list below names **its own** route file rather than one file repeated twenty-five times.

**This plan implements POS last and splits it into one commit per controller**, each with its own allow/deny test and its own ceiling decrement, so a POS regression is revertable to a single controller rather than to "the POS". **The bounded alternative** — one POS commit — is rejected: 91 conversions in one diff is precisely the shape of change where a reviewer approves the pattern and misses the one route where the permission is subtly different.

### Q-w3-4 — **CORRECTED: `fetchDiscountPermissions` is ALREADY wired; the residual is the PIN-SETUP ladder**

Spec §4.10 item 4 says removing the ladder means *"the offline path must fall back to the last synced permission payload instead of to a role name"*.

**Rev 1's premise was stale and is withdrawn** (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M3-1**, citation audit item 2). It claimed `fetchDiscountPermissions` *"exists at `dev:apps/pos/src/api/discountApi.ts:14` and is never called from the store"*. Re-verified against the tree:

```
grep -n "fetchDiscountPermissions\|resolveOnlineDiscountPermissions" apps/pos/src/stores/operatorStore.ts
#   4:import { fetchDiscountPermissions } from '@/api/discountApi';
# 128:async function resolveOnlineDiscountPermissions(operator: Operator): Promise<Operator> {
# 136:    permissions = await fetchDiscountPermissions(terminalCode, operator.id);
# 269:      void resolveOnlineDiscountPermissions(offlineMatch)
# 294:      const operator = await resolveOnlineDiscountPermissions(
# 368:      const operator = await resolveOnlineDiscountPermissions(
# 446:      const refreshed = await resolveOnlineDiscountPermissions(operator);
```

It is **production code**: imported at `:4`, called inside `resolveOnlineDiscountPermissions()` at `:136`, and that function is used on **offline acceptance** (`:269`), **online PIN verification** (`:294`), **setup** (`:368`) and **refresh** (`:446`). The terminal-aware, `min(terminal, user)` flow already reaches the device on every one of those paths.

**What IS real is the residual role-name ladder at PIN setup**: `const isAdmin = user.roles.includes('super_admin') || user.roles.includes('admin')` at `dev:apps/pos/src/stores/operatorStore.ts:354`, feeding `can_discount` and `max_discount_percent` in the branch that runs **before** `resolveOnlineDiscountPermissions()` returns — so an admin setting a PIN gets `100` from a role name rather than from the terminal chain, and an offline admin gets it with no terminal ceiling at all.

**This plan therefore implements Task 3-3 as: (1) a regression test that PINS the already-wired terminal-aware flow, then (2) removal of the residual ladder** — not as *"wire a feature that already exists"*. The ordering inside the task is unchanged and still load-bearing, because deleting `:354`'s two assignments without first proving the existing source covers the setup path would leave an admin's offline PIN setup with `can_discount: false` and no ceiling — a visible till-side outage. **The bounded alternative** — remove the ladder and accept the regression until a later POS release — is rejected for that reason.

**The ladder is removed in Task 3-3, not in Task 3-2** (plan gate r1, **minor m3-1**): rev 1's cross-reference said *"Task 3-2 step POS-last"*, which is the server-side `Gate::authorize` sweep and touches no device code at all.

### Q-w3-5 — "after one release" is defined mechanically

Spec §8's wave-3 row says the 22 deprecated keys — a set whose twenty-second entry **is** `reports.view` — are removed *"after one release"*. That is not a checkable condition as written.

**This plan implements it as three facts, all verifiable, all required:**
1. **Wave 1's deploy has been live in production for at least one full release cycle** — i.e. a **later, unrelated** production deploy has happened since it, so every tenant has been synced at least twice with the keys flagged deprecated.
2. **`php artisan permissions:report-dead` returns exactly the 22** on the current tree, scanning its four roots (`apps/api`, `apps/web/src`, `apps/pos/src`, and the generated map) under its two honesty rules — a hit in the **generated map alone** is not evidence of use, and a hit must be **permission-shaped** (`apps/pos/src/components/pos/TodaySalesPanel.tsx:308` is `t('reports.view')`, a translation key; counting it would keep a genuinely retired permission alive forever).
3. **A live-tenant orphan report** shows the keys are held by no **custom** role on any tenant — because sync's step 6 revokes them from uncustomised template roles but never from a custom one, and pruning a key a custom role holds silently removes a capability.

**If any of the three fails, Task 3-7 is DEFERRED and recorded as a residual** — not partially performed. Removing 21 of 22 is worse than removing none: it makes the set no longer authoritative. The bounded alternative — remove them now regardless — is rejected for exactly the reason the deprecation window exists.

### Q-w3-6 — the `Sidebar.permission` rename is `permission` → `moduleKey`, not the reverse

`dev:apps/web/src/components/organisms/Sidebar/Sidebar.tsx:110,120` declares `permission?: ModuleKey` on both the item and the group interfaces, and its own docblock at `:101` says *"`permission` — key into `MODULE_PERMISSIONS` (role gating)"*. The values in use are a mixture: `'services'`, `'purchases'`, `'dashboard'`, `'sales'` are `ModuleKey`s, while `'deliveries.view'` (`:170`), `'partners.view'`, `'goods-receipt.create-standalone'` (`:200`) and `'document-ingestions'` (`:201`) are **permission-shaped**, and `'nav.purchasesGroup'` / `'nav.purchaseOrders'` (`:187,196`) are neither.

**This plan implements the rename as `permission` → `moduleKey`, typed `ModuleKey`, and introduces a SECOND optional field `permission?: Permission`** typed against wave 1's generated union — so the mixed values separate into the two things they actually are, and the `nav.*` pseudo-keys become **explicit `MODULE_PERMISSIONS` entries** rather than strings that happen to resolve. Any value that is neither is a finding. **The bounded alternative** — rename the type instead of the field — is rejected: it would leave `'deliveries.view'` typed as a `ModuleKey`, which is the confusion, not the fix.

---

## Phase 0: Pre-flight (60 minutes, before Task 3-1)

- [ ] **Re-run both entry-condition checks**, including the staging one for 2b's migration.
- [ ] **Create the lane and record the base:**
      ```
      git -C /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp worktree add .worktrees/rbac-w3 -b lane/rbac-w3 dev
      git -C /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w3 rev-parse HEAD
      ```
      **Record the SHA in the handback.** Re-pin every `dev:` line number in this plan at that SHA before the task that edits it — wave 2 rewrote `RoleController`, `UserController`, `User`, `AuthController`, `PosAuthController` and `usePermissions.ts`, so **every Identity and POS citation here has moved**.
- [ ] **Confirm the `Phase` ordinal (Q-w3-1).**
- [ ] **Define `SCRATCH` once, here, and export it.** Every command in this plan that writes a working file uses it (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 1**: rev 2 used `tee "$SCRATCH/…"` in Task 3-6 and never defined the variable, so the command as printed wrote to `/w3-literal-sites-by-file.txt`).
      ```
      # The session scratchpad if the agent has one exported, else a fresh mktemp -d.
      # NO placeholder token: rev 3 wrote a literal "<session-id>" into this path, so the
      # command as printed was not runnable (gate r3, Wave 3 MAJOR 3).
      SCRATCH="${CLAUDE_SCRATCHPAD:-$(mktemp -d -t rbac-w3)}"
      export SCRATCH
      mkdir -p "$SCRATCH" && test -w "$SCRATCH" || { echo "SCRATCH not writable: $SCRATCH"; exit 1; }
      case "$SCRATCH" in /tmp/*|*/apps/web/*) echo "SCRATCH must be neither /tmp nor inside apps/web: $SCRATCH"; exit 1;; esac
      echo "SCRATCH=$SCRATCH"
      ```
      **Never `/tmp` and never a path inside `apps/web`** — an `outputDir` inside the vite root makes vite full-page-reload the SPA mid-test, and the two `case` arms above refuse both mechanically rather than by instruction. If the agent's environment block names a session scratchpad, export it as `CLAUDE_SCRATCHPAD` before running this; otherwise the `mktemp -d` arm is the answer and no substitution is needed.

- [ ] **Regenerate the enforcement classification from the LIVE ROUTER (Q-w3-2), with the GENERATOR THIS PLAN SUPPLIES**, and freeze it as the sweep's baseline.

      > **Rev 2 said *"then generate"* and named no generator** (plan gate r2, **BLOCKER B1**). Route metadata alone cannot produce this classification: `route:list` knows a route's middleware stack, its controller and its URI, but *"this route's authorization lives in the controller method body"* and *"…in its FormRequest's `authorize()`"* are facts about **source**, not about the registry. An implementer handed *"generate a classification"* would have had to design the analyzer, which is the hard part and the part a reviewer would have had to check. It is supplied below.

      **`apps/api/tools/generate-enforcement-baseline.php`**, in full, and **this is the version that was EXECUTED against the shared checkout and reproduced the audit's classification exactly** (gate r3, Wave 3 **BLOCKER 1** and **BLOCKER 2**). Rev 3 shipped a `nikic/php-parser` AST walker that **crashed on the live router** — `ReflectionException: Method Closure::__invoke() does not exist`, because it mapped a closure action to `Closure::__invoke` and then reflected it — and, once that crash was bypassed, produced `136 CONTROLLER / 2 FORMREQUEST`, baseline **138**, with the `POLICY` bucket gone entirely. **Two root causes, both structural rather than incidental:** its `$checksIn` walker visited `MethodCall|StaticCall` but **not `NullsafeMethodCall`**, so every `return $this->user()?->can('…')` FormRequest was invisible (e.g. `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Promotion/Presentation/Requests/StorePromotionRequest.php:15-18`); and its `POLICY` predicate was *"the method calls `$this->authorize()`"*, which is not the audit's predicate at all — the audit's is *"the ability named is a **bare**, non-dotted policy word"*.

      **Rev 4 stops reimplementing the oracle and IS the oracle.** The audit's classification (`docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-09-roles-permissions/02-route-enforcement-sweep.md` §Method, steps 2–5) was produced by a script that boots the kernel and **regex-scans each method's SOURCE** with eight textual predicates — including `abort_unless(… can`, `abort_if(… ! … can`, `hasRole(` and `hasAnyRole(`, none of which are call-name matches — then applies a fixed bucket precedence. Reproducing a regex oracle with a different engine is exactly how rev 3 got 136/2. This generator **is** that analyzer: made re-runnable, made read-only-safe through two output env vars so it can be executed against a shared checkout without writing into it, and extended to emit the `permission` / `permission_source` fields Task 3-9's fixture registry consumes.

~~~php
<?php

declare(strict_types=1);

/**
 * Generate the enforcement-style baseline from the LIVE ROUTER plus source analysis.
 *
 * Standalone — it boots the framework itself, exactly the way the audit's
 * `analyze_routes.php` did (02-route-enforcement-sweep.md "Method" step 2), because
 * `route:list --json` does not resolve middleware to the raw route-level tokens
 * (`can:x`, `require.any.permission:a,b`, `super_admin`, `central_admin_role:x,y`)
 * and gives no controller `file:line`:
 *
 *   cd apps/api && php tools/generate-enforcement-baseline.php
 *
 * Env overrides (both optional):
 *   ENFORCEMENT_BASELINE_OUT  where the baseline JSON is written
 *                             (default tests/Architecture/baselines/enforcement-style-baseline.json)
 *   ENFORCEMENT_ROWS_OUT      where the full 1054-row classification JSON is written (default: not written)
 *
 * Buckets and their precedence are IDENTICAL to the audit's 02 sweep, so the
 * output is directly comparable with
 * docs/superpowers/audits/2026-09-09-roles-permissions/02-route-enforcement-sweep.csv
 * which produced the 130 CONTROLLER + 46 FORMREQUEST = 176 figure this wave converts:
 *
 *   PUBLIC           no `auth:` middleware at all
 *   MIDDLEWARE       the stack carries `can:` or `require.any.permission:`
 *   SUPERADMIN_ONLY  the stack carries super_admin / central_admin / central_admin_role
 *   POLICY           a controller check whose ability is a BARE (non-dotted) policy word
 *   CONTROLLER       a controller-body check naming a dotted Spatie permission
 *   FORMREQUEST      no middleware/controller check; an injected FormRequest's
 *                    authorize() is a real check (not an unconditional `return true`)
 *   SERVICE          the only check is one hop away in an injected service (0 today)
 *   AUTH_ONLY        authenticated, no action-level check found at any layer
 *
 * WHY REGEX OVER METHOD SOURCE AND NOT php-parser (rev 3 shipped an AST version and it
 * FAILED — plan gate r3, Wave 3 BLOCKER 1/2): the classification this wave must reproduce
 * is the audit's, and the audit's predicate set is textual and wider than a call-name
 * match — `abort_unless(... can`, `abort_if(... ! ... can`, `hasRole(`, `hasAnyRole(` —
 * and it must catch `$this->user()?->can(...)` (a NullsafeMethodCall, which the rev-3 AST
 * walker did not visit, e.g. dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Promotion/Presentation/Requests/StorePromotionRequest.php:15-18).
 * Reproducing a regex oracle with a different engine is how rev 3 produced 136/2 instead
 * of 130/46. This generator IS the audit's analyzer, made re-runnable and made to emit
 * the fixture registry's `permission` / `permission_source` fields.
 */

$root = getcwd();

if (! is_file($root.'/bootstrap/app.php')) {
    fwrite(STDERR, "Run this from apps/api (no bootstrap/app.php in {$root}).".PHP_EOL);
    exit(1);
}

require $root.'/vendor/autoload.php';

/** @var Illuminate\Foundation\Application $app */
$app = require $root.'/bootstrap/app.php';
$app->make(Illuminate\Contracts\Console\Kernel::class)->bootstrap();

/** The eight textual predicates that MEAN "an authorization decision happens here". */
const PERMISSION_PATTERNS = [
    '/->authorize\(/' => 'authorize()',
    '/Gate::/' => 'Gate::',
    '/->can\(/' => '->can(',
    '/->hasPermissionTo\(/' => 'hasPermissionTo()',
    '/abort_unless\([^)]*can/' => 'abort_unless(...can',
    '/abort_if\([^)]*!.*can/' => 'abort_if(...!can',
    '/->hasRole\(/' => 'hasRole()',
    '/->hasAnyRole\(/' => 'hasAnyRole()',
];

/** Bare ability words that mean a Laravel Policy, not a Spatie permission. */
const POLICY_BARE_WORDS = [
    'view', 'create', 'update', 'delete', 'post', 'reverse', 'void',
    'approve', 'reject', 'manage', 'restore', 'forceDelete', 'viewAny',
];

function methodSource(?ReflectionFunctionAbstract $m): string
{
    if ($m === null) {
        return '';
    }

    $file = $m->getFileName();
    $start = $m->getStartLine();
    $end = $m->getEndLine();

    if ($file === false || ! is_file($file) || $start === false || $end === false) {
        return '';
    }

    return implode('', array_slice(file($file), $start - 1, $end - $start + 1));
}

/** @return list<string> the pattern LABELS that matched */
function findPatterns(string $src): array
{
    $found = [];

    foreach (PERMISSION_PATTERNS as $re => $label) {
        if (preg_match($re, $src) === 1) {
            $found[] = $label;
        }
    }

    return $found;
}

/**
 * The ability strings a body names. `VAR:$x` records a non-literal ability, which is
 * itself a finding: such a route cannot be converted to `can:` without first resolving
 * the variable, and Task 3-9 reports it in the fixture registry's `notes`.
 *
 * @return list<string>
 */
function extractPermissionStrings(string $src): array
{
    $out = [];

    if (preg_match_all(
        '/(?:->can|->authorize|->hasPermissionTo|Gate::(?:allows|check|authorize|denies))\(\s*[\'"]([a-zA-Z0-9_.\-:]+)[\'"]/',
        $src,
        $m
    ) > 0) {
        foreach ($m[1] as $s) {
            $out[] = $s;
        }
    }

    if (preg_match_all('/->authorize\(\s*(\$[a-zA-Z0-9_]+)/', $src, $m) > 0) {
        foreach ($m[1] as $s) {
            $out[] = 'VAR:'.$s;
        }
    }

    return array_values(array_unique($out));
}

$rows = [];

/** @var Illuminate\Routing\Route $route */
foreach (app('router')->getRoutes() as $route) {
    $uri = '/'.ltrim($route->uri(), '/');

    // The sweep's denominator is the API surface, exactly as the audit's CSV is:
    // 1054 of the 1092 registered routes. The 38 excluded are Horizon/Debugbar/
    // Scramble/health/storage/sanctum/broadcasting/tenancy infrastructure.
    if (! str_starts_with($uri, '/api/')) {
        continue;
    }

    $middleware = array_values(array_filter($route->gatherMiddleware(), 'is_string'));
    $action = $route->getAction();
    $uses = $action['uses'] ?? null;

    $class = null;
    $method = null;
    $isClosure = false;
    $ctrlSrc = '';
    $ctrlFile = null;
    $ctrlLine = null;

    if ($route->getActionName() === 'Closure') {
        $isClosure = true;

        if ($uses instanceof Closure) {
            $rf = new ReflectionFunction($uses);
            $ctrlSrc = methodSource($rf);
            $ctrlFile = $rf->getFileName() ?: null;
            $ctrlLine = $rf->getStartLine() ?: null;
        }
    } elseif (is_string($uses) && str_contains($uses, '@')) {
        [$class, $method] = explode('@', $uses, 2);
    } elseif (is_array($uses) && count($uses) === 2) {
        $class = is_object($uses[0]) ? get_class($uses[0]) : $uses[0];
        $method = $uses[1];
    }

    $ctrlPatterns = $isClosure ? findPatterns($ctrlSrc) : [];
    $ctrlPerms = $isClosure ? extractPermissionStrings($ctrlSrc) : [];
    $frClass = null;
    $frOpen = null;          // true => authorize() is an unconditional `return true`
    $frPerms = [];
    $serviceHops = [];
    $reflError = null;

    if ($class !== null && class_exists($class) && $method !== null && method_exists($class, $method)) {
        try {
            $rc = new ReflectionClass($class);
            $rm = $rc->getMethod($method);
            $ctrlFile = $rm->getFileName() ?: null;
            $ctrlLine = $rm->getStartLine() ?: null;
            $ctrlSrc = methodSource($rm);
            $ctrlPatterns = findPatterns($ctrlSrc);
            $ctrlPerms = extractPermissionStrings($ctrlSrc);

            // FormRequest params resolved by TYPE, never by a *Request name convention.
            foreach ($rm->getParameters() as $p) {
                $t = $p->getType();

                if (! $t instanceof ReflectionNamedType || $t->isBuiltin()) {
                    continue;
                }

                $tn = $t->getName();

                if (! class_exists($tn) || ! is_subclass_of($tn, Illuminate\Foundation\Http\FormRequest::class)) {
                    continue;
                }

                $frClass = $tn;
                $frc = new ReflectionClass($tn);

                if (! $frc->hasMethod('authorize')) {
                    // No override => the base FormRequest::authorize() returns true.
                    $frOpen = true;
                    continue;
                }

                $frSrc = methodSource($frc->getMethod('authorize'));
                $frPerms = extractPermissionStrings($frSrc);
                $frOpen = preg_match(
                    '/function\s+authorize\s*\([^)]*\)[^{]*\{\s*return\s+true\s*;\s*\}/s',
                    $frSrc
                ) === 1;
            }

            // One-hop scan into constructor-injected services.
            $serviceTypes = [];

            if ($rc->hasMethod('__construct')) {
                foreach ($rc->getMethod('__construct')->getParameters() as $p) {
                    $t = $p->getType();

                    if ($t instanceof ReflectionNamedType && ! $t->isBuiltin()) {
                        $serviceTypes[$p->getName()] = $t->getName();
                    }
                }
            }

            if (preg_match_all('/\$this->([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)->([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)\(/', $ctrlSrc, $mm, PREG_SET_ORDER) > 0) {
                $seen = [];

                foreach ($mm as [$_, $prop, $called]) {
                    if (isset($seen[$prop.'::'.$called]) || ! isset($serviceTypes[$prop])) {
                        continue;
                    }

                    $seen[$prop.'::'.$called] = true;
                    $svc = $serviceTypes[$prop];

                    if (! class_exists($svc)) {
                        continue;
                    }

                    $svcRc = new ReflectionClass($svc);

                    if (! $svcRc->hasMethod($called)) {
                        continue;
                    }

                    $svcRm = $svcRc->getMethod($called);
                    $svcSrc = methodSource($svcRm);
                    $pats = findPatterns($svcSrc);
                    $perms = extractPermissionStrings($svcSrc);

                    if ($pats !== [] || $perms !== []) {
                        $serviceHops[] = [
                            'class' => $svc,
                            'method' => $called,
                            'file' => $svcRm->getFileName() ?: null,
                            'line' => $svcRm->getStartLine() ?: null,
                            'patterns' => $pats,
                            'perm_strings' => $perms,
                        ];
                    }
                }
            }
        } catch (Throwable $e) {
            $reflError = $e->getMessage();
        }
    } elseif ($class !== null) {
        $reflError = "class_or_method_not_found:{$class}@{$method}";
    }

    $aliasArgs = static function (string $alias) use ($middleware): array {
        $out = [];

        foreach ($middleware as $m) {
            $parts = explode(':', $m, 2);

            if ($parts[0] === $alias) {
                $out[] = $parts[1] ?? '(no-arg)';
            }
        }

        return $out;
    };

    $isAuth = false;
    $superadminMw = [];

    foreach ($middleware as $m) {
        $base = explode(':', $m, 2)[0];

        if ($base === 'auth') {
            $isAuth = true;
        }

        if (in_array($base, ['super_admin', 'central_admin', 'central_admin_role'], true)) {
            $superadminMw[] = $m;
        }
    }

    $canArgs = $aliasArgs('can');
    $ramArgs = $aliasArgs('require.any.permission');

    $policyLike = false;

    foreach ($ctrlPerms as $p) {
        if (! str_contains($p, '.') && ! str_starts_with($p, 'VAR:') && in_array($p, POLICY_BARE_WORDS, true)) {
            $policyLike = true;
        }
    }

    $classification = match (true) {
        ! $isAuth => 'PUBLIC',
        $canArgs !== [] || $ramArgs !== [] => 'MIDDLEWARE',
        $superadminMw !== [] => 'SUPERADMIN_ONLY',
        $ctrlPatterns !== [] => ($policyLike
            && (in_array('Gate::', $ctrlPatterns, true) || in_array('authorize()', $ctrlPatterns, true)))
                ? 'POLICY'
                : 'CONTROLLER',
        $frClass !== null && $frOpen === false => 'FORMREQUEST',
        $serviceHops !== [] => 'SERVICE',
        default => 'AUTH_ONLY',
    };

    // The 4 retired POS closures return HTTP 410 unconditionally. The audit records
    // them AUTH_ONLY for mechanical accuracy; they are behaviourally inert.
    if ($isClosure && $class === null) {
        $classification = 'AUTH_ONLY';
    }

    $permissionKeys = match ($classification) {
        'MIDDLEWARE' => array_merge($canArgs, array_merge(...array_map(
            static fn (string $a): array => array_map('trim', explode(',', $a)),
            $ramArgs
        ) ?: [[]])),
        'CONTROLLER', 'POLICY' => $ctrlPerms,
        'FORMREQUEST' => $frPerms,
        default => [],
    };

    $rows[] = [
        'method' => implode('|', $route->methods()),
        'uri' => $uri,
        'name' => $route->getName(),
        'controller' => $class ?? '(closure)',
        'action' => $method ?? '',
        'controller_file' => $ctrlFile,
        'controller_line' => $ctrlLine,
        'middleware_permission' => implode('; ', array_merge(
            $canArgs,
            array_map(static fn (string $a): string => "any-of({$a})", $ramArgs)
        )),
        'classification' => $classification,
        'permission' => $permissionKeys[0] ?? null,
        'permission_source' => match ($classification) {
            'MIDDLEWARE' => 'middleware',
            'CONTROLLER', 'POLICY' => 'controller',
            'FORMREQUEST' => 'formrequest',
            default => null,
        },
        'permission_keys' => array_values($permissionKeys),
        'form_request' => $frClass,
        'service_hops' => $serviceHops,
        'refl_error' => $reflError,
        'notes' => in_array($classification, ['CONTROLLER', 'FORMREQUEST'], true) && $permissionKeys === []
            ? 'check present but ability is not a literal — resolve before converting'
            : '',
    ];
}

usort($rows, static fn (array $a, array $b): int => [$a['uri'], $a['method']] <=> [$b['uri'], $b['method']]);

$tally = [];

foreach ($rows as $r) {
    $tally[$r['classification']] = ($tally[$r['classification']] ?? 0) + 1;
}

ksort($tally);

fwrite(STDERR, 'rows: '.count($rows).PHP_EOL);

foreach ($tally as $bucket => $n) {
    fwrite(STDERR, sprintf('%5d  %s%s', $n, $bucket, PHP_EOL));
}

// The BASELINE is the sweep's scope: only the two buckets this wave converts.
$baseline = array_values(array_filter(
    $rows,
    static fn (array $r): bool => in_array($r['classification'], ['CONTROLLER', 'FORMREQUEST'], true),
));

$out = getenv('ENFORCEMENT_BASELINE_OUT')
    ?: $root.'/tests/Architecture/baselines/enforcement-style-baseline.json';

@mkdir(dirname($out), 0o775, true);

file_put_contents(
    $out,
    json_encode($baseline, JSON_PRETTY_PRINT | JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES | JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE)."\n"
);

if (($rowsOut = getenv('ENFORCEMENT_ROWS_OUT')) !== false && $rowsOut !== '') {
    @mkdir(dirname($rowsOut), 0o775, true);
    file_put_contents($rowsOut, json_encode($rows, JSON_PRETTY_PRINT | JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES)."\n");
}

fwrite(STDERR, sprintf('baseline: %d entries written to %s%s', count($baseline), $out, PHP_EOL));

$errors = count(array_filter($rows, static fn (array $r): bool => $r['refl_error'] !== null));
fwrite(STDERR, sprintf('reflection errors: %d%s', $errors, PHP_EOL));
~~~

      **The exact command — it is a PLAIN `php` invocation, not `tinker --execute`.** The generator boots the framework itself (`require vendor/autoload.php`, `require bootstrap/app.php`, `Kernel::bootstrap()`), which is what the audit's `analyze_routes.php` did, so it needs no host command and no registered console command:
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w3/apps/api
      DB_CONNECTION=pgsql DB_HOST=127.0.0.1 DB_PORT=5433 DB_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w3 DB_CENTRAL_DATABASE=autoerp_test_w3_central \
        ./vendor/bin/phpunit tests/Architecture/RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest.php

      php artisan route:list --json > "$SCRATCH/routes-w3-base.json"     # the raw registry, for the handback

      # Writes tests/Architecture/baselines/enforcement-style-baseline.json in the lane.
      php tools/generate-enforcement-baseline.php 2>&1 | tee "$SCRATCH/w3-enforcement-tally.txt"

      # The same generator, READ-ONLY, when you want the tally without touching the tree
      # (this is the form the plan-gate fix round used against the shared checkout):
      ENFORCEMENT_BASELINE_OUT="$SCRATCH/enforcement-style-baseline.json" \
      ENFORCEMENT_ROWS_OUT="$SCRATCH/rows.json" \
        php tools/generate-enforcement-baseline.php
      ```
      **This is the output it ACTUALLY produced**, run on 2026-09-11 from `/Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/apps/api` (the shared checkout, read-only, both outputs redirected into the session scratchpad) against plan-lane HEAD `cbc95ecd8`:
      ```
      rows: 1054
        142  AUTH_ONLY
        130  CONTROLLER
         46  FORMREQUEST
        642  MIDDLEWARE
          8  POLICY
         18  PUBLIC
         68  SUPERADMIN_ONLY
      baseline: 176 entries written to <SCRATCH>/enforcement-style-baseline.json
      reflection errors: 0
      ```
      **That is the audit's CSV, bucket for bucket** — `642 / 142 / 130 / 68 / 46 / 18 / 8`, summing to 1 054, baseline `130 + 46 = 176`, with **zero** reflection errors, which is the same *"`refl_error` empty for every row"* the audit recorded (`02-route-enforcement-sweep.md` §Method step 5). **This is therefore no longer an expectation; it is a measurement, and Phase 0 re-takes it at the lane base.** The acceptance criterion is unchanged: **if the tally is not within a handful of these numbers, STOP and reconcile before converting anything.** Two legitimate sources of drift, each named per route in the commit body rather than absorbed into a number: a route waves 0a–2 have already gated (it moves `CONTROLLER` → `MIDDLEWARE`), and a route wave 2a/2b **added** (`/service-accounts/*`, which ship gated and should appear as `MIDDLEWARE`). **An unexplained difference is a finding**, because it means either the generator or the audit is wrong about what the router contains.
      > **Why a plain `php` invocation and not `tinker --execute`.** Rev 3 prescribed `php artisan tinker --execute="require 'tools/…';"`. That is a second interpreter between the plan and the measurement, it swallows the script's own exit status, and it is not how the audit ran. The generator boots the framework in its own process instead, which is what makes the read-only env-var form above possible at all — and that form is what let this plan's numbers be **executed** rather than asserted. **Task 3-1 Step 1 stages `tools/generate-enforcement-baseline.php` itself** (it is re-run after every cluster to prove the baseline shrank), so the file is committed and its invocation is a committed command rather than an ad-hoc one.
      > **The generator was also used to re-derive every count in this plan.** The eleven non-POS cluster sizes, the twenty-four POS controller classes, the POS FormRequest's owning controller and every add list below come from `ENFORCEMENT_ROWS_OUT`'s rows, grouped — not from grep and not from the CSV re-parsed by hand.
      > **`permission` / `permission_source` / `permission_keys` are the fields Task 3-9's fixture registry consumes** for the routes that carry no `can:` to read (**BLOCKER B3**). A `notes`-flagged row whose ability is not a literal (`VAR:$permission`) is a **finding to raise before converting that route**, not a route to guess at.
- [ ] **Record every ceiling before anything moves:** `UNCOVERED_WRITE_CEILING`, `UNCOVERED_READ_CEILING`, the two PHPStan baselines' entry counts, `SOD_BASELINE_CEILING`, the role-name baseline's last entry, and every feature-lane-manifest group this wave touches.
- [ ] **Run `permissions:report-dead` and record its output verbatim** — Task 3-7's gate depends on it, and running it once at the start tells you immediately whether that task is in or out of this wave.
      ```

      ```
- [ ] **Re-pin the POS facts:**

Rev 1 recorded it as *"called from tests only"*, which was false at this SHA (**Q-w3-4**, plan gate r1 **MAJOR M3-1**). **If the grep shows fewer than those four call sites, that is a finding** — something unwired it between `630afa86f` and the lane base — and Task 3-3 grows a wiring step rather than only a pinning one.
- [ ] **Re-pin `MembershipRole`'s single authorization read:**

      ```

Never the MCP browser: its Chrome profile is shared and often locked by another session.

---

## Task 3-1: the enforcement-style sweep — eleven non-POS clusters

**Files:** the eleven module directories listed in File Structure, plus `apps/api/tests/Architecture/EnforcementStyleRatchetTest.php` and one feature test per cluster.

**Interfaces**

- Produces: `EnforcementStyleRatchetTest` with a shrink-only baseline; per-cluster `<Module>RouteEnforcementTest` with allow **and** deny assertions.
- Consumes: wave 0a's `RoutePermissionCoverageRatchetTest` and its two ceilings; wave 1's `PermissionRegistry` for the key that each converted route names.

**The recipe, applied identically 176 times.** It is written once here and referenced by every cluster; a cluster that deviates says why in its commit body.

1. **Read the existing check** and write down, literally, *who could do this before* — the permission string, or the role name, or the ownership predicate.
2. **Classify it against E-1..E-4.** A **dotted permission** check → move to `can:` on the route (E-1). An **any-of** → `require.any.permission:a,b`. A **row-level ownership** check → **leave it in the controller or move it to a policy** (E-3); it is not a middleware. A FormRequest `authorize()` performing a real check → the check moves to the route and `authorize()` becomes `return true;` **or** an exact duplicate of the middleware (E-4).
3. **Add the route middleware.** Use `'can:'.SomeModulePermission::Case->value`, never a bare string literal — wave 1's `RoutePermissionLiteralTest` **accepts** that form and `ForbidPermissionStringLiteral` rejects the literal.
4. **Keep or delete the controller check** per E-2: keep it only where it is genuine defence in depth (e.g. a service called from more than one entry point); delete it where it merely duplicates the new middleware and would drift.
5. **Write the allow and the deny test.** Allow: a principal holding the key gets 2xx **and the row is written**. Deny: a principal **not** holding it gets 403 **and the row is not written**.
6. **Delete the route's baseline entry** in `enforcement-style-baseline.json` and decrement `UNCOVERED_WRITE_CEILING` or `UNCOVERED_READ_CEILING` in the **same commit**.
7. **Assert the before/after sets are identical.** If they differ, stop: record it in the handback as a finding and leave the route unconverted rather than shipping a silent widening.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — write `EnforcementStyleRatchetTest` first, and name the assertion that is actually red.** It reuses `tools/generate-enforcement-baseline.php` (never a second classifier — convention 11) and asserts three things about the live set against the committed baseline: **subset** (every live `CONTROLLER`/`FORMREQUEST` route has a baseline entry — the **anti-growth** direction), **no stale entries** (every baseline entry is still live and still unconverted — fails with *"remove this entry"*), and **ceiling** (`count(live) <= ENFORCEMENT_STYLE_CEILING`).
      > **The first-red claim is now truthful** (gate r3, Wave 3 **MAJOR 1**). Rev 3 said this test is *"red on the full baseline"*. It is not: on the freshly generated full baseline the live set **equals** the baseline, so subset holds, no entry is stale, and `176 <= 176` holds — **all three assertions are green, by construction.** A test that is green the moment it is written proves nothing about the sweep.
      >
      > **Rev 4's answer to that was self-contradictory and rev 5 replaces it outright** (gate r4, Wave 3 **BLOCKER 1** and **MAJOR 1**). Rev 4 committed `ENFORCEMENT_STYLE_CEILING` at the wave's **exit** value `0` *and* told every cluster to decrement it as a running total. **A constant already at zero cannot be decremented 176 times**, and a ceiling committed at `0` leaves the branch deliberately red through all thirty-six later conversion commits — against this wave's own per-cluster test-and-reviewer gate (`…rbac-wave-3.md` Task 3-1 Step 10, the Rollback section) and against CI.
      >
      > **THE `ENFORCEMENT_STYLE_CEILING` PROTOCOL, in three rules and one table.**
      > 1. **It is committed at the MEASURED starting value, `176`** — not at `0`, and not at a number anybody guessed. `176` is what `tools/generate-enforcement-baseline.php` printed when it was executed read-only against the shared checkout on 2026-09-11 (`baseline: 176 entries`, `130 CONTROLLER + 46 FORMREQUEST`, `1054` rows, **zero** reflection errors). **Phase 0 re-takes the measurement at the lane base and the ceiling is set to what Phase 0 prints**, not to the literal `176`, if the two differ — the two legitimate drift sources (a route waves 0a–2 already gated; a route wave 2a/2b added) are named per route in the commit body.
      > 2. **Each cluster commit lowers it by exactly that cluster's row count**, in the same commit as the conversion and the baseline-entry deletions (recipe step 6). The ceiling assertion is therefore `count(live) <= ENFORCEMENT_STYLE_CEILING` and is **green at every commit boundary** — which is what makes each cluster independently reviewable, revertible and CI-clean.
      > 3. **The FIRST RED is observed BEFORE the first commit, and it is a pre-implementation red rather than a committed one.** Step 1 writes the test with the ceiling at the post-`0.6.1` value (`175`) and runs it against the **unconverted** tree: `176 > 175` fails, which is the red that proves the ceiling assertion is wired to the live count and not to the baseline file. `Phase 0.6.1`'s conversion makes it green in the same commit, and the ceiling is committed at `175`. Nothing is ever committed red. *(The **subset** and **stale** directions are the anti-drift guards and are green throughout — which is their job, and it is why they are not the red.)*
      >
      > **The per-commit value table, derived from the generator's REAL output** (the per-module grouping of `ENFORCEMENT_ROWS_OUT`'s 176 baseline rows on `controller`/`form_request`, and — for POS — the per-class grouping that Q-w3-3's table already carries). A cluster commit whose diff does not write **exactly** its row's value is wrong, and the reviewer has one number to check:

      > **The sum of the "rows converted" column is 176 and the last row writes `0`** — that is the arithmetic identity a reviewer checks the table by, and it is the only place `0` appears. **If a cluster's commit forgets its decrement**, the ceiling is *above* the live count and the ceiling assertion stays green — which is why recipe step 6 also **deletes the baseline entries**, and why the **stale** direction is what catches that omission: a converted route that kept its baseline entry fails immediately, in that cluster's own commit.
      > **Task 3-6 step 1's tail (`0.6.48` onward) does NOT touch this ceiling.** It converts permission-string *literals* to registry enum cases; it converts no enforcement site, so the live `CONTROLLER`/`FORMREQUEST` count is already `0` when it starts and stays there.
### The twelve add lists, ENUMERATED — every path written out in full

> **Rev 3's table still abbreviated.** It used a leading `…` for the module prefix and a trailing *"+ tail"* for the three files every block stages, and the plan's own Global Constraint forbids a path that is not a path (gate r2 **MAJOR 3**; gate r3, Wave 3 **MAJOR 2**). Rev 4 writes **every** path in full, including the three tail files, in **every** row. Nothing below is a template, a prefix or a reference.
>
> **Where these lists come from.** Not from the CSV re-parsed by hand and not from grep: from `ENFORCEMENT_ROWS_OUT`'s rows — the **executed** generator's own output — grouped on the `controller` column for the `CONTROLLER` rows and on `form_request` for the `FORMREQUEST` rows, with each class resolved to its path through `ReflectionClass::getFileName()` in the same pass. The per-cluster counts below are that grouping's, and they sum to 176:
>
> ```
> Replenishment 1   Voucher 2   Tenant 2   PlatformIntegration 2   Promotion 4
> Coupon 3          BatchExpiry 4          Menu 6                  Identity 14
> Scheduling 17     Workshop 29            POS 92                  = 176
> ```
>
> **Route counts and CLASS counts are different numbers, and both are stated.** Identity is **7 `CONTROLLER` + 7 `FORMREQUEST` routes** but only **1 controller class + 6 FormRequest classes** — `UpdateRoleRequest` gates two routes. Workshop is **14 + 15 routes** across **9 controller classes + 15 FormRequest classes**. A reviewer counting files against route counts and finding them unequal is seeing this, not an omission.
>
> **`apps/api/app/Modules/Tenant/routes.php` has no `Presentation/` segment** (`git ls-tree -r --name-only dev` confirms), unlike the other eleven. That is the module's layout, not a typo. **Workshop has three route files** (`WorkOrder`, `Bundle`, `Technician`) and `0.6.11` stages all three because its nine controllers span all three.
>
> **The three tail files are in every row, written out, and they are load-bearing:** the baseline entries deleted, the ratchet whose ceiling is decremented, and wave 0a's coverage ratchet whose two ceilings are decremented — all **in the same commit as the conversion that earns them**. A partial revert that leaves any of them behind turns a ratchet red for a reason unrelated to the revert's intent (see Rollback).
>
> **Phase 0 re-resolves every path at the lane base.** Wave 2a rewrote the two Identity controllers end to end; a path that has moved is re-pinned there and the handback records the move. **The re-resolution is the same command that produced this table** — re-run the generator and re-group — not a manual sweep.

      const serverErrors: string[] = []

## Task 3-2: the POS cluster — 91 conversions, one commit per controller

**Files:** `apps/api/app/Modules/POS/Presentation/Controllers/*` and their routes; one `tests/Feature/POS/<Controller>EnforcementTest.php` per controller.

**Why POS is different and last.** Three reasons, each of which changes how the recipe is applied: it is **most** of the sweep (91 of **176**); its clients are **offline-capable**, so a gate that starts denying is not a 403 the operator can retry past; and its contracts are **device-authored** (CLAUDE.md rule 20) — a permission that gates a *sync* endpoint can silently strand a device's queue rather than showing an error.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — enumerate from the LIVE ROUTER; use grep only as a secondary census.** `grep -rc` counts **textual authorization calls**: a route whose controller action calls `Gate::authorize` twice contributes two, and a call site in a private helper or a non-route method contributes one while gating no route at all. **The route arithmetic is a route fact, so the router is the oracle** (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M3-5**). Rev 1 had the grep as the primary source, which is how 91 route conversions could be planned against a number that counts something else.
      **Primary — from Phase 0's regenerated classification**, filtered to POS and grouped by controller:
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w3/apps/api
      php -r '$b = json_decode(file_get_contents("tests/Architecture/baselines/enforcement-style-baseline.json"), true);
        $by = [];
        foreach ($b as $row) {
          if (! str_contains($row["controller"], "Modules\\POS\\")) { continue; }
          $c = substr($row["controller"], strrpos($row["controller"], "\\") + 1);
          $by[$c][$row["classification"]] = ($by[$c][$row["classification"]] ?? 0) + 1;
        }
        ksort($by); print_r($by);
        printf("%d rows across %d classes%s", count(array_filter($b, fn ($r) => str_contains($r["controller"], "Modules\\POS\\"))), count($by), PHP_EOL);'
      ```
      **This exact grouping was run on 2026-09-11 against the shared checkout and printed 25 classes: 24 with `CONTROLLER` rows summing to 91, and `ManagerPinController` with `FORMREQUEST => 1`.** That is Q-w3-3's table, measured rather than asserted.
      **Measured total, from the executed generator: 91 `CONTROLLER` rows across TWENTY-FOUR controller classes, plus 1 `FORMREQUEST` row hosted by a TWENTY-FIFTH class** — 92 POS rows of the 176, 25 classes in all. Grouping `ENFORCEMENT_ROWS_OUT` by `controller` printed exactly the Q-w3-3 table below.
      **The one `FORMREQUEST` row is `VerifyManagerPinRequest` on `ManagerPinController::verify`, NOT on `PosAuthController`** (gate r3, Wave 3 **MINOR 1** — rev 3 said PosAuth and staged it with that commit). The measured row is:
      ```
      POST /api/v1/pos/verify-manager-pin
        controller      App\Modules\POS\Presentation\Controllers\ManagerPinController::verify
        form_request    App\Modules\POS\Presentation\Requests\VerifyManagerPinRequest
        permission      null          <- no literal ability; the generator flags this in `notes`
      ```
      confirmed at `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/POS/Presentation/Controllers/ManagerPinController.php:40` and routed at `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/POS/routes.php:260`. It therefore gets **its own ordinal, `Phase 0.6.37`**, and its own test — `ManagerPinControllerEnforcementTest` — rather than riding on PosAuth's commit.
      > **And it is a FINDING, recorded before it is converted.** `VerifyManagerPinRequest::authorize()` is `return $this->user() !== null;` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/POS/Presentation/Requests/VerifyManagerPinRequest.php:24-27`). That is an **authentication** check, not a permission check; it classifies `FORMREQUEST` only because the audit's open-FormRequest detector matches an unconditional `return true;` and this is not literally that. So this route is **effectively AUTH_ONLY** and its conversion is not *"move an existing key to the route"* — it is *"decide which key gates manager-PIN verification and add it"*. That decision is recorded in the commit body with the reviewer who approved it, and it is the one POS route in this task whose conversion **changes who can call it**. Do not convert it silently alongside the other 91.
      **Secondary — the implementation census**, run for a different question: *"did I convert every call site inside the controller I just changed?"*
      ```
      grep -rc 'Gate::authorize\|->can(\|->cant(\|authorize(' app/Modules/POS/Presentation/Controllers/ | sort -t: -k2 -rn
      ```
      **At `dev:630afa86f` this grep's `Gate::authorize` distribution was:** `OrderController` 9, `AnalyticsController` 8, `PosAuthController` 5, `CashDrawerController` 4, `KitchenDisplayController` 4, `PosReplenishmentController` 2, and singles in `AuditEventSyncController`, `PosCustomerSyncController`, `PosPendingCustomerController`, `PosStockLevelController` — **thirty-eight call sites across ten controllers, which is NOT the ninety-one routes**, because one action gates one route and one call site may serve several actions. **Where the two disagree, the router wins and the difference is recorded in the commit body.** Re-run both; wave 2b edited `PosAuthController`.
- [ ] **Step 2 — convert the SEVEN device-sync controllers first, and treat them as a special case.** `AuditEventSyncController`, `PosCustomerSyncController`, `PosPendingCustomerController`, `PosStockLevelController`, `SyncController`, `ZReportSyncController`, `VoucherSyncController` — `Phase 0.6.13` … `0.6.19` in that order. Rev 2 named only the first four; the other three are the same shape and the retry rule below applies to all seven or it is arbitrary. For each, the deny path must be checked **against the device's retry behaviour**: a 403 on a sync endpoint must surface on the device as a **visible authority problem**, not as a silently retried queue. If the device treats 403 like a transient failure, **record it as a finding and leave the gate as it is** — this is exactly the class of defect rule 20 exists for (*"retiring/gating a server endpoint: the client's fallback path becomes the PRIMARY path"*).
- [ ] **Step 3 — convert the remaining SEVENTEEN controllers, one commit each with its own subject**, largest last, **then `ManagerPinController` as an eighteenth and final commit**. The subjects are **`Phase 0.6.20` … `Phase 0.6.36`**, in Q-w3-3's table order: six one-route controllers, then `PosReplenishmentController` (2), `CashDrawerController` (4), `KitchenDisplayController` (4), `PosAuthController` (5), `ReceiptController` (5), `ShiftController` (6), `ReportController` (7), `AnalyticsController` (8), `OrderController` (8), `TableController` (10), `TerminalController` (16) — and **`Phase 0.6.37` is `ManagerPinController`**, last because it is the one POS commit that **changes who can call a route** rather than moving an existing check (Step 1's finding). Step 2's **seven** device-sync controllers took `Phase 0.6.13` … `Phase 0.6.19`. **Twenty-five classes, twenty-five distinct subjects**; rev 1 gave all of them `Phase 0.6.2` (plan gate r1, **minor m3-3**), rev 2 allocated ten ordinals for twenty-four commits (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 3**), and rev 3 folded the twenty-fifth into PosAuth's (gate r3, **MINOR 1**).
      **`PosAuthController` needs care**: wave 2b added the `principal_kind` checks and the `pinHolders()` predicate there. Its `Gate::authorize` calls gate the **terminal's** authority, while `verifyPin` returns the **PIN holder's** grants — two different subjects in one controller. Do not collapse them.
- [ ] **Step 4 — the dead `pos.*` keys are NOT pruned here.** Fourteen `pos.*` keys are in wave 1's 22-key deprecation set; **deprecation does not change runtime behaviour** (the row survives one release). Their **removal** is Task 3-7, gated on Q-w3-5. This task only converts enforcement style.
- [ ] **Step 5 — per-controller allow/deny tests**, and the ceiling decremented in the same commit as its controller.
- [ ] **Step 6 — the POS browser probe.** Drive the dev IziPOS build and complete a **real sale** end to end as a cashier, then a **manager override** via PIN step-up, asserting zero 5xx and zero console errors. **"The POS compiles" is not evidence for a wave that moved 91 authorization checks** (CLAUDE.md rule 5: verify the critical path).
- [ ] **Step 7 — commit each class**, with its own subject and ordinal from Q-w3-3's table (`Phase 0.6.13` … `Phase 0.6.37`: *"Move `<the named controller>`'s in-controller permission checks to route middleware"* — the controller is **named** in the subject, never a token), both trailers.
      **All TWENTY-FIVE add lists, written out.** Rev 3 printed three and said the other twenty-one were *"exactly these paths with that row's controller name substituted at the commit"* — which is a template, and the plan's own Global Constraints forbid one (gate r3, Wave 3 **MAJOR 2**). Every path below is literal; the route file is **that class's own**, taken from the four-file map in Q-w3-3, not `routes.php` repeated twenty-five times.
