## Task 3-3: POS legacy cleanup — wire the terminal ceiling, then remove the ladder

**Files**

- Modify: `dev:apps/pos/src/stores/operatorStore.ts:348-372` (the residual ladder at `:354` and the two fields it feeds), `dev:apps/pos/src/lib/auth/roles.ts`. **`dev:apps/pos/src/api/discountApi.ts` is NOT modified** — `fetchDiscountPermissions` is already correct and already wired (Q-w3-4); this task **pins** it with a regression test rather than rewriting it
- Create: `apps/pos/src/lib/auth/__tests__/offlineAuthority.test.ts`
- Modify: `apps/pos/src/lib/auth/__tests__/roles.test.ts` (existing, at `dev:apps/pos/src/lib/auth/roles.test.ts`)
- Modify: **`apps/web/eslint.config.js`** — the `no-role-name-authorization` rule's shrink-only **allowlist** loses its last entry. **It lives there, not in `apps/api/phpstan.neon`**: wave 1 rev 6 Task 1.16 Step 5 ships *"the ESLint companion … with its own shrink-only allowlist in `apps/web/eslint.config.js`"* and stages `apps/web/eslint.config.js` + `apps/web/tools/eslint-rules/no-role-name-authorization.mjs`. Rev 1 of this plan located it in `phpstan.neon`, which is a PHP-only config (plan gate r1, cross-plan **MAJOR 2**, citation audit item 5)
- Dispose of: `docs/superpowers/tickets/2026-09-10-batch-writes-ignore-location-scope.md`

**The order is load-bearing (Q-w3-4).** **Pin** the already-wired terminal-aware source first; remove the residual ladder second. Reversing it ships an admin who cannot discount at PIN setup.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — write the offline test first (red).** `offlineAuthority.test.ts` asserts, against a cached operator row with **no role names at all**:
      - an operator whose cached `permissions` include `pos.view_reports` **has** manager access to the reports surface;
      - the same operator with `authority_stale: true` **does not** (`dev:apps/pos/src/lib/auth/roles.ts:123` is the guard that stays, and the existing `roles.test.ts:173-185` already asserts this half — extend it rather than duplicating);
      - an operator whose cached `permissions` are **empty** and whose `roles` say `['admin']` **does not** — i.e. the ladder is gone and a role name grants nothing;
      - an operator who set a PIN **offline** retains the discount authority of their **last synced payload**, not `100`.
- [ ] **Step 2 — PIN the existing terminal-aware flow, and close the ONE path it does not cover.** `fetchDiscountPermissions` is already imported and called (`dev:apps/pos/src/stores/operatorStore.ts:4,136`) from `resolveOnlineDiscountPermissions()`, which runs on offline acceptance (`:269`), online PIN verification (`:294`), setup (`:368`) and refresh (`:446`). **Do not re-implement it.** Two things to do instead:
      1. **A regression test that pins all four call sites**, so a later refactor cannot quietly unwire them: `operatorStore.discountSource.test.ts` asserts that each of the four entry points results in a `fetchDiscountPermissions(terminalCode, operatorId)` call and that the resulting operator row's `max_discount_percent` is `min(terminal.max_discount_percent, user.max_discount_percent)` with `user.can_discount` as the gate. `discountApi.terminalContext.test.ts` (`dev:apps/pos/src/api/__tests__/discountApi.terminalContext.test.ts`) already pins the call's terminal/operator arguments — **extend it, do not replace it**.
      2. **The setup path's pre-resolution window.** At `dev:354-364` the store assigns `can_discount` and `max_discount_percent` from the role ladder **before** `resolveOnlineDiscountPermissions()` at `:368` returns, and keeps those values when the call fails or the device is offline. That window is the only place a role name still decides discount authority, and Step 3 closes it by seeding those two fields from the **last synced payload** instead.
      > **Terminal-aware means per-terminal, not per-device-session.** The value is fetched for the terminal the operator is signed into and re-fetched on terminal change; an operator moving tills must not carry the previous till's ceiling. That property is already true of the existing implementation and the regression test above is what keeps it true.
      > **`pos_terminals.max_discount_percent` defaults to `0.00`**, i.e. a freshly provisioned till blocks every discount until an operator sets it. That is server-enforced today and stays so; the device now shows the same number, which is the point — a cashier should not meet a 422 they could not predict.
- [ ] **Step 3 — delete the ladder.** Remove `ROLE_LEVEL`, `getAccessLevel()`, `isManagerRole()` and the role-name arm of `hasManagerAccess` from `dev:apps/pos/src/lib/auth/roles.ts`; remove `const isAdmin = user.roles.includes('super_admin') || user.roles.includes('admin')` at `dev:apps/pos/src/stores/operatorStore.ts:354` and the two fields it feeds (`can_discount: isAdmin`, `max_discount_percent: isAdmin ? 100 : null`, `dev:361-363`). **They are replaced by the operator's last synced `can_discount` / `max_discount_percent`**, read from the cached operator row — which `resolveOnlineDiscountPermissions()` has been writing on every successful call — and by `{ can_discount: false, max_discount_percent: null }` for an operator who has **never** synced. A never-synced operator setting a PIN offline having no discount authority is correct and is the fourth case in Step 1's test; an *admin* in that state is a provisioning problem, not an authority problem.
      > **`'super_admin'` was already dead.** It reads against the **tenant** login user, whose roles come from the seeded set, and `super_admin` is a **central** model on its own guard — it can never appear there. Only the `'admin'` half was ever reachable. That is worth stating in the commit body, because a reviewer looking at the deleted line will otherwise assume a super-admin capability was removed.
- [ ] **Step 4 — empty the ESLint allowlist.** With `operatorStore.ts:354` gone and wave 2a having removed `usePermissions.ts:244,251`, the `no-role-name-authorization` **allowlist in `apps/web/eslint.config.js`** holds nothing but the two waived central-guard sites; reduce it to those and switch the rule to hard-fail on everything else. **There is no separate baseline FILE to delete** — the allowlist is inline in the ESLint config (wave 1 rev 6, Task 1.16 Step 5). The PHP-side `apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-role-name-baseline.json` is a **different** artefact and is emptied by Task 3-1/3-2's conversions; it **is** a file, and it is deleted when it reaches zero. The **two waived central-guard sites** (`AdminLayout.tsx:60`, `adminRolePolicy.ts:52`) stay waived — they test `SuperAdmin.role` against a policy table, not a Spatie role, and the waiver is an explicit allow-list **in the rule**, not an absent baseline entry.
- [ ] **Step 5 — dispose of the batch-location ticket, explicitly.** `docs/superpowers/tickets/2026-09-10-batch-writes-ignore-location-scope.md` records that `DELETE /batches/{uuid}` and `POST /batches/{uuid}/recall` consult no location scope. **This wave does not close it silently.** Either (a) close it by adding `LocationScopeResolver` to the two writes **with the inventory reviewer mandatory** — a stock-ownership behaviour change, so it needs its own allow/deny test at two locations; or (b) leave it open with a dated note saying wave 3 considered it and deferred it, and why. **Record which, in the handback.** Defaulting to (b) is acceptable; defaulting to silence is not.
- [ ] **Step 6 — run:**
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w3/apps/pos
      pnpm test -- src/lib/auth src/stores src/api
      pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint
      ```

      then the **POS device probe**: drive the dev IziPOS build, sign in with a **cashier** PIN and confirm a discount is refused above the terminal ceiling; sign in with an **admin** PIN and confirm the manager override still works; **pull the network** and confirm the offline path uses the last synced payload rather than falling to zero. Capture the result verbatim.

## Task 3-4: `MembershipRole` reconciliation — one vocabulary

**Files**

- Modify: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Company/Domain/Enums/MembershipRole.php`
- Modify: `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php:929` (re-pinned after wave 2a)
- Create: `apps/api/tests/Architecture/MembershipRoleVocabularyTest.php`
- Modify: `dev:docs/glossary.md` (the Membership row)

**The problem, stated precisely.** `MembershipRole` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Company/Domain/Enums/MembershipRole.php`) declares `Owner, Admin, Manager, Accountant, Cashier, Technician, Viewer` — **seven cases, six of which are also Spatie role names**, plus `Owner`, which is **not** a Spatie role at all (it was one of the three names the old `$systemRoles` list protected, and wave 2a deleted that list). Spec §2's Membership row settles the semantics: **membership answers *which company and which locations*; role answers *which actions***. They are **orthogonal**, and having the same seven words mean both is exactly the duplicate-surface problem convention 11 exists to prevent.

**The single authorization read.** `UserController.php:929` — `$isOwner = $membership?->isOwner() ?? false;`, consumed at `:933`. That is the **only** place a `MembershipRole` value decides an authorization outcome; everywhere else it is display or a company-scope label.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — write `MembershipRoleVocabularyTest` first (red).** Static analysis over `app/`: **no production call to `MembershipRole::*`, `isOwner()` or `isAdminRole()` appears in an authorization decision** — defined as: inside a FormRequest `authorize()`, inside a `Gate` / policy method, or as the condition of a branch that returns 403/422.
      **Two lists, and they are not interchangeable** (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M3-3**):
      | Constant | Meaning | Ships with |
      |---|---|---|
      | `ALLOWED_NON_AUTHZ_READS` | Display and company-scope reads. `MembershipRole` is a **label** here and decides nothing. | every non-authz read the census finds |
      | `SOLE_AUTHZ_EXCEPTION` | **At most one** entry, permitted only while Step 2 cannot convert it. Today: `UserController::<the isOwner read>`. | the one entry, or **empty** if Step 2 converts it |
      Rev 1 put `UserController.php:929`'s `isOwner()` into `ALLOWED_NON_AUTHZ_READS`, which is a **mislabel**: the value gates a `403 LOCATION_ESCALATION` at `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php:928-940` — `$isOwner = $membership?->isOwner() ?? false;` consumed as `if (! $isOwner && $callerAllowed !== null && …) { return $this->forbidden('LOCATION_ESCALATION', …); }`. It **is** an authorization read; spec §2's Membership row says so in as many words (*"has exactly one production authz read today … wave 3 either removes that read or documents it as the sole exception"*). Filing it as non-authz would make the census green while the thing it exists to detect is still there.
      **`SOLE_AUTHZ_EXCEPTION` is capped at one entry by the test itself** (`assertLessThanOrEqual(1, count(self::SOLE_AUTHZ_EXCEPTION))`), so it can never become a second `ALLOWED_NON_AUTHZ_READS`. Both maps are **shrink-only**.
- [ ] **Step 2 — convert `UserController.php:929`.** The predicate becomes a **permission** check. **Which permission is a finding, not a guess**: read the branch at `:933` and record what it actually gates, then either use the existing key that means that, or raise it. **Do not invent a key in this task** — wave 3 declares no new permissions, and a new key here would need a template default, a label in three locales and a sync deploy, which is a wave-1-shaped change landing inside a sweep.
      **What the branch actually gates, read from the tree:** `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php:928-940`. `$isOwner` is a **bypass** of the `LOCATION_ESCALATION` refusal — an owner may grant a user locations outside the *caller's own* `allowed_location_ids`; a non-owner may not. So the capability is *"grant location access beyond my own scope"*, and the existing key that means it is **`users.manage_location_access`** (the gate `CreateUserRequest.php:27-29` and `UpdateUserRequest.php:27-29` already apply for the same operation). **Verify that reading before converting**: if `users.manage_location_access` is held by the same principals `isOwner()` admits today, the conversion is a **rename**, not a widening; if it is held by more, that is a **finding** — record the before/after sets and stop.
      > **If the branch turns out to gate something no existing key expresses**, the bounded action is: leave `isOwner()` in place, record it as the **single** `SOLE_AUTHZ_EXCEPTION` entry with the reason *"gates X; no permission key expresses X; needs a declaration"*, update spec §2's Membership row to say the exception was documented rather than removed, and file it as a residual for a follow-up lane. That is honest; converting it to the nearest key is a silent widening, and filing it under `ALLOWED_NON_AUTHZ_READS` is a mislabel.
- [ ] **Step 3 — document the vocabulary in the enum itself.** A class docblock stating: *"`MembershipRole` is a COMPANY-SCOPE label, not an authorization role. It answers 'which company, in what capacity' for display and for scope; it never answers 'may this principal do X' — that is a Spatie role and a permission. `Owner` has no Spatie counterpart and exists only here."* Plus the glossary row.
- [ ] **Step 4 — reconcile `SystemRoleName` with `SystemRoleTemplate`** in the same commit — wave 1's residual **R-w1-4**. Wave 1 shipped both with a test asserting their `GeneralManager` values are equal so they could not drift; wave 3 deletes one. **Keep `SystemRoleTemplate`** (it is the registry's, has all eight cases, and is what `roles.template_key` is typed against) and delete `SystemRoleName`, re-pointing its callers. Delete the equality test in the same commit — a test asserting two enums agree is meaningless once there is one.
- [ ] **Step 5 — run** the vocabulary test plus the Identity and Company feature suites, then **commit.** Exact add list:
      ```
      git add apps/api/app/Modules/Company/Domain/Enums/MembershipRole.php \
              apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/MembershipRoleVocabularyTest.php \
              docs/glossary.md
      ```
      **Plus `SystemRoleName`'s own file and every caller of it, ENUMERATED AT THE COMMIT from one command** — this plan cannot name them because `SystemRoleName` is `lane/w-lot-a-1a`'s and its call sites move with that lane (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 3**: a `<…>` token is not an add list, and neither is a promise):
      ```
      # Run this, paste its output into the add list, and paste the SAME output into
      # the commit body so a reviewer can re-derive it:
      git -C /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w3 grep -l 'SystemRoleName' -- 'apps/api/**/*.php'
      ```
      **Expected at plan-writing time: the enum itself, `SystemRoleTemplate`'s equality test, and the lane's own callers.** If the command returns more than a handful, that is a finding for the handback — a two-vocabulary enum with a wide call surface is a bigger change than a sweep commit, and the bounded action is to split it into its own commit with the inventory reviewer.
      **The `SystemRoleName` deletion and its equality test's removal are in this same commit** — a test asserting two enums agree is meaningless once there is one, and leaving it staged separately makes the lane red between the two.
      Subject: **`Phase 0.6.39: Make MembershipRole a company-scope label only and collapse the two system-role enums into one`**, both trailers.

## Task 3-5: the glossary — one row per noun in spec §2

**Files:** `dev:docs/glossary.md`

**Convention 11's requirement** (`docs/conventions/11-ONE-SURFACE-PER-CONCEPT.md`): every noun is in the glossary with **one table, one primary write path, one operator surface**, and **synonyms are declared there, never discovered**.

**The nouns are spec §2's, and §2's rows are ADOPTED, not paraphrased.** Rev 1 reproduced a simplified table beside the accepted one, and six of its eighteen rows disagreed with it (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M3-2**):

| Row | Rev 1 said | Spec §2 says |
|---|---|---|
| **API token** | *"the token dialog"* | Settings → Service accounts → Tokens **and** *"a human's own tokens under Profile → API tokens"* — rev 1 dropped the human surface entirely |
| **Permission** | operator surface *"none"* | **Settings → Roles** (the matrix, grouped by module with en/fr/ar labels) **and** Settings → Users → *Effective permissions*. §2 states the distinction explicitly: convention 11 asks for one operator **surface**, not one operator **write** surface |
| **Permission verb** | *"none"* | **Settings → Roles** — the matrix's column header is the verb's translated action label (`permissions.actions.<action>`) |
| **Permission manifest** | *"none"* | **code review**, with `permissions:sync --dry-run` as its operator-visible projection |
| **Permission registry** | *"none"* | **`GET /api/v1/permissions` → Settings → Roles**, which is the registry rendered |
| **Grant** | one write path, one surface | **two** write surfaces (Settings → Roles for role↔permission, Settings → Users → Roles for principal↔role) and a **read** surface (Effective permissions, whose `granted_by` answers *"where did this grant come from"*) |
| **Membership** | *"Settings → Users → company access"* | **Settings → Users** |
| **General manager** | *"Settings → Roles"* | **Settings → Users** — *"the lane's sole assignment surface"*, quoting `lane/w-lot-a-1a:docs/glossary.md:91`; §2 records that rev 1 of the **spec** made this same error and rev 2 corrected it |

**This plan therefore ships the rows by COPY plus a MECHANICAL EQUIVALENCE ASSERTION, not by re-typing.** Step 2 transcribes spec §2's eighteen rows into `docs/glossary.md`'s existing format, and `GlossaryParityTest` (new, `apps/api/tests/Architecture/GlossaryParityTest.php`) parses **both** tables — §2's in the spec and the *Identity and authorization* section in `docs/glossary.md` — and asserts, per noun, that the **table**, the **primary write path** and the **canonical surface** cells match after whitespace normalisation. A glossary that drifts from the spec then fails a test rather than a reading. **A row that genuinely must differ is a spec amendment**, raised with the orchestrator, not a quiet edit on one side.

**The eighteen nouns**, so a reader knows the expected count without opening the spec: Principal, User, Service account, API token, Token scope, Role, System role, Custom role, Permission, Permission verb, Permission manifest, Permission registry, Grant, Membership, Effective permissions, Super admin, Support approver, General manager.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — read the glossary as it stands.** `lane/w-lot-a-1a` already added the **General manager** row and a sole-writer sentence (spec §8's rev-2 re-scope moved that from wave 0a to 0b for exactly this reason), and `lane/t2-receipt-spine` also edits the file. **Adopt the existing General-manager row verbatim** — spec §2 says it is the canonical one — and do not restate it.
- [ ] **Step 2 — transcribe spec §2's rows verbatim** into the file's existing format, each with its table, write path and surface **exactly as §2 states them** — including the human *Profile → API tokens* surface, the two Grant write surfaces, and General manager's **Settings → Users**. Then write `GlossaryParityTest` and watch it go green on the transcription and red on a deliberate one-word edit, so the parity is proven rather than asserted. **Declare the synonyms explicitly**: *"role" is not "membership role"*; *"permission" is not "module key"* (the FE's `ModuleKey` is grouping data, not authorization — Task 3-8); *"admin" the Spatie role is not "admin" the `MembershipRole` case is not "super admin" the platform operator*. These three collisions are the ones that actually cause defects, and a glossary that lists nouns without listing them adds no safety.
- [ ] **Step 3 — commit.** Exact add list:
      ```
      git add docs/glossary.md apps/api/tests/Architecture/GlossaryParityTest.php
      ```
      Subject: **`Phase 0.6.40: Give every roles-and-permissions noun a glossary row matching spec §2, and assert the parity mechanically`**, both trailers. **`docs/glossary.md` is the only doc path in the add list**, so a conflict with another lane's glossary edit stays a one-file conflict.

## Task 3-6: empty the exception lists

**Files:** `apps/api/tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-literal-baseline.json` (wave 1's actual path — rev 2 of this plan wrote `tests/PHPStan/baselines/*`, which wave 1 does not use; plan gate r2, **minor 2**), `apps/api/phpstan.neon` (**read only** — the rule and its arguments are registered there by wave 1 and this wave does not edit it), `dev:apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php`, `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/LotActionPermissionDelta.php`, `apps/api/config/lot_action_permissions.php`, `apps/api/app/Console/Commands/ExportPermissionsFrontendMap.php`, and the two new prune commands.

**Four lists, four dispositions.**

**Steps**

> **Step 1 is EXECUTED LAST in this task and in the lane** (Global Constraints' allocation table), because its commit count is not known until its by-file list is generated and a variable-length block interleaved with fixed ones would scatter the ordinals. Read it first — its Phase 0 measurement gates whether the step fits this lane at all — and run it after Step 4.

- [ ] **Step 1 — empty `ForbidPermissionStringLiteral`'s backend baseline, ENUMERATED BY FILE.** Wave 1's residual **R-w1-3** is 608 route middleware strings and 309 call sites. Tasks 3-1 and 3-2 convert the middleware strings **on the 176 routes they touch** and no others; hundreds of pre-existing middleware literals and every call site outside those routes are still there. Rev 1 said *"this step converts whatever call sites remain"* and named no file, no test and no commit for them — which is not executable and not reviewable (plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B3-2**).

      **The list is generated, not guessed.** Wave 1 ships the baseline as a sorted **list** of `<file>:<line>:<key>` sites relative to `projectRoot`, written by `apps/api/tools/generate-permission-baselines.php` (`…-wave-1.md` rev 6, Task 1.16). **This is the exact command that produces the by-file work list:**
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w3/apps/api
      php -r '$sites = json_decode(file_get_contents("tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-literal-baseline.json"), true);
        $by = [];
        foreach ($sites as $s) { $by[explode(":", $s)[0]][] = $s; }
        ksort($by);
        foreach ($by as $file => $rows) { printf("%4d  %s\n", count($rows), $file); }
        printf("%4d  TOTAL across %d files\n", count($sites), count($by));' \
        | tee "$SCRATCH/w3-literal-sites-by-file.txt"
      ```
      **`SCRATCH` is exported in Phase 0** and is this session's scratchpad directory; rev 2 used it here and defined it nowhere, so the command as printed wrote to `/w3-literal-sites-by-file.txt` (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 1**). If the variable is unset when this runs, `tee` fails loudly rather than writing to the filesystem root — but set it in Phase 0 rather than relying on that.
      **Run it in Phase 0 and again at the start of this task**, and paste both outputs into the handback: the difference is exactly what Tasks 3-1 and 3-2 absorbed, and it is the first honest measure of how much work this step is. **The task is scoped by that file list**, module by module, in the same cluster order Task 3-1 used.

      **Per owning module, in that order:**
      1. Convert every site the list names in that module to `PermissionEnum::Case->value` (route strings) or `->can(PermissionEnum::Case->value)` (call sites). **A site whose key is not in `PermissionRegistry` is a FINDING, not a conversion** — wave 1's rule already reports it as *"unknown permission literal … this gate denies every caller including admin"* — so it is raised, not renamed.
      2. **One test per module**: `tests/Feature/<Module>/<Module>PermissionLiteralConversionTest.php`, asserting for each converted route an **allow and a deny** (the same standard as the sweep's recipe), because a literal-to-enum change that mistypes the key is a silent 403 for everyone.
      3. **One commit per module**, subject `Phase 0.6.<n>: Replace <Module>'s permission-string literals with registry enum cases`, add list naming each file the list named plus that module's test plus the regenerated baseline.
      4. **Regenerate the baseline after each module** (never hand-edit it) and confirm the count fell by exactly that module's contribution:
         ```
         ./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse --level=8 --error-format=json app \
           | php tools/generate-permission-baselines.php permission.literal \
                 tests/Architecture/baselines/permission-literal-baseline.json
         ```
      **When the list is empty, delete the baseline file** and switch the rule to hard-fail, in its own commit. **If any site cannot be converted**, it stays with a stated reason in an explicit in-rule allow-list — not as a baseline entry, because a baseline says *"this is debt"* and an allow-list says *"this is intended"*, and mixing them is how debt becomes permanent. **Every allow-list entry is named in the handback**, and a step that ends with a non-empty baseline is a step that ends with a residual (**R-w3-9**), not a step that quietly deletes the file.
      > **This step may be larger than the sweep it follows.** If the by-file list is materially bigger than the lane can absorb, the bounded action is to **split it out as its own lane** — the ratchet is shrink-only either way, so a partial conversion loses nothing — and record which modules were done. **Do not** delete a non-empty baseline to make the step finish.
- [ ] **Step 2 — retire `LotActionPermissionDelta` as DEAD CODE.** Wave 1 established that sync **never called it** (spec §4.3.0a) — so retirement is a **deletion with no re-pointing and no behavioural change**, once every tenant carries the marker. **Verify the marker fleet-wide before deleting:**
      ```
      # per tenant, on staging and then production:
      psql … -d tenant<uuid> -c "SELECT count(*) FROM roles WHERE provisioning_source = 'w-lot-a-1a';"
      # expect >= 1 on every tenant
      ```
      **If any tenant lacks the marker, DEFER this step** and record it — deleting the delta on a fleet where a tenant never received it removes the only code that could have applied it. Delete the class, its config (`apps/api/config/lot_action_permissions.php`) and the `LOT_ACTION_PERMISSIONS_ENFORCE` flag together, or none of them: a flag whose consumer is gone is a trap for the next operator who sets it.
- [ ] **Step 3 — delete the `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` static shims and point the exporter at the registry directly.** Wave 1 kept two `public static` shims (`permissionNames()`, `rolePermissionGrants()`) as the **one sanctioned `app()`-free exception**, because two contracts named them: the frontend-map exporter and `lane/w-lot-a-1a`'s staging protocol. **Both contracts are now discharged** — the lane's protocol completed before wave 1 opened, and the exporter is re-pointed here. Delete both shims, re-point `permissions:export-frontend-map` at `PermissionRegistry`, and **prove determinism survives**:
      ```
      cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w3/apps/api
      php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map && php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map
      git diff --stat -- ../web/src/hooks/permissionsMap.generated.ts     # expect: no change on the second run
      ```
      W-LOT's determinism gate asserts byte identity across two generations **and** against the committed artifact; **that gate must keep passing.**
      **Check the seeder is still referenced by nothing that matters:** `grep -rn "RolesAndPermissionsSeeder" apps/api --include='*.php'`. Wave 1's shim `run()` kept the lane's marked-tenant preservation branch; **that branch stays** unless the lane owner agrees to retire `RolesAndPermissionsSeederMarkedTenantTest`, which is that lane's evidence, not this one's. **Do not delete another lane's mandatory test to make your own cleanup simpler.**
- [ ] **Step 4 — `permissions:prune-orphans --confirm`.** Wave 1's step 3 **reports** orphans and never deletes; this is the deliberate operator action (residual **R-w1-6**). Write it as a **LOCKED** writer: inside a transaction, `PermissionWriteLock::acquire($tenantId)` as the first statement, `FOR UPDATE` over the affected rows in `roles.name` ASC then `roles.id` ASC order, and add it to `PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest`'s `LOCKED` list. **Without `--confirm` it reports and exits 0, writing nothing**; with it, it deletes only `permissions` rows absent from `PermissionRegistry::keys()` **and held by no role**, and refuses (exit 2, `reason=orphan_is_granted`) on any row a role still holds, naming the role. `permissions:prune-orphans-fleet` uses wave 0b's `TenantFleetRunner` verbatim.
      **It is never a deploy step** — `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` will fail the build if it appears in a wave's deploy row, and that is intended.
- [ ] **Step 5 — `PruneOrphanPermissionsTest`**: run twice → the second run **deletes nothing**, asserted by row count (convention-09's re-run axis); a granted orphan is refused with the role named; `--confirm` absent writes nothing, asserted by a query-log count.
- [ ] **Step 6 — run** `./vendor/bin/phpstan analyse --level=8 app` and **commit each step separately**, because each is independently revertable and step 2 has a fleet precondition the others do not:
      | Step | Ordinal | Subject | Add list |
      |---|---|---|---|
      | 2 | `Phase 0.6.41` | *Delete the dead LotActionPermissionDelta with its config and its env flag* | `apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Application/Services/LotActionPermissionDelta.php` (deleted), `apps/api/config/lot_action_permissions.php` (deleted), `apps/api/.env.example`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest.php` |
      | 3 | `Phase 0.6.42` | *Delete the RolesAndPermissionsSeeder statics and point the exporter at the registry* | `apps/api/database/seeders/RolesAndPermissionsSeeder.php`, `apps/api/app/Console/Commands/ExportPermissionsFrontendMap.php`, `apps/web/src/hooks/permissionsMap.generated.ts` |
      | 4 | `Phase 0.6.43` | *Add permissions:prune-orphans as a locked, confirm-gated operator command* | `apps/api/app/Console/Commands/PruneOrphanPermissions.php`, `…/PruneOrphanPermissionsFleet.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/PruneOrphanPermissionsTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/PermissionWriteLockCoverageTest.php` |
      | 1 | `Phase 0.6.48` onward | *Replace `<Module>`'s permission-string literals with registry enum cases*, one commit per module, then a final commit deleting the emptied baseline | per module, the files the by-file list named plus that module's conversion test plus the regenerated baseline |
      **Step 1 runs LAST** (see the Global Constraints' allocation table), so its variable-length block takes a contiguous tail of ordinals from **`0.6.48`**. Expect `[OK] No errors` **and no permission-literal baseline file** only after that tail completes; until then the baseline shrinks module by module and the rule stays baselined.

- Modify: `docs/superpowers/tickets/2026-09-10-marketplace-admin-platform-scope.md`
- Modify: `apps/api/app/Modules/Marketplace/Presentation/routes.php`

      - `'services'`, `'purchases'`, `'dashboard'`, `'sales'` and their kin → **`moduleKey`**;

Make them **explicit `MODULE_PERMISSIONS` entries** so `hasModule()` resolves them by declaration rather than by accident, and they become `moduleKey` values.
      **`MODULE_PERMISSIONS` stays fail-closed** (`dev:apps/web/src/hooks/usePermissions.ts:231-235`): a key outside it is a bug, not a permit. The rename must not turn a fail-closed lookup into an optional one.
      **Update the docblock at `dev:101`**, which today says *"`permission` — key into `MODULE_PERMISSIONS` (role gating)"* — after this change the sentence is true of `moduleKey` and the word "role" is wrong in both cases (it is permission gating, and has been since wave 2a).
- [ ] **Step 2 — `marketplace.admin`, with the exact middleware migration and both proofs.** It gates seller administration **inside the tenant API** yet is a **platform-scoped** capability — and under `admin = activeKeys()` **every tenant admin now holds it** (wave 1's residual **R-w1-7**). It is unreachable today: the module sits behind a kill-switch defaulting to `false` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Marketplace/Presentation/routes.php:49`).
      **Verify the kill-switch default is still `false`**, then perform the migration. Rev 1 said *"re-gate those routes on the central `super_admin` guard"* and named neither the current stack nor the target one nor a test (plan gate r1, **MAJOR M3-4**).
      **The four routes, and what they carry today** (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Marketplace/Presentation/routes.php:87-105`) — the group is
      ```php
      Route::prefix('api/v1/admin/marketplace')
          ->middleware(['api', 'auth:sanctum', SetPermissionsTeam::class, EnforceTokenTenantClaim::class])
      ```
      with `->middleware('can:marketplace.admin')` per route: `GET sellers`, `POST sellers`, `PATCH sellers/{id}`, `POST sellers/{id}/suspend`.
      **The target stack is the central admin pipeline the repository already uses** (`dev:apps/api/routes/api.php:56-58`):
      ```php
      Route::prefix('admin/marketplace')
          ->middleware(['auth:sanctum-admin', 'super_admin', 'throttle:admin-sensitive'])
      ```
      **Three deletions and one addition, each named:**
      1. `auth:sanctum` → **`auth:sanctum-admin`** — a different guard over a different model (`dev:apps/api/config/auth.php:50-53`);
      2. **`SetPermissionsTeam::class` is removed** — it sets the Spatie permissions team from the tenant, and a central `SuperAdmin` has no tenant;
      3. **`EnforceTokenTenantClaim::class` is removed** — a central token carries no `tenant:` ability, so leaving it would 401 every central caller;
      4. **`can:marketplace.admin` is removed from each of the four routes** and replaced by the group's **`super_admin`** middleware; **this task then DEPRECATES the key itself, in its own commit**, because Task 3-7's authoritative 22-key list does **not** contain `marketplace.admin` and Task 3-7 runs **first** (`Phase 0.6.44`, before this task's `Phase 0.6.46`). Rev 2 said the key *"joins Task 3-7's deprecation set"*, which is impossible in both directions: the set is fixed at twenty-two and the task that would have absorbed it has already committed (plan gate r2, **minor 3**). **The edit is in the Marketplace manifest, in `Phase 0.6.46`'s own add list**: mark the `marketplace.admin` definition deprecated exactly as wave 1's deprecation flag does it, and **do not** touch Task 3-7's list or its count. Its *removal* — as distinct from its deprecation — is a later release's, on the same one-release rule Q-w3-5 states, and is recorded as residual **R-w3-12**.
      **`EnforceTokenScope` (wave 2b) is unaffected**: its first statement is the subject-type no-op, so a central `SuperAdmin` passes through it untouched — which `EnforceTokenScopeNoOpTest` already asserts, and which is why moving these routes to the central guard cannot re-open EC-33.
      **Two backend proofs, both required** — `MarketplaceAdminScopeTest`:
      - a central **super admin** authenticated on `auth:sanctum-admin` reaches all four routes and the write **lands its row**;
      - a **tenant admin** holding every key in `PermissionRegistry::activeKeys()` is **401** on all four (no central guard), asserted alongside the row being **unchanged** — the whole point is that `marketplace.admin` no longer admits anybody.
      **If the module has since been enabled for any tenant**, **do not** re-gate in this sweep — that is a live behaviour change needing its own lane; record it, leave the ticket open with the new fact, and skip **only** this step (the sidebar rename in Step 1 is independent).
      > Inventing a `platformScoped` flag for one dormant key would be worse than the problem — which is why wave 1 declined to, and why this is a route change plus a deprecation rather than a registry feature.

A sidebar refactor that typechecks and renders the wrong tree for a cashier is the exact defect this probe exists to catch.

## Task 3-9: the per-role regression matrix

**Files:** `apps/api/tests/Feature/Authorization/RolePermissionMatrixTest.php`, `apps/api/tests/Feature/Authorization/matrix/README.md`

**Why it is last, and why it is the wave's real deliverable.** Today's `dev:apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/RBACTest.php` acts as `admin` throughout: it proves an **allow** path and nothing else. After a sweep that moved 176 authorization checks between layers, the question a reviewer cannot answer by reading is *"does each seeded role still deny exactly what it denied before?"* — and only a matrix answers it.

**Steps**

**The registry decides the EXPECTATION. It cannot manufacture the REQUEST.** `PermissionRegistry::templateGrants()` maps a role to the keys it holds, so for a given (role, route) pair it says *"expect 2xx"* or *"expect 403"*. It does **not** know that `PATCH /work-orders/{id}` needs a work order in a particular state, that `{id}` must be a real UUID, or what a body that would have succeeded looks like. Without those, a "deny" cell cannot be distinguished from a 404 on a fabricated id, a 422 from validation, or a domain refusal — and an 8 × ~800 grid of indistinguishable failures proves nothing (plan gate r1, **BLOCKER B3-3**). Rev 1 promised the grid and supplied no request source.

**The fixture registry, `apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/route-action-fixtures.json`.** One entry per gated route, giving the matrix everything the registry cannot: what to create first, what to put in the URI, what body to send, and **what row proves the action happened**.

> **Rev 3 changes the registry's KEY and adds its missing field** (plan gate r2, **BLOCKERs B2 and B3**).
>
> **B2 — the key.** Rev 2 keyed every entry on a route **name**, and at `dev` only **three of the twenty families** have one. Re-derived against the tree: `roles.*` and `users.*` are named (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/routes.php:59-80`) and so are `vouchers.*` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Voucher/Presentation/routes.php:22-42`) and `replenishment-requests.*` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Replenishment/Presentation/routes.php:14-31`) and `platform.*` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/PlatformIntegration/Presentation/routes.php:20-93`) — while **Coupon, Promotion, Scheduling, Menu, BatchExpiry and Workshop declare NO route names at all** (`grep -c '\->name(' ` over each of those six `routes.php` at `dev` returns **0**). `vouchers.issue` is really `vouchers.issue-goodwill`; `replenishment-requests.approve` does not exist in any spelling; `platform-integrations.update` does not exist and the module's names are `platform.*`. A registry keyed on names is therefore **unbuildable for two-thirds of the API**.
> **The key is `"METHOD uri"`** — the router's own primary identity, which every route has — with `name` kept as an **optional cross-check** that `RouteActionFixtureContractTest` asserts when present. That is also what makes the registry survive wave 3 itself: this sweep does not name routes, and a later lane that does adds a `name` without re-keying anything.
>
> **B3 — the missing field.** Rev 2's schema recorded route, method, URI, setup, payload and mutation evidence — and **not the permission key**, so *"`templateGrants()` decides each route's expectation"* had no route-to-key input. `templateGrants()` maps **role → keys**; to turn that into an expectation you need **route → key**, and a live route exposes one only through `can:` middleware — which is exactly the enforcement style this wave is **moving routes away from**. Every entry now carries **`permission`**, and the contract test proves it against the live router or the generator.

~~~jsonc
// Schema. One object per entry; the file is a JSON array, sorted by `key`.
// Rev 6 adds `expect.kind` and `expect.soft_deletes`, because rev 5's single
// table/column/value shape could not express a DELETE or a pivot COUNT and the
// matrix therefore asserted a falsehood on entries 2, 3, 7 and 13
// (plan gate r5, Wave 3 BLOCKER 2).
{
  "key":      "PATCH api/v1/workshop/work-orders/{id}",   // METHOD + SPACE + the router's uri.
                                                          // THE PRIMARY KEY. Every route has one;
                                                          // two-thirds of them have no name (B2).
  "name":     null,                                       // the route name IF it has one, else null.
                                                          // Cross-checked, never keyed on.
  "permission": "work-orders.update",                     // REQUIRED (B3). The key the route's gate
                                                          // names. Three sources, in this order:
                                                          //   1. the route's `can:` parameter, when
                                                          //      the resolved stack has one;
                                                          //   2. the generator's `permission_keys`
                                                          //      for a CONTROLLER/FORMREQUEST route;
                                                          //   3. the key the conversion GIVES it, for
                                                          //      a route this wave is converting --
                                                          //      written in the same commit as the
                                                          //      conversion, never before it.
  "permission_source": "generator",                       // "middleware" | "generator" | "conversion"
                                                          // -- so the contract test knows WHICH
                                                          // oracle to check this entry against.
  "module":   "Workshop",                                 // the owning reviewer's cluster
  "setup":    "WorkshopFixture::inProgressWorkOrder",     // static factory: (Company, User) -> array
                                                          // returns the values params/payload interpolate
  "params":   { "id": "{work_order_id}" },                // {curly} tokens resolve from setup's return,
                                                          // plus the MATRIX-supplied {template} (below)
  "payload":  { "internal_notes": "matrix probe" },       // a body that WOULD succeed for a holder; {} for none
  "requires": ["http_fake"],                              // OPTIONAL. Environment the ALLOW cell needs
                                                          // before the request. Exactly one entry uses it.
  "expect": {                                             // what proves the action happened, on the ALLOW
                                                          // cell -- and, negated, on the DENY cell
    "kind":   "update",                                   // create | update | delete | count. REQUIRED.
                                                          // It selects the predicate; see the table below.
    "table":  "workshop_work_orders",
    "where":  { "id": "{work_order_id}" },
    "column": "internal_notes",                           // update ONLY (forbidden on create/delete)
    "value":  "matrix probe",                             // update ONLY
    "count":  2,                                          // count ONLY: the EXACT row count AFTER a
                                                          // successful action. The fixture seeds a
                                                          // different one, so before != after.
    "soft_deletes": true                                  // REQUIRED on every entry. Whether `table`
                                                          // carries `deleted_at`. The contract test
                                                          // asserts it against Schema::hasColumn, so a
                                                          // wrong declaration is red, not silent.
  },
  "notes": "row-level ownership is E-3 and is NOT asserted here"
}
~~~

**`expect.kind` is the whole repair, and each kind is a different predicate over the same row** (plan gate r5, Wave 3 **BLOCKER 2**). `expectMatches()` answers exactly one question — *"has the action happened?"* — and the matrix asserts it is **false before** the request and **true after** on the allow cell, **false after** on the deny cell. Rev 5 defined *"no `column` ⇒ `exists()`"*, which makes that question unanswerable for a delete (the row exists **before**, so the universal pre-assertion fails on entries 3 and 13) and inexpressible for a pivot count (entries 2 and 7 were described as *(count)* in the table and as `exists()` in the body).

| `kind` | Before the request | After a successful request | `expectMatches()` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `create` | the row is **absent** | the row is **present** | `query()->exists()` |
| `update` | `column` holds a **different** value | `column` holds `value` | `query()->where(column, value)->exists()` |
| `delete` | the row is **present** | the row is **gone from the live set** | `! query()->exists()` |
| `count` | the count is **something else** | the count is exactly `count` | `query()->count() === count` |

**A soft-deleted row is still a row, and `delete` is the kind that cares.** Four of this registry's tables carry `deleted_at` and two of them are delete targets: `scheduling_appointments` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Scheduling/Domain/Appointment.php:89`) and `roles` (which does **not**). `expectMatches()` therefore appends `whereNull('deleted_at')` **whenever `expect.soft_deletes` is true**, for every kind — so `delete` means *"no longer in the live set"*, and `create`/`update` cannot be satisfied by a tombstone left over from an earlier grid cell.

**`{template}` is supplied by the MATRIX, not by a fixture, and it exists for one reason: `AssignableRole`.** Three entries (4, 7, 8) send a role name, and `AssignableRole::validate()` fails *"a role that grants permissions beyond your own"* (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Rules/AssignableRole.php:50`). A hard-coded role name would therefore make the **allow** cell 422 for every template that does not outrank it — a red cell that says nothing about authorization. The principal's **own** template role always passes the guard by construction, so the payload names `{template}` and the matrix interpolates it alongside the fixture's tokens. The **deny** cell is unaffected: `authorize()` returns false and Laravel 403s **before** `rules()` runs.

**The twenty entries, RE-DERIVED against `dev` so every one resolves, and RE-VALIDATED for rev 6 against the live tree — route, gate, table, column and payload rule** (plan gate r5, Wave 3 **BLOCKER 1**). `setup` values name static methods on `apps/api/tests/Architecture/Support/RouteActionFixtures.php`. **`name` is `null` wherever the route declares none** — that is a fact about the tree, not a gap in the registry. The full JSON follows this table; the table is its index, not its source.

**`apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/route-action-fixtures.json` — THE COMPLETE FILE, not a schema and twenty summary rows** (plan gate r5, Wave 3 **BLOCKER 1**: rev 5 printed one JSONC object and a table and told the implementer to write the twenty entries, so neither the first red nor the supplied matrix class was reproducible from the plan). It is committed verbatim as printed. **It parses**: `python3 -c "import json;d=json.load(open('…/route-action-fixtures.json'));print(len(d))"` → `20`, sorted by `key`, keys unique, every entry carrying the ten required fields, `permission_source` in the closed set, and `column`/`value` present **iff** `kind == "update"`, `count` present **iff** `kind == "count"`.

~~~json
      "soft_deletes": false
    },
    "notes": "UserController::deactivate() gates inline on can('users.update') (dev:.../Controllers/UserController.php:585). UserStatus::Inactive = 'inactive'. The target is a SECOND active admin so the LastAdminFloor refusal cannot green a deny cell for the wrong reason."
~~~

</details>

> **The four `permission_source: "middleware"` entries are the ones whose `can:` already exists at the lane base** — `POST vouchers/issue-goodwill` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Voucher/Presentation/routes.php:25-27`), `POST replenishment-requests` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Replenishment/Presentation/routes.php:17-19`) and wave 2b's two `/service-accounts` routes. The fifteen `"generator"` entries are CONTROLLER or FORMREQUEST routes whose key the Phase-0 enforcement generator already extracts. **Exactly one entry is `"conversion"`** — `POST batches/{uuid}/recall`, which has **no gate of any kind** at `dev`: no `can:` on the route (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/routes.php:29`), no `Gate::`, no `can()` and no FormRequest in `BatchController::recall()` (`dev:…/Controllers/BatchController.php:193-210`). Its entry is committed in the same commit as the conversion that gives it `batches.recall`, never before.
> **Three rev-5 facts were wrong against the tree and are corrected in the JSON above**: `work_orders` is really **`workshop_work_orders`** (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Workshop/WorkOrder/Domain/WorkOrder.php:104`), `appointments` is really **`scheduling_appointments`** (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Scheduling/Domain/Appointment.php:92`), and `IdentityFixture::serviceAccount` wrote a column the model does not have — see the `pos_pin` correction in the factory block below.

**The seven corrections rev 3 made, each against the tree**, so a reader can check them rather than trusting the table. **Two of these rows described rev-3 entries that rev 4 and rev 5 had already moved on from, so the "corrections" table and the registry table gave the implementer two different definitions** (plan gate r5, Wave 3 **MAJOR 1**); both are re-stated below against the JSON above, which is now the single source and which the rows point at rather than paraphrase:

| Rev 2 wrote | Why it could not resolve | Rev 3's entry |
|---|---|---|
| `work-orders.update`, `work-orders.close` | `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Workshop/WorkOrder/Presentation/routes.php` declares **zero** `->name()` calls; and there is **no `close` action** — the transition is `POST work-orders/{id}/complete` (`:49`) | the two `workshop/work-orders` entries, keyed by `METHOD uri`, `name: null` |
| `appointments.store`, `appointments.destroy` | `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Scheduling/Presentation/routes.php` declares **zero** names; the URIs are under the `api/v1/scheduling` prefix (`:51-70`) | the two `scheduling/appointments` entries |
| `menus.update` | `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Menu/Presentation/routes.php` declares **zero** names (`:17`) | `PATCH api/v1/menus/{id}` |
| `batches.recall` | `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Presentation/routes.php` declares **zero** names; the param is `{uuid}`, not `{id}` (`:29`). **`batch_recalls` is not a table** — the recall sets the batch's own flag | `POST api/v1/batches/{uuid}/recall`, `expect` `update` on **`product_batches`** / **`is_recalled`**. Rev 3 wrote *"`batches` / `status`"* and **both halves are wrong**: `Batch::$table = 'product_batches'` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Domain/Entities/Batch.php:19`) and `recall()` writes `is_recalled`/`recall_reason`/`recalled_at`, never a `status` column (`:134-138`) |
| `coupons.store`, `promotions.update` | `dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Coupon/Presentation/routes.php` and `…/Promotion/Presentation/routes.php` declare **zero** names (`:13`, `:15`) | `POST api/v1/coupons` and `PATCH api/v1/promotions/{id}` |
| `vouchers.issue` | the route is `vouchers.issue-goodwill` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Voucher/Presentation/routes.php:25-27`) | `POST api/v1/vouchers/issue-goodwill`, with the real name |
| `replenishment-requests.approve` | **no such route in any spelling.** The module's six named routes are `index`, `store`, `actions.create-transfer`, `actions.create-po`, `actions.reject`, `cancel` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Replenishment/Presentation/routes.php:14-31`) | `POST api/v1/replenishment-requests` is the entry (`replenishment-requests.store`, gated `can:replenishment.create` at `:17-19`). Rev 3's *"approve is expressed as creating the transfer"* pointed at `actions.create-transfer`, which rev 4 replaced with `store` and rev 5 never updated here. **`store` is the right cell**: it is the module's own write with a first-party `can:` already on the route, while `actions.create-transfer` writes a *transfer* in another module and would have made this cell a two-module assertion |
| `platform-integrations.update` | **no such route and no such name prefix.** The module's names are `platform.*` and its write routes are `platform.submit-for-enrichment`, `platform.vin-decode`, `platform.upload-url` (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/PlatformIntegration/Presentation/routes.php:83-93`). `platform_integrations` is **not a table** — submission writes `products.enrichment_status` (`dev:apps/api/database/migrations/tenant/2026_03_28_100000_add_enrichment_columns_to_products_table.php:15`), and the controller's own gate is `can('enrichment.submit')` (`dev:…/Controllers/ProductSubmissionController.php:33`) | `POST api/v1/platform/submit-for-enrichment`, `expect` `update` on `products` / `enrichment_status` |

> **The two `/service-accounts` entries are WAVE 2b's routes, not `dev`'s**, and that is correct rather than an oversight: wave 3's entry condition is *"wave 2 merged into local `dev`, 2a AND 2b"*, so by the time this registry is written those routes exist. **Phase 0 verifies them like any other** — a `service-accounts` route that does not resolve at the lane base means 2b did not merge, which is a **STOP**, not a fixture to drop.
> **Every one of the twenty is re-pinned in Phase 0 against the live router**, and the re-pin is now a **mechanical equality** rather than a judgement: `RouteActionFixtureContractTest` asserts each `key` resolves and, where `name` is non-null, that the router agrees. A key that does not resolve is a finding — waves 0b, 2a and 2b touched these modules, and this plan is written before they land.

**Steps**

- [ ] **Step 1 — build the fixture registry and its two guards.** Rev 2 described all three artefacts and supplied none of them, so the first red test's expected failure was not reproducible from the plan (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 5**). All three are below.

      1. **`route-action-fixtures.json`** — the twenty entries above, in the rev-3 schema, sorted by `key`.

      2. **`apps/api/tests/Architecture/Support/RouteActionFixtures.php`** — **all fifteen factories, in full.** Rev 3 printed one and deferred the rest to the implementer (gate r3, Wave 3 **BLOCKER 3**). **It is fifteen, not twenty:** the twenty registry entries name **fifteen distinct `setup` values** across **ten fixture classes** — `POST roles`, `POST users` and `POST service-accounts` share `tenantWithAdmin`; `PATCH roles/{id}` and `DELETE roles/{id}` share `customRole`; `PATCH users/{id}` and `POST users/{userId}/roles` share `secondHumanUser`; and the two work-order routes share `inProgressWorkOrder`. Saying "twenty factories" was itself a count nobody had taken.
         > **Every factory below is documented by its ROUTE KEY, never by a registry ORDINAL** (plan gate r5 fix round). The JSON is sorted by `key`, so an entry's position moves the moment a route is added or re-spelled; rev 5's *"Entry 12"* comments were already one re-sort away from pointing at the wrong factory. The route key is the registry's primary key and cannot drift.
         ~~~php
         <?php

         declare(strict_types=1);

         namespace Tests\Architecture\Support;

         use App\Modules\BatchExpiry\Domain\Entities\Batch;
         use App\Modules\Company\Domain\Company;
         use App\Modules\Company\Domain\Location;
         use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums\UserStatus;
         use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\User;
         use App\Modules\Menu\Domain\Entities\Menu;
         use App\Modules\Partner\Domain\Partner;
         use App\Modules\Product\Domain\Product;
         use App\Modules\Promotion\Domain\Enums\PromotionStatus;
         use App\Modules\Promotion\Domain\Promotion;
         use App\Modules\Scheduling\Domain\Appointment;
         use App\Modules\Scheduling\Domain\Bay;
         use App\Modules\Scheduling\Domain\Enums\AppointmentStatus;
         use App\Modules\Service\Domain\Service;
         use App\Modules\Workshop\WorkOrder\Domain\Enums\WorkOrderStatus;
         use App\Modules\Workshop\WorkOrder\Domain\WorkOrder;
         use Spatie\Permission\Models\Role;

         /**
          * The `setup` factories route-action-fixtures.json names.
          *
          * CONTRACT, identical for every one of the fifteen:
          *   - signature: static function (Company $company, User $actor): array
          *   - returns a FLAT array<string, string> of tokens that `params` and
          *     `payload` interpolate by {name}. Nothing else. No models, no objects:
          *     an interpolated token must be something that can go in a URI.
          *   - creates ONLY what the route needs to be reachable and mutable. It does
          *     NOT create the acting principal -- the matrix supplies that, holding
          *     exactly one template role, which is the whole point of the grid.
          *   - is IDEMPOTENT per call: two invocations create two independent rows, so
          *     an 8-template x N-route grid cannot collide with itself.
          *   - seeds every `expect.column` to a KNOWN, NON-MATCHING value, so the deny
          *     cell's "unchanged" assertion is falsifiable. A column that was null
          *     before and null after proves nothing about whether the write was refused.
          *
          * This class is a DISPATCHER over ten <Module>Fixture classes, one per the
          * registry's `module` column, so a cluster's reviewer reads one block.
          *
          * EVERY symbol below was checked against `dev` while rev 5 was written. The four
          * that rev 4 invented are named in the "six corrections rev 5 made" table after
          * this block; do not reintroduce them.
          */
         final class RouteActionFixtures
         {
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function make(string $setup, Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 [$class, $method] = explode('::', $setup, 2);
                 /** @var callable(Company, User): array<string, string> $factory */
                 $factory = [__NAMESPACE__.'\\'.$class, $method];

                 return $factory($company, $actor);
             }
         }

         final class IdentityFixture
         {
             /** POST roles, POST users, POST service-accounts -- the route creates the row,
              *  so setup only needs the tenant to exist. */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function tenantWithAdmin(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 return [
                     'company_id' => (string) $company->id,
                     'tenant_id' => (string) $company->tenant_id,
                     // A name/email the route will CREATE with; unique per call so two grid
                     // cells cannot collide on a unique index.
                     'new_role_name' => 'matrix-role-'.bin2hex(random_bytes(4)),
                     'new_email' => 'matrix-'.bin2hex(random_bytes(4)).'@example.test',
                     // POST service-accounts creates a users row whose only falsifiable
                     // discriminator is its NAME, so the token is minted here too.
                     'new_service_name' => 'matrix-sa-'.bin2hex(random_bytes(4)),
                 ];
             }

             /** PATCH roles/{id} and DELETE roles/{id} -- a CUSTOM role, never a system one:
              *  the DELETE cell removes it, and SYSTEM_ROLE_PROTECTED would otherwise refuse. */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function customRole(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 $role = Role::create([
                     'name' => 'matrix-custom-'.bin2hex(random_bytes(4)),
                     'guard_name' => 'sanctum',
                     config('permission.column_names.team_foreign_key') => $company->tenant_id,
                 ]);
                 // PATCH roles/{id} expects kind=count over role_has_permissions; seed EXACTLY
                 // ONE pivot row so before = 1 and the entry's `count: 2` is falsifiable.
                 $role->syncPermissions(['products.view']);

                 return ['id' => (string) $role->id, 'role_name' => $role->name];
             }

             /** PATCH users/{id} and POST users/{userId}/roles -- a second HUMAN user, so neither
              *  the last-admin floor nor the service-target refusal can green a deny cell.
              *  It holds ZERO roles, which is what makes the pivot count 0 -> 1. */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function secondHumanUser(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 $user = User::factory()->create([
                     'tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id,
                     'name' => 'before the matrix probe',   // the non-matching pre-state
                     'status' => UserStatus::Active->value,
                     'principal_kind' => 'human',           // wave 2a's column
                 ]);

                 return ['id' => (string) $user->id, 'userId' => (string) $user->id];
             }

             /**
              * POST users/{id}/deactivate. The target must NOT be the last admin, or the
              * refusal under test would be the FLOOR rather than the permission gate,
              * and every deny cell would be green for the wrong reason.
              */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function secondActiveAdmin(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 $user = User::factory()->create([
                     'tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id,
                     'status' => UserStatus::Active->value,
                     'principal_kind' => 'human',
                 ]);
                 $user->assignRole('admin');

                 return ['id' => (string) $user->id];
             }

             /**
              * POST service-accounts/{id}/tokens -- issuance needs an EXISTING service principal
              * (wave 2b's shape).
              *
              * THE COLUMN IS `pos_pin`, NOT `pos_pin_hash` (plan gate r5, Wave 3 BLOCKER 3).
              * `pos_pin_hash` exists nowhere on the model: the property is
              * `@property string|null $pos_pin` (dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/User.php:40),
              * the fillable entry is 'pos_pin' (:81-97) and the cast is
              * 'pos_pin' => 'hashed' (:115-126). Wave 2a makes that column NULLABLE for a
              * service principal; it does not rename it. Rev 5 wrote the invented name, which
              * User::factory()->create() would have raised on as an unknown attribute.
              */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function serviceAccount(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 $sa = User::factory()->create([
                     'tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id,
                     'status' => UserStatus::Active->value,
                     'principal_kind' => 'service',
                     'password' => null,
                     'pos_pin' => null,
                 ]);

                 return ['id' => (string) $sa->id];
             }
         }

         final class WorkshopFixture
         {
             /**
              * PATCH workshop/work-orders/{id} and POST workshop/work-orders/{id}/complete --
              * one factory, two routes. THE TABLE IS `workshop_work_orders`, not `work_orders`
              * (dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Workshop/WorkOrder/Domain/WorkOrder.php:104), which is
              * what both entries' `expect.table` now says (plan gate r5 fix round).
              *
              * IN_PROGRESS, not "Open": `WorkOrderStatus` has NO `Open` case
              * (dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Workshop/WorkOrder/Domain/Enums/WorkOrderStatus.php:13-23
              * -- Received, Diagnosed, Quoted, Approved, InProgress, Paused, WaitingParts,
              * Completed, Invoiced, Closed, Cancelled), and `InProgress` is the ONLY state whose
              * allowed targets contain `Completed`
              * (dev:…/WorkOrder/Domain/Services/StatusMachine.php:64-67). The factory's own default
              * is `Received` (dev:apps/api/database/factories/Workshop/WorkOrderFactory.php), from
              * which `complete` is not a legal transition -- so the complete route would have
              * failed every cell on the state machine rather than on authorization.
              *
              * `internal_notes`, not `notes`: dev:…/WorkOrder/Domain/WorkOrder.php:53,127.
              * `Received` is also mutable, but `InProgress` serves BOTH entries and
              * `requireMutableWorkOrder` admits it (dev:…/Application/Services/
              * WorkOrderAuthoringService.php:124-140 blocks only Completed/Invoiced/Closed/Cancelled).
              */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function inProgressWorkOrder(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 $order = WorkOrder::factory()->create([
                     'tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id,
                     'company_id' => $company->id,
                     'status' => WorkOrderStatus::InProgress->value,
                     'internal_notes' => 'before the matrix probe',   // so `expect` can prove a CHANGE
                 ]);

                 return ['id' => (string) $order->id, 'work_order_id' => (string) $order->id];
             }
         }

         final class SchedulingFixture
         {
             /** POST scheduling/appointments -- the route creates the appointment, so setup supplies
              *  the location, the bay, the customer AND a real service row: `planned_services` is
              *  ['required','array','min:1'] and each element's `service_ref_id` must be a uuid
              *  (dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Scheduling/Presentation/Requests/StoreAppointmentRequest.php).
              *  The payload's date fields are `scheduled_start`/`scheduled_end`, NOT `starts_at`:
              *  that is the request rule AND the column (dev:.../Domain/Appointment.php fillable). */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function bayAndCustomer(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 $location = Location::factory()->create(['company_id' => $company->id]);
                 $bay = Bay::factory()->create([
                     'tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id,
                     'company_id' => $company->id,
                     'location_id' => $location->id,
                 ]);
                 $customer = Partner::factory()->create([
                     'tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id,
                     'company_id' => $company->id,
                 ]);

                 // planned_services[].service_ref_id must be a REAL uuid the request accepts.
                 $service = Service::factory()->create([
                     'tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id,
                     'company_id' => $company->id,
                 ]);

                 $start = now()->addDay()->startOfHour();

                 return [
                     'bay_id' => (string) $bay->id,
                     'location_id' => (string) $location->id,
                     'partner_id' => (string) $customer->id,
                     'service_id' => (string) $service->id,
                     // Fixed OFFSETS, never literal dates, or the fixture rots the first time
                     // the schedule window moves. `scheduled_end` must be AFTER `scheduled_start`
                     // ('after:scheduled_start') and the pair is what the entry's `where` keys on.
                     'scheduled_start' => $start->toIso8601String(),
                     'scheduled_end' => $start->copy()->addMinutes(30)->toIso8601String(),
                 ];
             }

             /** DELETE scheduling/appointments/{id} -- the row must already exist and be cancellable.
              *  `scheduling_appointments` SOFT-DELETES (dev:.../Domain/Appointment.php:89,92), so the
              *  entry declares soft_deletes: true and the delete predicate is
              *  "gone from the live set", not "gone from the table". */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function bookedAppointment(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 $seed = self::bayAndCustomer($company, $actor);
                 $appointment = Appointment::factory()->create([
                     'tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id,
                     'company_id' => $company->id,
                     'location_id' => $seed['location_id'],
                     'bay_id' => $seed['bay_id'],
                     'status' => AppointmentStatus::Scheduled->value,
                 ]);

                 return $seed + ['id' => (string) $appointment->id];
             }
         }

              * rev 4 called `app(MenuService::class)->create(companyId:, name:)` and the class is

              * (git ls-files over apps/api/app/Modules/Menu returns exactly one

              * The module's PRIMARY WRITE PATH is `Menu::create([...])` inside

             /** @return array<string, string> */

             {
                 $menu = Menu::create([
                     'tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id,
                     'company_id' => $company->id,

                     'is_active' => true,
                 ]);

                 return ['id' => (string) $menu->id];
             }
         }

         final class BatchExpiryFixture
         {
             /**
              * POST batches/{uuid}/recall -- recall sets the BATCH's own flags. There is NO `batches` table and NO
              * `status` column: the model is `product_batches` and the recall writes
              * `is_recalled` / `recall_reason` / `recalled_at`
              * (dev:apps/api/app/Modules/BatchExpiry/Domain/Entities/Batch.php:21,31-45 and its
              * `recall(string $reason)`). Rev 4 wrote `'status' => 'active'` into a column that
              * does not exist, so every cell would have failed on the insert (gate r4, Wave 3
              * BLOCKER 2). The route parameter is `{uuid}`, not `{id}`.
              */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function activeBatch(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 $product = Product::factory()->create(['tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id]);
                 $batch = Batch::factory()->create([
                     'tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id,
                     'company_id' => $company->id,
                     'product_id' => $product->id,
                     'is_active' => true,
                     'is_recalled' => false,          // the non-matching pre-state for `is_recalled`
                 ]);

                 return ['uuid' => (string) $batch->uuid, 'product_id' => (string) $product->id];
             }
         }

         final class CouponFixture
         {
             /** POST coupons. No CouponFactory and no coupon creation service -- POST creates the row. */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function campaignReady(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 return [
                     'company_id' => (string) $company->id,
                     // POST creates the coupon; the token is the code it will be created WITH.
                     'new_code' => 'MATRIX'.strtoupper(bin2hex(random_bytes(3))),
                 ];
         ~~~
