         final class PromotionFixture
         {
             /**
              * PATCH promotions/{id}. No PromotionFactory, and NO `PromotionService` -- rev 4 named one and the
              * module's services are `CartPromotionService`, `PromotionManagementService` and
              * `PromotionEvaluationService`, none of which creates a promotion
              * (`PromotionManagementService` exposes activate/pause/archive/recordUsage only:
              * dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Promotion/Application/Services/PromotionManagementService.php:15,29,43,57).
              * The primary write path is `Promotion::create([...])` in `PromotionController::store()`
              * (dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Promotion/Presentation/Controllers/PromotionController.php:83-95),
              * and that is what this reproduces -- including the five NOT NULL columns the
              * migration requires (dev:apps/api/database/migrations/tenant/
              * 2026_03_02_200000_create_promotions_table.php: name, type, discount_type,
              * discount_value, applies_to).
              */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function activePromotion(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 $promotion = Promotion::create([
                     'tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id,
                     'company_id' => $company->id,
                     'name' => 'before the matrix probe',   // the non-matching pre-state for `name`
                     'type' => 'cart_discount',
                     'status' => PromotionStatus::Draft,
                     'discount_type' => 'percentage',
                     'discount_value' => '10.0000',
                     'applies_to' => 'cart',
                 ]);

                 return ['id' => (string) $promotion->id];
             }
         }

         final class VoucherFixture
         {
             /** POST vouchers/issue-goodwill -- issuance needs a customer, not an existing voucher.
              *  It also returns a NOTE TOKEN, because the voucher CODE is generated server-side and
              *  is therefore unusable as the entry's `where` clause. */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function customerWithBalance(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 $customer = Partner::factory()->create([
                     'tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id,
                     'company_id' => $company->id,
                 ]);

                 return [
                     'partner_id' => (string) $customer->id,
                     'company_id' => (string) $company->id,
                     // IssueGoodwillRequest validates currency as size:3, and the issued row
                     // must carry the COMPANY's currency or the ledger is cross-currency.
                     'currency' => (string) $company->currency,
                     // The falsifiable discriminator: `vouchers.code` is generated server-side,
                     // so `notes` is the only field the payload controls and `expect.where` can
                     // key on. Unique per call so two grid cells cannot collide.
                     'new_note' => 'matrix probe '.bin2hex(random_bytes(4)),
                 ];
             }
         }

         final class ReplenishmentFixture
         {
             /**
              * POST replenishment-requests. No ReplenishmentFactory and NO `ReplenishmentRequestService` -- rev 4
              * named one; the module's three services are `ReplenishmentCaptureService` (whose
              * only public method is `capture(CaptureRequestData $data)`), `ReplenishmentQueryService`
              * and `ReplenishmentFulfillmentService` (gate r4, Wave 3 BLOCKER 2).
              *
              * The ENTRY ALSO MOVED, and for a reason rev 4's route did not survive: rev 4 used
              * `POST /replenishment-requests/actions/create-transfer`, which needs a stocked source
              * location and writes TRANSFERS, not `replenishment_requests.status`
              * (dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Replenishment/Presentation/Controllers/
              * ReplenishmentActionController.php:27-55). The entry is now
              * `POST api/v1/replenishment-requests` (`replenishment-requests.store`,
              * `can:replenishment.create`, dev:…/Presentation/routes.php:17-19), whose row proof is
              * the created `replenishment_requests` row itself. Setup supplies only what the
              * request body must reference.
              */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function locationAndProduct(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 $location = Location::factory()->create(['company_id' => $company->id]);
                 $product = Product::factory()->create(['tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id]);

                 return [
                     'location_id' => (string) $location->id,
                     'product_id' => (string) $product->id,
                     'new_note' => 'matrix probe',
                 ];
             }
         }

         final class PlatformIntegrationFixture
         {
             /**
              * POST platform/submit-for-enrichment -- submission writes products.enrichment_status
              * (dev:apps/api/database/migrations/tenant/2026_03_28_100000_add_enrichment_columns_to_products_table.php:15).
              * `platform_integrations` is NOT a table.
              *
              * The column is NULLABLE with no default, so the non-matching pre-state is set
              * EXPLICITLY rather than relied on.
              */
             /** @return array<string, string> */
             public static function productReadyToSubmit(Company $company, User $actor): array
             {
                 $product = Product::factory()->create([
                     'tenant_id' => $company->tenant_id,
                     'enrichment_status' => 'none',     // the non-matching pre-state
                 ]);

                 return ['id' => (string) $product->id, 'product_id' => (string) $product->id];
             }
         }
         ~~~
         > **There is now NO `app(...)` in any factory, because there is no service for any of them to resolve** (gate r4, Wave 3 **BLOCKER 2** and **MINOR 1**). Rev 4 resolved `MenuService`, `PromotionService` and `ReplenishmentRequestService`; **none of the three exists on `dev`**, and its own note called that "four modules" while printing three calls. The three modules with no model factory now build through the **same model call their own controller makes** — `Menu::create()` (`dev:…/Menu/Presentation/Controllers/MenuController.php:73-94`), `Promotion::create()` (`dev:…/Promotion/Presentation/Controllers/PromotionController.php:83-95`) and, for Replenishment, the route's **own** `POST` (the entry moved; see `ReplenishmentFixture`). That is not a raw insert bypassing the domain: it is the module's **primary write path**, which is exactly what convention 11 says a fixture should reproduce.
         > **The model-factory census is unchanged and was re-verified**: `git ls-tree -r dev --name-only | grep 'database/factories/'` lists factories for Workshop (`Workshop/WorkOrderFactory`), Scheduling (`Scheduling/AppointmentFactory`, `Scheduling/BayFactory`), BatchExpiry (`BatchExpiry/BatchFactory`), Voucher, Product, User, Company, Location, Partner and **`ServiceFactory`** — which `SchedulingFixture::bayAndCustomer` now consumes, because `planned_services[].service_ref_id` must be a real uuid — and **none** for Menu, Coupon, Promotion or Replenishment. **Note the two subdirectories** — `Workshop/`, `Scheduling/` and `BatchExpiry/` — which rev 4's flat `'apps/api/database/factories/*'` glob would not have matched.
         > **Phase 0 re-reads each factory's `definition()` before the first grid run** and fills in whatever required column the factory does not default — `git ls-files 'apps/api/database/factories/*'`, then `grep -n "'.*' =>" <factory>`. A fixture that fails on a NOT NULL column fails **every** cell of its row identically, which is the cheapest possible failure to diagnose and the reason this is a Phase-0 step rather than a debugging session.
         > **No signature above is a "best reading"; every one was read off `dev` while rev 5 was written.** The six corrections rev 5 made, each checkable:
         >
         | Rev 4 wrote | Why it could not run | Rev 5's factory |
         |---|---|---|
         | `WorkOrderStatus::Open` | the enum has **no `Open` case** (`dev:…/WorkOrder/Domain/Enums/WorkOrderStatus.php:13-23`); and `Completed` is reachable **only from `InProgress`** (`dev:…/Domain/Services/StatusMachine.php:64-67`), so the complete route would have failed on the state machine | `WorkOrderStatus::InProgress`, factory renamed `inProgressWorkOrder` |
         | `'notes'` on a work order | the column is **`internal_notes`** (`dev:…/Domain/WorkOrder.php:53,127`) and it is the only free-text field `update()` carries | `internal_notes`, in the factory, the payload and `expect.column` |
         | `Batch` `'status' => 'active'` | `product_batches` has **no `status` column** — it has `is_active` / `is_expired` / `is_recalled` (`dev:…/BatchExpiry/Domain/Entities/Batch.php:31-45`), and `recall()` writes `is_recalled` | `is_recalled`, `expect.table` corrected from `batches` to **`product_batches`** |
         | `app(MenuService::class)->create(...)` | **no such class**; the module's one service is `MenuResolutionService` | `Menu::create([...])`, the controller's own write |
         | `app(PromotionService::class)->create(...)` | **no such class**; `PromotionManagementService` exposes only activate/pause/archive/recordUsage (`dev:…/PromotionManagementService.php:15,29,43,57) | `Promotion::create([...])` with the migration's five NOT NULL columns |
         | `app(ReplenishmentRequestService::class)->create(...)` | **no such class**; and the old route writes **transfers**, not `replenishment_requests.status` (`dev:…/ReplenishmentActionController.php:27-55`) | the entry moved to `POST api/v1/replenishment-requests` (`replenishment-requests.store`, `can:replenishment.create`) |
         >
         > **Phase 0 still re-reads every factory `definition()` at the lane base** and fills in whatever required column a factory does not default. That is a re-pin against drift, not a licence to guess: **eighteen of the twenty `METHOD uri` keys were resolved against `php artisan route:list --json` on the shared checkout while rev 5 was written and every one matched, name for name**; the two that did not resolve are entries **8 and 9**, which are wave 2b's routes and do not exist until 2b merges — exactly what the **†** footnote says and exactly what Phase 0 is told to treat as a **STOP**.

      3. **`apps/api/tests/Architecture/RouteActionFixtureContractTest`** — **seven** assertions, with the implementation, because rev 3 described them and shipped none (gate r3, Wave 3 **BLOCKER 3**). Rev 6 adds the two that rev 5's single-predicate `expect` did not need: `test_every_expect_is_well_formed_for_its_kind` (the `kind`/`column`/`value`/`count` shape **and** `soft_deletes` against `Schema::hasColumn`) and `test_the_registry_is_sorted_and_its_keys_are_unique`. **Both run before the matrix**, so a malformed entry is one named failure rather than 8 × N indistinguishable grid failures:
         ~~~php
         <?php

         declare(strict_types=1);

         namespace Tests\Architecture;

         use App\Modules\Company\Domain\Company;
         use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\User;
         use Database\Seeders\RolesAndPermissionsSeeder;
         use Illuminate\Foundation\Testing\RefreshDatabase;
         use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Route as RouteFacade;
         use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Schema;
         use Spatie\Permission\PermissionRegistrar;
         use Tests\TestCase;

         final class RouteActionFixtureContractTest extends TestCase
         {
             use RefreshDatabase;

             /**
              * The base Tests\TestCase declares NEITHER $company NOR $actor — it carries
              * set_time_limit, the lazy-load reset and the quarantine skip and nothing else
              * (dev:apps/api/tests/TestCase.php:9-49). Rev 4 used $this->company /
              * $this->actor and deferred "pin their real names" to Phase 0, which made the
              * printed test not the claimed implementation (gate r4, Wave 3 BLOCKER 2).
              * This test therefore builds its own, in the shape dev:apps/api/tests/Feature/
              * Identity/RBACTest.php:35-60 already uses.
              */
             private Company $company;

             private User $actor;

             protected function setUp(): void
             {
                 parent::setUp();

                 $this->company = Company::factory()->create();
                 app(PermissionRegistrar::class)->setPermissionsTeamId($this->company->tenant_id);
                 $this->seed(RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::class);

                 $this->actor = User::factory()->create([
                     'tenant_id' => $this->company->tenant_id,
                 ]);
                 $this->actor->assignRole('admin');
             }

             /**
              * Gated routes today MINUS the 20 fixtured ones. Phase 0's generator run
              * prints the gated count (MIDDLEWARE + CONTROLLER + FORMREQUEST = 818 at
              * the audit's SHA), so this starts at 818 - 20 = 798 and MAY ONLY FALL.
              * It is set from the generator's printed number in the commit that creates
              * the file, never guessed here.
              */
             private const UNFIXTURED_ROUTE_CEILING = 798;

             /** @return list<array<string, mixed>> */
             private function registry(): array
             {
                 return json_decode(
                     (string) file_get_contents(base_path('tests/Architecture/fixtures/route-action-fixtures.json')),
                     true,
                     flags: JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR,
                 );
             }

             /** @return array<string, \Illuminate\Routing\Route> keyed "METHOD uri" */
             private function liveRoutes(): array
             {
                 $out = [];

                 foreach (RouteFacade::getRoutes() as $route) {
                     foreach ($route->methods() as $verb) {
                         $out[$verb.' '.$route->uri()] = $route;
                     }
                 }

                 return $out;
             }

             public function test_every_key_resolves_in_the_live_router(): void
             {
                 $live = $this->liveRoutes();

                 foreach ($this->registry() as $e) {
                     $this->assertArrayHasKey(
                         $e['key'],
                         $live,
                         "route-action-fixtures.json key does not resolve: {$e['key']}. A lane renamed or removed it; "
                         ."fix the registry rather than letting this become 8 x N indistinguishable grid failures.",
                     );
                 }
             }

             public function test_every_declared_name_matches_the_router(): void
             {
                 $live = $this->liveRoutes();

                 foreach ($this->registry() as $e) {
                     if ($e['name'] === null) {
                         continue;                      // two-thirds of the API declares none
                     }

                     $this->assertSame($e['name'], $live[$e['key']]->getName(), "stale name on {$e['key']}");
                 }
             }

             /** B3's assertion: the expectation's INPUT is checked against the route's actual gate. */
             public function test_permission_agrees_with_its_declared_source(): void
             {
                 $live = $this->liveRoutes();
                 $baseline = json_decode(
                     (string) file_get_contents(base_path('tests/Architecture/baselines/enforcement-style-baseline.json')),
                     true,
                     flags: JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR,
                 );
                 $byKey = [];

                 foreach ($baseline as $row) {
                     foreach (explode('|', $row['method']) as $verb) {
                         $byKey[$verb.' '.ltrim($row['uri'], '/')] = $row;
                     }
                 }

                 foreach ($this->registry() as $e) {
                     $stack = implode('|', $live[$e['key']]->gatherMiddleware());

                     match ($e['permission_source']) {
                         'middleware' => $this->assertStringContainsString(
                             'can:'.$e['permission'],
                             $stack,
                             "{$e['key']} declares permission_source=middleware but its stack has no can:{$e['permission']}",
                         ),
                         'generator' => $this->assertContains(
                             $e['permission'],
                             $byKey[$e['key']]['permission_keys'] ?? [],
                             "{$e['key']} declares permission_source=generator but the enforcement baseline does not "
                             ."name {$e['permission']} for it",
                         ),
                         // A route this wave is CONVERTING: before its cluster's commit the
                         // route still has no can:, and the matrix must not consult the entry.
                         // After it, the assertion is the middleware one.
                         'conversion' => str_contains($stack, 'can:'.$e['permission'])
                             ? $this->addToAssertionCount(1)
                             : $this->assertArrayHasKey(
                                 $e['key'],
                                 $byKey,
                                 "{$e['key']} declares permission_source=conversion but is neither still in the "
                                 .'enforcement baseline nor yet carrying its can: -- one of the two must be true',
                             ),
                         default => $this->fail("unknown permission_source on {$e['key']}"),
                     };
                 }
             }

             public function test_every_interpolation_token_is_produced_by_setup(): void
             {
                 foreach ($this->registry() as $e) {
                     $produced = array_keys(
                         \Tests\Architecture\Support\RouteActionFixtures::make(
                             $e['setup'],
                             $this->company,       // the base TestCase's tenant fixture
                             $this->actor,
                         ),
                     );

                     // {template} is the MATRIX's token, not a fixture's (RolePermissionMatrixTest
                     // injects it because AssignableRole refuses a role outranking the actor).
                     $produced[] = 'template';

                     $used = [];

                     // The scan must cover `where` and NESTED payload structures too: rev 5
                     // walked only the TOP-LEVEL payload values, so
                     // planned_services[0].service_ref_id -- the one nested token in the
                     // registry -- was invisible to this guard (plan gate r5 fix round).
                     $blob = json_encode(
                         [$e['params'], $e['payload'], $e['expect']],
                         JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR,
                     );

                     if (preg_match_all('/\{([a-z0-9_]+)\}/i', $blob, $m) > 0) {
                         $used = $m[1];
                     }

                     foreach (array_unique($used) as $token) {
                         $this->assertContains(
                             $token,
                             $produced,
                             "{$e['key']} interpolates {{$token}} but {$e['setup']} does not return it -- the URI would "
                             .'keep the literal brace and 404, which reads as a deny and is not one',
                         );
                     }
                 }
             }

             public function test_every_expect_table_and_column_exists_in_the_live_schema(): void
             {
                 foreach ($this->registry() as $e) {
                     $table = $e['expect']['table'];
                     $this->assertTrue(Schema::hasTable($table), "{$e['key']} expects a table that does not exist: {$table}");

                     if (($column = $e['expect']['column'] ?? null) !== null) {
                         $this->assertTrue(
                             Schema::hasColumn($table, $column),
                             "{$e['key']} expects {$table}.{$column}, which does not exist",
                         );
                     }

                     foreach (array_keys($e['expect']['where']) as $whereColumn) {
                         $this->assertTrue(
                             Schema::hasColumn($table, (string) $whereColumn),
                             "{$e['key']} keys its expect on {$table}.{$whereColumn}, which does not exist",
                         );
                     }
                 }
             }

             /**
              * expect.kind is WELL FORMED, and soft_deletes is TRUE OF THE SCHEMA.
              *
              * This is the guard that makes rev 6's four predicates safe (plan gate r5, Wave 3
              * BLOCKER 2). Without it, `column` on a delete or a missing `count` on a count
              * entry surfaces as 8 x N indistinguishable grid failures instead of one
              * sentence naming the entry.
              */
             public function test_every_expect_is_well_formed_for_its_kind(): void
             {
                 foreach ($this->registry() as $e) {
                     $expect = $e['expect'];
                     $kind = $expect['kind'] ?? null;

                     $this->assertContains(
                         $kind,
                         ['create', 'update', 'delete', 'count'],
                         "{$e['key']} declares no usable expect.kind",
                     );
                     $this->assertIsBool($expect['soft_deletes'] ?? null, "{$e['key']} must declare expect.soft_deletes");

                     // The declaration is checked against the SCHEMA, so a wrong one is red.
                     $this->assertSame(
                         Schema::hasColumn($expect['table'], 'deleted_at'),
                         $expect['soft_deletes'],
                         "{$e['key']} declares soft_deletes={$expect['soft_deletes']} but "
                         ."{$expect['table']} says otherwise; a delete against a soft-deleting table "
                         .'that ignores deleted_at is green on a row that is still there',
                     );

                     match ($kind) {
                         'update' => $this->assertTrue(
                             array_key_exists('column', $expect) && array_key_exists('value', $expect),
                             "{$e['key']} is kind=update and must carry column AND value",
                         ),
                         'count' => $this->assertIsInt(
                             $expect['count'] ?? null,
                             "{$e['key']} is kind=count and must carry an integer count (the EXACT count AFTER a "
                             .'successful action; the fixture seeds a different one)',
                         ),
                         'create', 'delete' => $this->assertFalse(
                             array_key_exists('column', $expect) || array_key_exists('count', $expect),
                             "{$e['key']} is kind={$kind} and must carry NEITHER column NOR count -- its predicate is "
                             .'row presence, and a column would silently narrow it',
                         ),
                         default => $this->fail("unreachable: kind {$kind} on {$e['key']}"),
                     };
                 }
             }

             /** The registry is SORTED BY KEY and its keys are unique -- the file's own contract. */
             public function test_the_registry_is_sorted_and_its_keys_are_unique(): void
             {
                 $keys = array_column($this->registry(), 'key');
                 $sorted = $keys;
                 sort($sorted, SORT_STRING);

                 $this->assertSame($sorted, $keys, 'route-action-fixtures.json must be sorted by key');
                 $this->assertSame(count($keys), count(array_unique($keys)), 'duplicate key in route-action-fixtures.json');
                 $this->assertCount(20, $keys, 'the registry is twenty entries; growing it is a deliberate act');
             }

             public function test_the_unfixtured_list_is_generated_and_shrink_only(): void
             {
                 $unfixtured = json_decode(
                     (string) file_get_contents(base_path('tests/Architecture/fixtures/unfixtured-routes.json')),
                     true,
                     flags: JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR,
                 );

                 $this->assertLessThanOrEqual(
                     self::UNFIXTURED_ROUTE_CEILING,
                     count($unfixtured),
                     'the unfixtured-route ceiling may only fall',
                 );

                 // ANTI-GROWTH: a NEW gated route with no fixture must appear in the file,
                 // so the matrix cannot silently stop covering new surface. Regenerate and
                 // compare as SETS -- the generator is the oracle, the file is the record.
                 exec('php '.base_path('tools/generate-unfixtured-routes.php').' --stdout', $out, $code);
                 $this->assertSame(0, $code);
                 $this->assertSame(
                     $unfixtured,
                     json_decode(implode('', $out), true, flags: JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR),
                     'unfixtured-routes.json is stale -- re-run tools/generate-unfixtured-routes.php and commit it',
                 );
             }
         }
         ~~~
         > **Assertion 3 is the one B3 adds** and the one that makes a whole grid meaningful: without it, an entry whose `permission` is a key the route does not actually gate on produces a grid that is **green and says nothing**.
         > **`$this->company` / `$this->actor` are THIS test's own properties, built in its own `setUp()` above** — the base `Tests\TestCase` declares neither, which was checked rather than assumed (`dev:apps/api/tests/TestCase.php:9-49` is `set_time_limit(0)`, `LazyLoadViolationLog::reset()` and the `QuarantinedTests` skip). The `setUp()` shape — company, permissions team id, `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder`, an `admin` actor — is copied from `dev:apps/api/tests/Feature/Identity/RBACTest.php:35-60`, which is the repository's existing pattern for exactly this.

      4. **`apps/api/tools/generate-unfixtured-routes.php`** — **the body, not "a short sibling"** (gate r3, Wave 3 **BLOCKER 3**). It is the same booted-router pass, emitting the **gated** routes minus the registry's keys:
         ~~~php
         <?php

         declare(strict_types=1);

         /**
          * Every GATED route with no route-action fixture, as a sorted JSON array of the
          * same "METHOD uri" keys route-action-fixtures.json uses -- so the two files are
          * set-comparable with one array_diff.
          *
          *   cd apps/api && php tools/generate-unfixtured-routes.php            # writes the file
          *   cd apps/api && php tools/generate-unfixtured-routes.php --stdout   # prints it
          *
          * "Gated" is MIDDLEWARE + CONTROLLER + FORMREQUEST, read from the enforcement
          * baseline plus the live stack -- never re-derived here, because two classifiers
          * for one question is the duplicate surface convention 11 forbids.
          */

         $root = getcwd();
         require $root.'/vendor/autoload.php';
         $app = require $root.'/bootstrap/app.php';
         $app->make(Illuminate\Contracts\Console\Kernel::class)->bootstrap();

         $fixtured = array_column(
             json_decode((string) file_get_contents($root.'/tests/Architecture/fixtures/route-action-fixtures.json'), true, flags: JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR),
             'key',
         );

         // The two buckets this wave is CONVERTING are gated too, and they are in the
         // enforcement baseline the sibling generator writes.
         $baselineKeys = [];

         foreach (json_decode((string) file_get_contents($root.'/tests/Architecture/baselines/enforcement-style-baseline.json'), true, flags: JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR) as $row) {
             foreach (explode('|', $row['method']) as $verb) {
                 $baselineKeys[$verb.' '.ltrim($row['uri'], '/')] = true;
             }
         }

         $gated = [];

         /** @var Illuminate\Routing\Route $route */
         foreach (app('router')->getRoutes() as $route) {
             $uri = $route->uri();

             if (! str_starts_with($uri, 'api/')) {
                 continue;
             }

             $stack = implode('|', array_filter($route->gatherMiddleware(), 'is_string'));

             foreach ($route->methods() as $verb) {
                 if ($verb === 'HEAD') {
                     continue;                       // HEAD is Laravel's mirror of GET, never its own surface
                 }

                 $key = $verb.' '.$uri;

                 if (str_contains($stack, 'can:') || str_contains($stack, 'require.any.permission:') || isset($baselineKeys[$key])) {
                     $gated[$key] = true;
                 }
             }
         }

         $unfixtured = array_values(array_diff(array_keys($gated), $fixtured));
         sort($unfixtured);

         $json = json_encode($unfixtured, JSON_PRETTY_PRINT | JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES)."\n";

         if (in_array('--stdout', $argv, true)) {
             echo $json;
             exit(0);
         }

         file_put_contents($root.'/tests/Architecture/fixtures/unfixtured-routes.json', $json);
         fwrite(STDERR, sprintf("gated: %d  fixtured: %d  unfixtured: %d%s", count($gated), count($fixtured), count($unfixtured), PHP_EOL));
         ~~~
         **Run it, and record its STDERR in the handback:**
         ```
         cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/.worktrees/rbac-w3/apps/api
         php tools/generate-unfixtured-routes.php
         ```
         **`UNFIXTURED_ROUTE_CEILING`'s starting value is that run's `unfixtured:` number**, written into the constant in the commit that creates the file — **not guessed here**. *(At the audit's SHA the arithmetic is `642 MIDDLEWARE + 130 CONTROLLER + 46 FORMREQUEST = 818` gated API routes, so the ceiling starts around **798** and the matrix covers about **2.4%**. That is the honest number, and stating it here is what stops "the matrix is green" from being read as "every route is covered".)*
         **Two directions, exactly as the other ratchets have:** the ceiling **may only fall**, and a **new** gated route with no fixture changes the generated set, which `test_the_unfixtured_list_is_generated_and_shrink_only` catches as a stale file — so the matrix cannot silently stop covering new surface even while its absolute coverage is small.
- [ ] **Step 2 — generate the matrix from the registry × the fixtures, and exercise both directions for every cell.** For each `SystemRoleTemplate` case and each **fixtured** route, the expected outcome is derived from `PermissionRegistry::templateGrants()`; the request comes from the fixture. A hand-maintained expectation table would be a second catalogue (convention 11) and would drift the first time a template default changed — which is why the **expectation** stays generated and only the **request** is declared.
      > **`test_every_expect_is_well_formed_for_its_kind` needs `Schema` and nothing else new** — the block already imports `Illuminate\Support\Facades\Schema` for the table/column assertion, and `json_encode` in the token scan is a language function.
      **Every deny cell sends a payload that would have succeeded for a holder**, and asserts the `expect` row is unchanged. **A 403 that happens because the request body was invalid is not a deny**, and `RouteActionFixtureContractTest` is what stops one reaching the grid.
      > **Runtime is the risk.** 8 templates × the fixtured route count is not a test that can run on every push. **This plan implements it as a PG-lane test excluded from the fast lane and run in CI on a schedule and before promotion**, with a `--filter` by module so a lane can run only its own cluster. Its README states that; a matrix that is too slow to run is a matrix nobody runs.
      > **Coverage is stated, never implied.** The matrix's README and the handback both record *"N of M gated routes fixtured"*, so *"the matrix is green"* can never be read as *"every route is covered"*.
      **`apps/api/tests/Feature/Authorization/RolePermissionMatrixTest.php`, the class body** — rev 4 left this as an algorithm and rev 5 supplies it, because a "final regression matrix" that exists only as prose is the one artefact of this wave a reviewer cannot check (gate r4, Wave 3 **BLOCKER 2**, last bullet):
      ~~~php
      <?php

      declare(strict_types=1);

      namespace Tests\Feature\Authorization;

      use App\Modules\Company\Domain\Company;
      use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Authorization\PermissionRegistry;
      use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\Enums\SystemRoleTemplate;
      use App\Modules\Identity\Domain\User;
      use Database\Seeders\RolesAndPermissionsSeeder;
      use Illuminate\Foundation\Testing\RefreshDatabase;
      use Illuminate\Support\Facades\DB;
      use PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\DataProvider;
      use Spatie\Permission\PermissionRegistrar;
      use Tests\Architecture\Support\RouteActionFixtures;
      use Tests\TestCase;

      /**
       * The per-role regression matrix: every seeded template x every FIXTURED route,
       * both directions, with the SAME payload on the allow and the deny cell.
       *
       * EXPECTATION comes from PermissionRegistry::templateGrants() (role -> keys) joined
       * to the registry entry's `permission` (route -> key). REQUEST comes from the
       * fixture. Neither is hand-maintained; a hand-written expectation table would be a
       * second catalogue (convention 11).
       *
       * PG lane only, excluded from the fast lane (see the group attribute), because
       * 8 templates x N fixtured routes is not a per-push cost.
       */
      #[\PHPUnit\Framework\Attributes\Group('matrix')]
      final class RolePermissionMatrixTest extends TestCase
      {
          use RefreshDatabase;

          private Company $company;

          /** The grid, as PHPUnit cases: one per (template, entry). */
          public static function cells(): \Generator
          {
              /** @var list<array<string, mixed>> $registry */
              $registry = json_decode(
                  (string) file_get_contents(
                      dirname(__DIR__, 2).'/Architecture/fixtures/route-action-fixtures.json',
                  ),
                  true,
                  flags: JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR,
              );

              foreach (SystemRoleTemplate::cases() as $template) {
                  foreach ($registry as $entry) {
                      yield $template->value.' :: '.$entry['key'] => [$template->value, $entry];
                  }
              }
          }

          protected function setUp(): void
          {
              parent::setUp();

              $this->company = Company::factory()->create();
              app(PermissionRegistrar::class)->setPermissionsTeamId($this->company->tenant_id);
              $this->seed(RolesAndPermissionsSeeder::class);
          }

          /**
           * @param  array<string, mixed>  $entry
           */
          #[DataProvider('cells')]
          public function test_cell(string $template, array $entry): void
          {
              /** @var array<string, list<string>> $grants */
              $grants = app(PermissionRegistry::class)->templateGrants();
              $holds = in_array($entry['permission'], $grants[$template] ?? [], true);

              // The principal holds EXACTLY ONE template role. That is the whole point of
              // the grid: a cell must be attributable to one template, never to a union.
              $principal = User::factory()->create([
                  'tenant_id' => $this->company->tenant_id,
                  'principal_kind' => 'human',
              ]);
              $principal->syncRoles([$template]);

              // The fixture is created by a SEPARATE actor with admin rights, so a setup
              // failure can never be confused with the denial under test.
              $admin = User::factory()->create(['tenant_id' => $this->company->tenant_id]);
              $admin->assignRole('admin');
              $tokens = RouteActionFixtures::make($entry['setup'], $this->company, $admin);

              // {template} is the MATRIX's own token, not a fixture's. Three entries send a
              // role name, and AssignableRole refuses "a role that grants permissions beyond
              // your own" (dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Rules/AssignableRole.php:50),
              // so a hard-coded name would 422 the ALLOW cell of every template that does not
              // outrank it. A principal's OWN template role always passes the guard.
              $tokens['template'] = $template;

              // Exactly one entry declares an environment precondition (the enrichment
              // submission makes an outbound platform call). It is DECLARED, never implied.
              foreach ($entry['requires'] ?? [] as $requirement) {
                  match ($requirement) {
                      'http_fake' => Http::fake(),
                      default => self::fail("unknown requires '{$requirement}' on {$entry['key']}"),
                  };
              }

              [$method, $uri] = explode(' ', (string) $entry['key'], 2);
              $uri = '/'.ltrim($this->interpolate($uri, $entry['params'], $tokens), '/');
              /** @var array<string, mixed> $payload */
              $payload = json_decode($this->interpolate(
                  json_encode($entry['payload'], JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR),
                  [],
                  $tokens,
              ), true, flags: JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR);

              $before = $this->expectMatches($entry, $tokens);
              self::assertFalse(
                  $before,
                  "{$entry['setup']} did not seed a NON-MATCHING pre-state for {$entry['key']}; "
                  .'an unchanged-because-already-matching row proves nothing on the deny cell',
              );

              $response = $this->actingAs($principal, 'sanctum')->json($method, $uri, $payload);

              if ($holds) {
                  $response->assertSuccessful();
                  self::assertTrue(
                      $this->expectMatches($entry, $tokens),
                      "{$template} holds {$entry['permission']} and got 2xx on {$entry['key']}, but the "
                      .'expect row did not change — a 2xx that wrote nothing is not an allow',
                  );

                  return;
              }

              $response->assertForbidden();
              self::assertFalse(
                  $this->expectMatches($entry, $tokens),
                  "{$template} was refused {$entry['key']} with 403 but the expect row CHANGED — "
                  .'the denial did not prevent the write',
              );
          }

          /**
           * "HAS THE ACTION HAPPENED?" -- evaluated against the database, never the response,
           * and DISPATCHED ON expect.kind (plan gate r5, Wave 3 BLOCKER 2).
           *
           * Rev 5 had one predicate -- exists(), with a column narrowing it -- and defined
           * "no column" as existence. That is wrong in two directions at once:
           *
           *   - a DELETE's row EXISTS before the request, so the universal
           *     assertFalse($before) guard above was RED BY CONSTRUCTION on
           *     `DELETE roles/{id}` and `DELETE scheduling/appointments/{id}`; and
           *   - a pivot COUNT ("this role now has 2 permissions", "this user now has 1 role")
           *     has no existence form at all, so `PATCH roles/{id}` and
           *     `POST users/{userId}/roles` could not express what they were measuring.
           *
           * Four kinds, four predicates, each one true EXACTLY when the action has happened.
           *
           * SOFT DELETES: a tombstone is still a row. Whenever the entry declares
           * soft_deletes, every kind adds whereNull('deleted_at') -- so `delete` means
           * "gone from the LIVE set" and `create`/`update` cannot be satisfied by a row an
           * earlier grid cell soft-deleted. RouteActionFixtureContractTest asserts the
           * declaration against Schema::hasColumn(), so a wrong one is red, not silent.
           */
          private function expectMatches(array $entry, array $tokens): bool
          {
              /** @var array{kind: string, table: string, where: array<string, mixed>, soft_deletes: bool, column?: string, value?: mixed, count?: int} $expect */
              $expect = $entry['expect'];
              $where = [];

              foreach ($expect['where'] as $col => $v) {
                  $where[$col] = is_bool($v) ? $v : $this->interpolate((string) $v, [], $tokens);
              }

              $query = DB::table($expect['table'])->where($where);

              if ($expect['soft_deletes'] === true) {
                  $query->whereNull('deleted_at');
              }

              return match ($expect['kind']) {
                  // The row the route CREATES did not exist before and does after.
                  'create' => $query->exists(),

                  // The column held something else before and holds `value` after. A bool
                  // value (is_recalled) must NOT go through string interpolation.
                  'update' => $query
                      ->where(
                          $expect['column'],
                          is_bool($expect['value'])
                              ? $expect['value']
                              : $this->interpolate((string) $expect['value'], [], $tokens),
                      )
                      ->exists(),

                  // The row was there before and is gone from the live set after.
                  'delete' => ! $query->exists(),

                  // The pivot count was something else before and is exactly `count` after.
                  'count' => $query->count() === $expect['count'],

                  default => throw new LogicException(
                      "unknown expect.kind '{$expect['kind']}' on {$entry['key']}; the contract "
                      .'test should have refused this entry before the matrix ever ran',
                  ),
              };
          }

          /**
           * Replace every {token} from the fixture's return, plus the entry's own params.
           *
           * RouteActionFixtureContractTest has already proved every token used here is
           * produced, so a surviving brace is a contract-test regression, not a skip.
           *
           * @param  array<string, mixed>  $params
           * @param  array<string, string>  $tokens
           */
          private function interpolate(string $subject, array $params, array $tokens): string
          {
              foreach ($params as $name => $value) {
                  $subject = str_replace('{'.$name.'}', (string) $value, $subject);
              }

              foreach ($tokens as $name => $value) {
                  $subject = str_replace('{'.$name.'}', $value, $subject);
              }

              return $subject;
          }
      }
      ~~~
      > **Four things this body pins that the algorithm left open.** (1) **The pre-state is asserted before the request**, so a fixture that forgot to seed a non-matching value fails loudly instead of making every deny cell pass for free — and it is now a *truthful* assertion for all four kinds, which rev 5's single-predicate version was not. (2) **The fixture is created by a separate `admin` actor**, so a setup failure can never be read as the denial under test. (3) **`expectMatches()` dispatches on `expect.kind`** — `create` → `exists()`, `update` → value match, `delete` → `! exists()` over the live set, `count` → exact pivot count. The eight `create` entries, eight `update` entries, two `delete` entries and two `count` entries each get the predicate that is actually true of them (plan gate r5, Wave 3 **BLOCKER 2**). (4) **`{template}` and `requires` are the matrix's two injections**, and both are declared in the registry rather than implied by a comment.
      > **Imports this body needs beyond rev 5's**: `Illuminate\Support\Facades\Http` (the one `requires: ["http_fake"]` entry) and `LogicException` (the `match` default arm). `DB`, `User`, `PermissionRegistry` and `RouteActionFixtures` were already imported.

- [ ] **Step 3 — the deny paths the spec names explicitly** get their own named cases beside the generated grid, because they are the ones a generated grid cannot express: `ADMIN_PERMISSION_FLOOR` (EC-13), `LAST_ADMIN_*` (EC-1..EC-4b), `SYSTEM_ROLE_PROTECTED` (EC-12), the D4 shaping (a bare `users.assign-roles` holder sees no `permissions` key), and `SERVICE_ACCOUNT_USE_DEDICATED_ROUTES` (EC-34).
- [ ] **Step 4 — `cashier`, `operator`, `viewer` and `technician` are asserted CLEAN of every financially decisive key** in the eight SoD groups — the same four templates spec §4.7 names as already satisfying SoD-1. That makes the matrix double as an SoD regression, without touching the baseline or the ceiling.
- [ ] **Step 5 — run** (PG lane, expect a long run; record the wall time in the handback so the schedule can be sized) and **commit.** Exact add list:
      ```
      git add apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/route-action-fixtures.json \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/fixtures/unfixtured-routes.json \
              apps/api/tools/generate-unfixtured-routes.php \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/Support/RouteActionFixtures.php \
              apps/api/tests/Architecture/RouteActionFixtureContractTest.php \
              apps/api/tests/Feature/Authorization/RolePermissionMatrixTest.php \
              apps/api/tests/Feature/Authorization/matrix/README.md
      ```
      **`tools/generate-unfixtured-routes.php` is in the list** because `unfixtured-routes.json` is generated, never hand-written, and a generated artefact whose generator is not committed is one nobody can regenerate. It is a short sibling of Phase 0's enforcement generator: the same booted-router pass, emitting the gated routes **minus** the registry's keys.
      Subject: **`Phase 0.6.47: Add the route-action fixture registry and the per-role regression matrix asserting every seeded role's deny paths`**, both trailers. The body records the fixtured/unfixtured split and the `UNFIXTURED_ROUTE_CEILING`'s starting value.

## Convention-09 obligations (spec §6, wave-3 row)

| Axis | Test | Assertion (on **data meaning**, never on status) |
|---|---|---|
| **Second company** | `PosPinPopulationSecondCompanyTest` | After `principal_kind` filtering (wave 2b) **and** this wave's POS conversions, the PIN population for company A's terminal contains **exactly** company A's active human PIN holders — a company-B operator with a PIN is **absent from the returned rows**, not merely denied |
| **Second location** | `PosApprovalScopeSecondLocationTest` | A `pos.approve_*` permission is honoured at the **selected** terminal's location and refused at the other, asserted on the approval **outcome**, not on the button's presence |
| **Re-run / idempotency** | `PruneOrphanPermissionsTest` | `permissions:prune-orphans --confirm` run **twice** → the second run **deletes nothing**, asserted by row count and by a query-log count of zero DELETEs |

`TenantOnlyUniqueOnCatalogueTablesRatchetTest` must be **green and unedited** — wave 3 adds no unique key and no table.

## Reviewer gate

### The gate is PER CLUSTER, and the whole-diff review is in addition to it, not instead of it

> **Rev 2 described one whole-diff tenancy review plus special reviews for selected modules** (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 4**). The programme requires `tenancy-authz-reviewer` **plus the owning-module reviewer** attached to **each converted cluster** (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-10-rbac-programme-execution-plan.md:199-209`). The difference is not procedural: a whole-diff review of a 176-site sweep across thirty-six commits is where a reviewer approves the pattern and misses the one route whose permission is subtly different — which is the defect check 1 below exists to catch and the reason the programme attaches the gate to the unit a reviewer can actually hold in their head.

**Every cluster commit is gated before the next cluster opens.** The gate is two reviewers, and the second is decided by the cluster, not by preference:

| Cluster commit | `tenancy-authz-reviewer` | Owning-module reviewer (**also mandatory**) |
|---|---|---|
| `0.6.1` Replenishment | ✔ | `inventory-costing-reviewer` — a replenishment request becomes a transfer or a PO |
| `0.6.2` Voucher | ✔ | `treasury-reviewer` — a voucher is a monetary instrument |
| `0.6.3` Tenant | ✔ | none beyond tenancy; **confirm no route is a mis-classified `SUPERADMIN_ONLY`** |
| `0.6.4` PlatformIntegration | ✔ | `imports-reviewer` — enrichment submission is an import-shaped surface |
| `0.6.5` Promotion · `0.6.6` Coupon | ✔ | `treasury-reviewer` — both change what a sale collects |
| `0.6.7` BatchExpiry | ✔ | `inventory-costing-reviewer` — **mandatory**, and it also carries Task 3-3 step 5's location-ticket disposition |
| `0.6.8` Menu | ✔ | none beyond tenancy |
| `0.6.9` Identity | ✔ | **wave 2a/2b rewrote both controllers** — the reviewer re-pins every line and confirms no D4 shaping, floor call or service-target refusal was disturbed |
| `0.6.10` Scheduling | ✔ | none beyond tenancy |
| `0.6.11`–`0.6.12` Workshop | ✔ | `inventory-costing-reviewer` for the line/bundle commits — a work-order line moves stock |
| `0.6.13`–`0.6.36` **each POS controller** | ✔ | `fiscal-pos-reviewer` — **mandatory on all twenty-four**, and on the seven device-sync ones it answers Step 2's retry question specifically |
| `0.6.38` POS ladder | ✔ | `fiscal-pos-reviewer` — it is a POS **release** |
| `0.6.46` Marketplace re-gating | ✔ | none beyond tenancy; the guard change is the whole content |
| `0.6.48`+ literal conversions | ✔ | the module's own reviewer, per the same table |

**Two economies, so twenty-four POS gates do not become twenty-four full reviews.** First, **the recipe is reviewed once** — at `0.6.1`, where the body records its seven steps — and every later cluster's prompt asks *"does this deviate from the recipe, and if so does its body say why?"* rather than re-deriving it. Second, **a cluster whose before/after principal sets are identical for every route and whose deny tests are present is a SHORT review**: checks 1 and 2 below, cited per route, and nothing else. The long review is reserved for a cluster that deviates.

**`treasury-reviewer` and `imports-reviewer` become mandatory by the table above, not by discovery.** Rev 2 said both were *"expected to be not required"* because neither module appears in the CSV's `CONTROLLER`/`FORMREQUEST` sets — which is true of the **Treasury and Imports modules** and beside the point: the reviewer's mandate is the **domain**, and Voucher, Promotion, Coupon and PlatformIntegration are treasury- and imports-shaped surfaces owned by other modules. **Phase 0's regenerated classification is still the authority for which modules appear at all**, and a module that appears and is not in the table above gains its owning reviewer there, recorded in the handback.

### Whole-diff review, IN ADDITION

**`tenancy-authz-reviewer` — mandatory, on the whole diff, after the last cluster.** It answers the questions no single cluster can: the ceilings' end state, the baseline's emptiness, and whether the thirty-six commits together left one enforcement style. Prompt outline:

> Review lane `lane/rbac-w3` (base `dev` at `<sha>`, tip `<sha>`) against
> `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-10-roles-permissions-catalogue-design.md` rev 9
> §§2, 4.2.2, 4.4.1, 4.4.3–4.4.4, 4.5, 4.9.2–4.9.3, 4.10 item 4, 6, 10 and 8's
> wave-3 row, and against this plan. Cite `file:line` for every claim; verify
> against the tree, never from the diff alone.
>
> 1. **Did any conversion change who may act?** This is the finding that matters.
>    Sample at least twenty converted routes across at least six modules; for
>    each, derive the set of principals allowed **before** (from `dev`) and
>    **after**, and say whether they are identical. Any difference is a BLOCKER
>    unless the plan named it as a deliberate finding.
> 2. **Is the deny path actually tested?** Pick ten converted routes and find the
>    test that asserts a non-holder gets 403 **and no row is written**. A test
>    asserting only 2xx for a holder does not count.
> 3. **Is E-3 respected?** Find every check the sweep left in a controller and
>    say, for each, whether it is row-level (correct) or an action gate that was
>    missed (a finding). `work-orders.view_financials` is the one to look at
>    first.
>  4. **Do the ceilings only fall?** Compare both `UNCOVERED_*_CEILING` values,
>    the enforcement-style baseline count and `UNFIXTURED_ROUTE_CEILING` against
>    the lane base. Any growth, in any direction, is a BLOCKER.
>  4a. **Is the sweep's denominator right?** Parse
>    `02-route-enforcement-sweep.csv` with a real CSV parser and report the
>    `classification` tally. It must sum to the row count. If a lane document
>    still says 127 / 44 anywhere, that is a finding — the file has no unescaped
>    commas and the number is 130 / 46 = 176.
>  4b. **Can the matrix tell a denial from a failure?** Open
>    `route-action-fixtures.json` and pick five entries. For each, say what
>    `setup` creates, what the deny cell sends, and which row proves the action
>    did not happen. Then read `unfixtured-routes.json`'s count and say what
>    fraction of gated routes the matrix actually covers — and whether the
>    README and the handback both say so.
> 5. **POS:** is each controller its own commit with its own tests? Then check
>    the four sync controllers specifically: does a 403 on a sync endpoint
>    surface on the device as a visible authority problem, or does the device
>    retry it silently? Cite the device code.
> 6. **The ladder removal:** confirm `fetchDiscountPermissions` is wired
>    **before** the ladder is deleted, and that an admin's offline PIN setup does
>    not end up with `can_discount: false`. Then read the offline test and say
>    what it proves that the online test does not.
> 7. **`MembershipRole`:** find every remaining read and classify each as authz
>    or display. If `UserController`'s `isOwner()` was converted, say which
>    permission and whether it means the same thing.
> 8. **Dead code:** confirm `LotActionPermissionDelta`, its config and its env
>    flag were deleted **together**, and that the fleet marker check was actually
>    run rather than asserted.
> 9. **The prune command:** confirm it is a LOCKED writer, that it refuses a
>    granted orphan, that `--confirm` is required to write, and that it appears
>    in **no** deploy row.
> 10. **The 22 keys:** if they were retired, verify all three Q-w3-5 conditions
>     were checked and how. If they were not, verify the deferral is recorded
>     rather than the task being half-done.
> 11. **The matrix:** confirm it is generated from the registry rather than
>     hand-written, that every deny cell sends a payload that would have
>     succeeded for a holder, and that `cashier`/`operator`/`viewer`/`technician`
>     are asserted clean of the financially decisive keys.
> 12. **Convention 11:** every noun this wave touched has one glossary row, one
>     table, one write path, one surface — and the three declared synonym
>     collisions are stated.
>
> Verdict: MERGE / MERGE-WITH-FIXES / CHANGES-REQUIRED. Never auto-merge.

**`fiscal-pos-reviewer` — mandatory** for Task 3-2 (91 conversions) and Task 3-3 (the ladder). Ask: does any converted POS route change what a cashier can do at the till? Is the device-authored contract intact — does a newly-gated sync endpoint's 403 reach the operator? Is `effective = min(terminal, user)` computed on the device now, and is `pos_terminals.max_discount_percent` (default `0.00`) handled so a freshly provisioned till does not silently block every discount? Does the offline path still work after days offline?

**`inventory-costing-reviewer` — mandatory** for the BatchExpiry cluster and for Task 3-3 step 5's disposition of the location ticket. Ask: if location scope was added to the batch writes, is the stock-ownership question answered, and is there a two-location allow/deny test? If it was deferred, is the ticket updated with a dated note rather than left as it was?

**`treasury-reviewer` and `imports-reviewer` — MANDATORY on the clusters the per-cluster table names** (Voucher, Promotion, Coupon for treasury; PlatformIntegration for imports), and additionally on any cluster Phase 0's regenerated classification shows touching their own modules' routes. Rev 2 expected both to be *"not required"* on the ground that neither **module** appears in the CSV's `CONTROLLER`/`FORMREQUEST` sets; that is true and beside the point, because a reviewer's mandate is the **domain** and a voucher route is a treasury surface wherever it is declared (plan gate r2, **MAJOR 4**). **Say so in the handback rather than deciding it silently.**

**`frontend-conventions-reviewer` — mandatory** for Task 3-8 and the POS FE changes. Ask: is `moduleKey` typed `ModuleKey` and `permission` typed against the generated `Permission` union, with the `nav.*` pseudo-keys now explicit `MODULE_PERMISSIONS` entries? Is `MODULE_PERMISSIONS` still **fail-closed**? Does the sidebar render the right tree for `admin`, `manager` and `cashier`, proven in the browser? Design tokens, `t()` for every string, `tenantScopedKey` for every tenant query?

## Handback

`docs/handoff/HANDBACK-rbac-w3-<ISO date>.md`.

It carries: the lane base and tip SHAs; the `Phase` ordinal actually used (Q-w3-1); **the regenerated enforcement counts and how they compare to 130 / 46 = 176** (Q-w3-2); **the `Phase 0.6.48`-onward tail's module-to-ordinal mapping** (Global Constraints); **the POS controller count the generator actually produced and, if it is not 24, the shifted allocation**; **the by-file permission-literal list, both runs** (Task 3-6 step 1); **the fixtured/unfixtured route split** and `UNFIXTURED_ROUTE_CEILING` before and after (Task 3-9); the per-cluster before/after allowed-principal sets for every route where they differed (there should be none — if there are, each is a finding, named); both ceilings before and after; the POS device probe's verbatim output; the Task 3-6 step 2 fleet-marker evidence; **Task 3-7's three Q-w3-5 conditions and their answers**; the disposition of the two tickets; the matrix's wall time; and the residuals below.

### Residuals

| # | Residual | Owner / wave |
|---|---|---|
| **R-w3-1** | Any route the sweep left unconverted because converting it would have **changed who may act**. Each is named with its before/after sets and the key that does not exist to express it. These are **permission-declaration** work, i.e. wave-1-shaped, and belong in a follow-up lane, not in a sweep. | follow-up lane |
| **R-w3-2** | Any E-3 (row-level) check deliberately left in a controller rather than moved to a policy. Moving it is a real improvement and a real risk; the sweep's job was the **action** gate. | follow-up lane |
| **R-w3-3** | **Task 3-7 if deferred** — the 22 keys stay deprecated-but-present, `permissions:report-dead` keeps returning 22, and the retirement is a one-commit follow-up once Q-w3-5's conditions hold. | next release |
| **R-w3-4** | **`marketplace.admin`** if the module has been enabled for any tenant since wave 1: re-gating becomes a live behaviour change and needs its own lane. The ticket stays open with the new fact. | follow-up lane |
| **R-w3-5** | **Batch writes and location scope** if Task 3-3 step 5 chose deferral: `DELETE /batches/{uuid}` and `POST /batches/{uuid}/recall` remain permission-gated but location-blind. | ticket, follow-up lane |
| **R-w3-6** | The **regression matrix is schedule-run, not push-run.** A role/permission regression introduced between scheduled runs is caught at promotion, not at push. Sizing it down (sampling, or per-module sharding on push) is a real option and is recorded rather than assumed. | CI owner |
| **R-w3-7** | `RolesAndPermissionsSeeder`'s **marked-tenant preservation branch** survives, because retiring it means retiring `lane/w-lot-a-1a`'s mandatory `RolesAndPermissionsSeederMarkedTenantTest` — that lane's evidence, not this one's. `tenants:seed RolesAndPermissionsSeeder` therefore remains a no-op on a marked tenant, which is harmless because the deploy path is `permissions:sync-fleet`. | lane owner |
| **R-w3-8** | The **5-minute `/auth/me` staleness window** (EC-17) is still untouched, and this wave makes it slightly more visible: a sidebar that now distinguishes `moduleKey` from `permission` renders from the same possibly-stale payload. **Every mutating request is still 403.** UX exposure, not a security one. | out of scope |
| **R-w3-9** | **Any permission-literal site Task 3-6 step 1 could not convert**, named by owning module from the generated by-file list, with the reason. If the list is materially larger than this lane can absorb, the step becomes its own lane and the modules done here are recorded — the ratchet is shrink-only either way, so a partial conversion loses nothing. **The baseline file is never deleted while non-empty.** | follow-up lane |
| **R-w3-10** | **The matrix's unfixtured routes.** `unfixtured-routes.json` starts large and is the honest measure of the matrix's reach: a gated route with no `route-action-fixtures.json` entry is asserted by nothing. `UNFIXTURED_ROUTE_CEILING` is shrink-only and a new gated route without a fixture fails the build, so the gap can only close — but *"the matrix is green"* must never be read as *"every route is covered"*, which is why the README and the handback both carry the *N of M* line. | follow-up lanes |
| **R-w3-12** | **`marketplace.admin`'s REMOVAL**, as distinct from its deprecation. Task 3-8 (`Phase 0.6.46`) deprecates the key in the Marketplace manifest; removing it follows the same one-release rule Q-w3-5 states for the other twenty-two, and Task 3-7 has already committed by then with a list of exactly twenty-two that does not contain it (plan gate r2, **minor 3**). One commit in a later release. | next release |
| **R-w3-11** | **`SOLE_AUTHZ_EXCEPTION` if Task 3-4 could not convert `isOwner()`.** The read stays, documented as spec §2's declared sole exception, and the spec's Membership row is updated to say *documented* rather than *removed*. Converting it to the nearest permission key without proving the before/after principal sets are identical would be a silent widening. | follow-up lane |

## Deploy

| # | Step | Notes |
|---:|---|---|
| 0 | `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet --dry-run` | **first, always.** WRITE-free, **not** failure-free. |
| 1 | `php artisan tenants:migrate-rolling` | wave 3 adds **no migration**; the step runs because the sequence is fixed and `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` asserts it. |
| 2 | `php artisan permissions:sync-fleet` | **only if Task 3-7 ran** — retiring 22 keys changes `activeKeys()`, so `admin`'s full-replace at sync step 8 must run. If 3-7 was deferred, wave 3 declares no key and this step is a no-op that still runs. |
| 3 | `php artisan permission:cache-reset` | |
| 4 | `php artisan permissions:export-frontend-map` | its input is now `PermissionRegistry` directly (Task 3-6 step 3). |
| — | **`marketplace.admin`'s re-gating is a ROUTE change, not a permission change** | The four seller routes move from the tenant pipeline to `['auth:sanctum-admin','super_admin','throttle:admin-sensitive']` (Task 3-8 step 2). No migration, no sync step — but the key joins the deprecation set, so if Task 3-7 also ran, step 2's sync is what removes it from `admin`. Confirm the module's kill-switch is still `false` **before** the deploy, not after. |
| — | **`php artisan permissions:prune-orphans --confirm`** | **NOT a deploy step.** A deliberate operator action, run **after** the deploy has been observed healthy, per tenant or via the fleet form. `PermissionDeploySequenceTest` fails the build if it appears in a deploy row. |
| — | **The POS build** | Task 3-3 is a **POS release**. It ships on the POS's own cadence, and until a device updates it keeps its ladder — which is safe, because the server-side gates are unchanged by the ladder's removal. Record the POS version that carries it. |

**Deploy-checklist row.** *After deploy, confirm `GET /api/v1/admin/monitoring/health` reports `permissions_sync.healthy = true`. Then, before running the prune, re-run `php artisan permissions:report-dead` on production and confirm it returns what the lane's handback says it should — the prune deletes rows, and a report that disagrees with the handback means the tree and the fleet are not in the state the retirement assumed.*

---

## Rollback

**Wave 3 has no migration and no destructive deploy step. Its rollback granularity is the per-module commit** — which is why the per-module commit boundary is a Global Constraint rather than a style preference.

| Revert | Effect | Must also revert |
|---|---|---|
| Task 3-9 (matrix) | the regression matrix disappears; nothing else changes | — |
| Task 3-8 (sidebar / marketplace) | `permission?: ModuleKey` returns; `marketplace.admin` returns to the tenant guard | — |
| Task 3-7 (key retirement) | the 22 keys return to the registry — **but the rows are already gone** if the prune was run | **re-run `permissions:sync-fleet`** to recreate the rows; a registry key with no row is a fail-closed 403 for everyone |
| Task 3-6 step 4 (prune command) | the command disappears; rows already deleted stay deleted | — |
| Task 3-6 step 3 (seeder shims) | the exporter's input returns to the statics | the statics must be restored **in the same revert commit**, or the exporter calls methods that do not exist |
| Task 3-6 step 2 (`LotActionPermissionDelta`) | the dead class returns; **no behavioural change either way** — sync never called it | — |
| Task 3-6 step 1 (literal baseline) | the PHPStan rule stops hard-failing | — |
| Task 3-5 (glossary) | documentation only | — |
| Task 3-4 (`MembershipRole` / enums) | `isOwner()` returns as an authz read; `SystemRoleName` returns | its callers, re-pointed in the same revert |
| **Task 3-3 (POS ladder)** | the ladder returns and the terminal ceiling is ignored again | **revert as a POS release**, and only as a whole: reverting the ladder deletion without reverting the `fetchDiscountPermissions` wiring leaves two authority sources, which is worse than either |
| **Task 3-2 (POS sweep)** | per controller — each is its own commit | each controller's ceiling decrement in the same revert |
| **Task 3-1 (non-POS sweep)** | per cluster — each is its own commit | each cluster's ceiling decrement and baseline entries in the same revert |

| Task 3-6 step 1 (literal conversions) | per module — each is its own commit | that module's regenerated baseline in the same revert; the baseline **file** returns if it had been deleted |
| The fixture registry (Task 3-9 step 1) | the matrix has no request source and cannot run | `unfixtured-routes.json` and `UNFIXTURED_ROUTE_CEILING` in the same revert — a registry reverted without its complement makes the ceiling assert against a set that no longer exists |

**A partial revert must restore its ceiling and its baseline entries in the SAME commit.** A reverted conversion whose baseline entry stays deleted makes the ratchet's **stale** direction fail on the next run, and a reverted conversion whose ceiling stays lowered makes the **anti-growth** direction fail — either way the build goes red for a reason that has nothing to do with the revert's intent, and someone "fixes" it by raising a ceiling. That is the failure this note exists to prevent.

---

## Change log rev 5 → rev 6

Fix round on **Codex plan gate r5** (`docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-11-rbac-plan-234-codex-gate-r5.md`). Wave 3: **3 BLOCKER, 1 MAJOR, 0 minor**. All applied; nothing rejected. Four further defects surfaced while re-validating the twenty entries against `dev`, and are recorded here.

| ID | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B1** | The committed fixture registry is still not supplied — one JSONC schema object plus a twenty-row summary table, then an instruction to build the twenty entries; the claimed first red and the supplied contract/matrix classes are therefore not reproducible from the plan | **ACCEPTED in full. The complete `route-action-fixtures.json` is in the plan**, inside a `<details>` block, as a JSON array of **20** entries sorted by `key`. **It was validated with a JSON parser**, and the validation covers more than parseability: unique keys, sorted order, the ten required fields on every entry, `permission_source` in the closed set `{middleware, generator, conversion}`, `column`+`value` present **iff** `kind == "update"`, `count` present **iff** `kind == "count"`, `column`/`count` **absent** on `create`/`delete`, and every `{token}` in `params`/`payload`/`expect` produced by that entry's declared `setup` (or by the matrix's own `{template}`). **All 20 were re-validated against `dev`**: 18 route keys resolved in the module `routes.php` files, the two `/service-accounts` ones are wave 2b's as the footnote already said; every `permission` was read off the route's `can:`, the controller's inline `can()`/`Gate::` or the FormRequest's `authorize()`; and every payload field was read off the live validation rules | Task 3-9 Step 1 item 1 (the schema, the index table and the full JSON) |
| **B2** | The supplied matrix cannot model its own delete and count rows: the test universally requires `expectMatches()` false **before** every request, while an `expect` with no `column` is defined as `exists()` — so the two delete entries fail before their DELETE is sent, and the two promised pivot-count transitions are inexpressible | **ACCEPTED.** `expect.kind` is a **required** field with four values, and `expectMatches()` is a `match` over it: `create` → `exists()`; `update` → value match; `delete` → **`! exists()`**; `count` → `count() === expect.count` (the exact count **after** a successful action, with the fixture seeding a different one). A kind table in the schema section states the before/after semantics of each. **Soft deletes are handled rather than assumed**: `expect.soft_deletes` is required on every entry, `expectMatches()` appends `whereNull('deleted_at')` whenever it is true — so `delete` means *"gone from the live set"*, which is the only correct reading for `scheduling_appointments` — and `RouteActionFixtureContractTest` asserts the declaration against `Schema::hasColumn()`, so a wrong one is red rather than silent. Two new contract-test cases (`…is_well_formed_for_its_kind`, `…sorted_and_its_keys_are_unique`) run **before** the matrix, so a malformed entry is one named failure instead of 8 × N | Task 3-9 Step 1 items 1 and 3; Step 2 (`expectMatches`, the `{template}`/`requires` injections and the four-things note) |
| **B3** | `IdentityFixture::serviceAccount()` writes a nonexistent user attribute, `pos_pin_hash` | **ACCEPTED.** The field is **`pos_pin`** — property (`dev:apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Domain/User.php:40`), fillable (`:81-97`) and cast `'hashed'` (`:115-126`); `pos_pin_hash` appears nowhere on the model, and `User::factory()->create()` would have raised on it. Corrected, with the citation and the note that wave 2a makes the column **nullable** for a service principal and does not rename it | Task 3-9 Step 1 item 2 (`IdentityFixture::serviceAccount`) |
| **M1** | The operative correction history contradicts the current route table: the corrections rows still say `batches`/`status` and `actions.create-transfer` | **ACCEPTED.** Both rows are re-stated against the JSON, with their evidence: `Batch::$table = 'product_batches'` and `recall()` writes `is_recalled`/`recall_reason`/`recalled_at` — there is no `status` column (`dev:…/Domain/Entities/Batch.php:19,134-138`); and the replenishment entry is `POST api/v1/replenishment-requests` (`can:replenishment.create`, `dev:…/routes.php:17-19`), with the reason `actions.create-transfer` was dropped (it writes a *transfer* in another module, which would make the cell a two-module assertion). The preamble says explicitly that two rows had gone stale and that the JSON is now the single source | Task 3-9, the seven-correction table |
| **found: two table names were wrong** | `work_orders` and `appointments` are the plan's names; the models declare **`workshop_work_orders`** (`dev:…/Workshop/WorkOrder/Domain/WorkOrder.php:104`) and **`scheduling_appointments`** (`dev:…/Scheduling/Domain/Appointment.php:92`). Every `expect` on either would have queried a table that does not exist | **CORRECTED** in the JSON, the index table and the factory docblocks | the three work-order and appointment entries; `WorkshopFixture`, `SchedulingFixture` |
| **found: the scheduling fixture did not return what its payload interpolates** | `bayAndCustomer` returned `starts_at`; the request rule and the column are **`scheduled_start`**, `scheduled_end` is required and must be `after:scheduled_start`, and `planned_services` is `['required','array','min:1']` with a uuid `service_ref_id` per element — so the factory could not satisfy the only payload that route accepts | **CORRECTED**: the factory creates a real `Service` (there **is** a `ServiceFactory`; the census row is updated) and returns `service_id`, `scheduled_start` and `scheduled_end` as fixed offsets | `SchedulingFixture::bayAndCustomer`; the factory census note |
| **found: `AssignableRole` would 422 the ALLOW cell** | Three entries send a role name, and `AssignableRole::validate()` fails *"a role that grants permissions beyond your own"* (`dev:…/Rules/AssignableRole.php:50`) — so a hard-coded role name makes the allow cell red for every template that does not outrank it, which says nothing about authorization | **RESOLVED with `{template}`**, a token the **matrix** injects (not a fixture): the principal's own single template role always passes the guard. The deny cell is unaffected — `authorize()` 403s before `rules()` runs. The contract test's token scan adds `template` to the produced set | `RolePermissionMatrixTest::test_cell`; the schema's `{template}` paragraph; the contract test |
| **found: the token scan was top-level only** | `test_every_interpolation_token_is_produced_by_setup` walked `array_values($e['payload'])`, so the one **nested** token in the registry — `planned_services[0].service_ref_id` — was invisible to the guard that exists to catch exactly that | **CORRECTED**: the scan `json_encode`s `params`, `payload` **and** `expect` and regexes the blob, so nesting depth is irrelevant. `expect.where` columns also gained a `Schema::hasColumn` assertion | `RouteActionFixtureContractTest` |
| **found: one entry needs an environment precondition** | `POST platform/submit-for-enrichment`'s allow cell makes an **outbound** platform call | **DECLARED, not implied**: an optional `requires: ["http_fake"]` field, consumed by a `match` in the matrix before the request. It is the only entry that uses it. `submissionEnabled()` defaults to **true** (`services.platform.push_enabled`), so the 422 disabled branch does not fire and needs no override — stated in the entry's `notes` | the platform entry; `RolePermissionMatrixTest::test_cell` |
| **found: registry ordinals rot** | The factory docblocks said *"Entry 12"*, *"Entries 10 and 11"*; the file is sorted by `key`, so one added route re-numbers them all | **Every factory, correction cell and footnote now names the ROUTE KEY.** The registry's primary key cannot drift, and the change is stated as a rule in the fifteen-factories note | all fifteen factory docblocks; the corrections table; the wave-2b footnote |

**Verification of this revision.** The embedded JSON was parsed with `json.load` and asserted **byte-identical** to the validated file (20 entries, sorted, unique keys, all shape invariants above). All **7** `php`-fenced blocks extracted and linted under **PHP 8.4.15**: **7 clean, 0 failures**.

| ID | Finding | Resolution | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B1** | The enforcement ratchet cannot satisfy its own workflow: `ENFORCEMENT_STYLE_CEILING` is committed at the final value `0` **and** decremented per cluster. A constant already at zero cannot be decremented 176 times, and every intermediate commit stays red | **ACCEPTED, and replaced with a three-rule protocol plus a per-commit table.** (1) The ceiling is committed at the **MEASURED** starting value — **176**, which is what `tools/generate-enforcement-baseline.php` printed when it was **re-extracted from this plan and re-run read-only against the shared checkout on 2026-09-11**: `rows: 1054`, `130 CONTROLLER + 46 FORMREQUEST`, `baseline: 176 entries`, **0 reflection errors**. Phase 0 re-takes it and the ceiling follows Phase 0. (2) Each cluster commit lowers it by **exactly that cluster's row count**, so the assertion is green at every commit boundary. (3) The **first red is observed BEFORE the first commit** — the test is written with the post-`0.6.1` value (`175`) and run against the unconverted tree, where `176 > 175` fails; `0.6.1`'s conversion makes it green in the same commit. **Nothing is ever committed red.** The **37-row value table** is derived from the generator's own per-module grouping (Replenishment 1, Voucher 2, Tenant 2, PlatformIntegration 2, Promotion 4, Coupon 3, BatchExpiry 4, Menu 6, Identity 14, Scheduling 17, Workshop 14+15) and the per-class POS grouping (25 classes, 92 rows), sums to 176 and ends at 0 | Task 3-1 Step 1 |
