the two that did not are entries 8/9, wave **2b**'s routes, which is what the **†** footnote already says and what Phase 0 treats as a STOP. **Then the bodies**: `WorkOrderStatus::InProgress` (the enum has no `Open`, and `InProgress` is the **only** state whose allowed targets contain `Completed` — `dev:…/StatusMachine.php:64-67`), `internal_notes` (`dev:…/WorkOrder.php:53,127`), `product_batches` / `is_recalled` (`dev:…/Batch.php:21,31-45`; there is no `batches` table and no `status` column), and the three phantom services replaced by the modules' **own primary write path** — `Menu::create()` and `Promotion::create()` exactly as their controllers do it, with entry 19 **moved** to `POST api/v1/replenishment-requests` because the old route writes transfers rather than `replenishment_requests`. **Zero `app(...)` remain.** The contract test builds its own `$company`/`$actor` in `setUp()`, in the shape `dev:…/RBACTest.php:35-60` uses — the base `Tests\TestCase` is `set_time_limit`, the lazy-load reset and the quarantine skip and nothing else (`dev:apps/api/tests/TestCase.php:9-49`). **`RolePermissionMatrixTest` is supplied in full**: a `cells()` provider over template × entry, a pre-state assertion before the request, an admin-authored fixture so a setup failure cannot read as the denial under test, and an existence-vs-value `expect` evaluation. **All four new/changed PHP blocks `php -l` clean** | Task 3-9 Step 1 items 2 and 3; Step 2 |

## Change log rev 3 → rev 4

Fix round on **Codex plan gate r3** (`docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-09-11-rbac-plan-234-codex-gate-r3.md`), wave-3 section: **3 BLOCKER / 3 MAJOR / 1 minor**, plus the cross-plan re-pin. **Every one is applied, and this round was EXECUTE-THEN-WRITE**: the generator was extracted, fixed and run before anything was written about it.

### The executed evidence, first, because the rest of this round depends on it

The rev-3 generator was extracted to a scratchpad, run from `/Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/apps/api` (the shared checkout, read-only), and **it reproduced the gate's findings exactly**: it crashed on `Closure::__invoke`, and after a diagnostic bypass produced 136/2, baseline 138. It was then **replaced** with the audit's own analyzer — booted kernel, regex over each method's source, the audit's eight predicates and its bucket precedence — made re-runnable and made read-only-safe through `ENFORCEMENT_BASELINE_OUT` / `ENFORCEMENT_ROWS_OUT`.

**The exact command, and its actual output:**
```
cd /Users/houssamr/Projects/syneriva/apps/erp/apps/api
ENFORCEMENT_BASELINE_OUT=$SCRATCH/enforcement-style-baseline.json \
ENFORCEMENT_ROWS_OUT=$SCRATCH/rows.json \
  php $SCRATCH/w3-gen/generate-enforcement-baseline.php

rows: 1054
  142  AUTH_ONLY
  130  CONTROLLER
   46  FORMREQUEST
  642  MIDDLEWARE
    8  POLICY
   18  PUBLIC
   68  SUPERADMIN_ONLY
baseline: 176 entries written to <SCRATCH>/enforcement-style-baseline.json
reflection errors: 0
```
**That is the audit's CSV bucket for bucket** (`02-route-enforcement-sweep.md` §Classification totals), `130 + 46 = 176`, with the same zero reflection errors the audit recorded. **Grouping `rows.json` then produced every count in this plan**, including two corrections the measurement forced (see MINOR 1 and the Identity note).

### BLOCKERs

| ID | Finding | What rev 4 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B1** | The supplied generator crashes on the live router — it maps a closure action to `Closure::__invoke` and reflects it | **Replaced the generator.** The new one resolves a closure action through `$route->getAction()['uses'] instanceof Closure` and `ReflectionFunction`, which is what the audit's `analyze_routes.php` did. **It ran clean: 0 reflection errors over all 1054 API routes.** The rev-3 change log's "executable full generator" claim is withdrawn in the intro note rather than quietly replaced | Phase 0's generator block + its intro |
| **B2** | Even after bypassing the crash it produces 138, not 176 — `$checksIn` never visits `NullsafeMethodCall`, and the `POLICY` bucket disappears | **Diagnosed both causes in the plan and removed the class of defect.** The AST rewrite is abandoned for the audit's **regex-over-method-source** oracle, because the audit's predicate set is textual and wider than any call-name match (`abort_unless(… can`, `abort_if(… ! … can`, `hasRole(`, `hasAnyRole(`) and its `POLICY` rule is *"the ability is a bare, non-dotted policy word"*, not *"the method calls `$this->authorize()`"*. `$this->user()?->can('promotions.manage')` (`dev:…/StorePromotionRequest.php:15-18`) is matched by `/->can\(/` with no node-type question to get wrong. **The invocation also changes** from `tinker --execute="require …"` to a plain `php tools/…`, because the generator boots the framework itself — which is what made the read-only run possible | the generator + the command block |
| **B3** | The matrix is a prose stub — `RouteActionFixtures` is an ellipsis, one of twenty factories is shown, `generate-unfixtured-routes.php` is "a short sibling" with no body | **All three supplied in full.** (a) `RouteActionFixtures.php` with **fifteen** factories across ten `<Module>Fixture` classes — and **it is fifteen, not twenty**: the twenty entries share five `setup` values, a count nobody had taken. Every model namespace is verified against `git ls-files 'apps/api/database/factories/*'`; the **four modules with no factory** (Menu, Coupon, Promotion, Replenishment) are named and build through their public service, with the `app(...)` use stated rather than smuggled. (b) `RouteActionFixtureContractTest` with all six assertions implemented, including B3's `permission`-vs-`permission_source` check across all three sources. (c) `generate-unfixtured-routes.php` in full, with a `--stdout` mode the contract test uses to assert the committed file is not stale. **All four PHP blocks in this plan pass `php -l`** *(rev 5 note, gate r4 Wave 3 **MINOR 2**: `php -l` proves SYNTAX and nothing else — it does not resolve a class, an enum case, a column or a factory, and rev 4's blocks were `php -l` clean while naming four classes that do not exist. The resolution evidence is now separate and is the six-row correction table in Task 3-9 Step 1, each row read off `dev`.)* | Task 3-9 Step 1 items 2, 3, 4 |

### MAJORs

| ID | Finding | What rev 4 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | The first-red statement is not truthful — on the freshly generated full baseline the described test is green | **Named the assertion that is actually red.** The subset and stale directions are green by construction (live set *equals* baseline on generation) and are the anti-drift guards. The red one is **`ENFORCEMENT_STYLE_CEILING`, committed at the wave's EXIT value of 0**, so `176 > 0` fails on the first commit and passes only on the last. Also stated why the stale direction is what catches a forgotten ceiling decrement | Task 3-1 Step 1 |
| **2** | The claimed exact add lists remain templates — `…` prefixes, "+ tail", `<Module>` in the run commands, 21 of 24 POS lists formed by later substitution | **Every path written out in full, in every row.** The twelve-row cluster table drops `…` and *"+ tail"* and prints the three tail files in each row. **All twenty-five POS add lists are printed** (24 controllers + `ManagerPinController`). Step 10's run block enumerates **twelve** invocations with no `<Module>`. **And the enumeration found a factual error**: POS has **five** route files, not one — `KitchenDisplayController` → `routes_kitchen.php`, `OrderController` → `routes_orders.php`, `TableController` → `routes_tables.php`, the other 22 → `routes.php`, and `routes_held_orders.php` is touched by nothing. Rev 3's *"routes.php appears in all twenty-four add lists"* was false | Task 3-1's table + Steps 2/10; Q-w3-3's route-file map; Task 3-2 Step 7 |
| **3** | `$SCRATCH` is not an exact command — it contains a literal `<session-id>` | **Made it runnable**: `SCRATCH="${CLAUDE_SCRATCHPAD:-$(mktemp -d -t rbac-w3)}"` with a writability check and a `case` that **refuses** `/tmp` and any path inside `apps/web` mechanically rather than by instruction | Phase 0 |

### minor

| ID | Finding | What rev 4 does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | The POS FormRequest belongs to `ManagerPinController`, not `PosAuthController` | **Confirmed by measurement and corrected everywhere.** The row is `POST /api/v1/pos/verify-manager-pin` → `ManagerPinController::verify` → `VerifyManagerPinRequest`, routed at `dev:…/POS/routes.php:260`. It gets **its own ordinal `Phase 0.6.37`** and its own test, and **everything after it shifts by one** (`0.6.38`…`0.6.48`). **And it is a finding, not just a re-attribution**: `VerifyManagerPinRequest::authorize()` is `return $this->user() !== null;` (`dev:…:24-27`) — an *authentication* check that classifies `FORMREQUEST` only because the audit's open-detector matches a literal `return true;`. So this route is effectively `AUTH_ONLY` and its conversion **changes who can call it**, which is recorded, reviewed and committed last | Q-w3-3 table; Task 3-2 Step 1/3/7; the ordinal tables; the verification checklist |

Six inline citations before the change-log sections were re-pointed; the change-log sections keep theirs as provenance.

### Q-w disposition (gate's own reading, applied)

`Q-w3-1`, `Q-w3-2` and `Q-w3-4` were named as *"must be corrected or re-pinned"*: `Q-w3-2` and `Q-w3-4` are rewritten as CLOSED above, and `Q-w3-1`'s ordinal premise is corrected in the Global Constraints. `Q-w3-3`, `Q-w3-5` and `Q-w3-6` stand as plan-author decisions, with **Q-w3-5** taking m3-2's corrected count. **No wave-3 finding escalates to a fifth owner question**; OQ-3 remains the owner's and remains **not** this wave's.

### Rejected

**None.** Every wave-3 finding reproduces. The three the gate itself listed as **rejected false positives** are retained as rev 1 had them and are worth recording, because a later reader may re-raise them:

- **POS is correctly scheduled last**, and its classification really is 91 controller + 1 FormRequest — only the global denominator was wrong.
- **The FormRequest conversion rule is sound**: authorization moves to route middleware and `authorize()` becomes `true` or an exact duplicate, with allow/deny mutation assertions.
- **`MembershipRole` really has only one production authorization read** — the finding was its *label*, not its count.

Verifications worth recording:

- **B3-1** — `csv.reader` over `02-route-enforcement-sweep.csv`: 1 054 data rows; `642 MIDDLEWARE / 142 AUTH_ONLY / 130 CONTROLLER / 68 SUPERADMIN_ONLY / 46 FORMREQUEST / 18 PUBLIC / 8 POLICY`. Grouping the `controller` column on `Modules\<Name>` gives POS 91+1, Workshop 14+15, Scheduling 9+8, Identity 7+7, Menu 0+6, BatchExpiry 0+4, Promotion 2+2, Coupon 0+3, Tenant 2+0, PlatformIntegration 2+0, Voucher 2+0, Replenishment 1+0 — totalling 130 / 46.
- **M3-1** — `grep -n "fetchDiscountPermissions\|resolveOnlineDiscountPermissions" apps/pos/src/stores/operatorStore.ts` returns the import at `:4`, the call at `:136` and four call sites. It is production code.
- **M3-3** — `sed -n '925,940p' apps/api/app/Modules/Identity/Presentation/Controllers/UserController.php` shows `$isOwner` guarding the `LOCATION_ESCALATION` `forbidden()` return.
- **M3-4** — `sed -n '87,105p' apps/api/app/Modules/Marketplace/Presentation/routes.php` shows the tenant group and the four `can:marketplace.admin` routes; `sed -n '56,58p' apps/api/routes/api.php` shows the central pipeline.
